# Kit API reference

Generated by `node tools/dev.js docs` from the JSDoc in `kit/*.js`. Do not edit: change the JSDoc and regenerate.
Each module lists what it assigns to `Kit`, then what its factories return, then its row and option types.

## core.js

> Kit core: the foundation every other kit file and every game builds on.
>
> Owns:  the `Kit` global and game registry, `Kit.math` (scalar helpers, easing, unseeded random),
>        number / time formatting, the event bus, the object pool, the game clock (timers and
>        tweens that run on game time) and the per-game persistent store.
> Does not own: anything that touches the DOM, THREE or the frame loop (see shell.js), seeded
>        randomness (Kit.rng in loot.js) or progression models (model.js).
>
> Pure enough to run in node: the only browser global it reads is `localStorage`, and it copes
> when that is missing or blocked. Unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### `Kit.math.TAU`

One full turn in radians.

### `Kit.math.clamp(value, min = 0, max = 1)`

Limits a value to a range.

- `value` *number*
- `[min=0]` *number*
- `[max=1]` *number*

**Returns** *number* `value`, or the nearer bound when it lies outside [min, max].

### `Kit.math.wrap(value, size)`

Wraps a value into [0, size), also for negatives (the `%` operator alone keeps the sign).

- `value` *number*
- `size` *number* - length of the cycle (must not be 0)

**Returns** *number*

```js
wrap(-1, 4) // 3
```

### `Kit.math.lerp(from, to, amount)`

Linear interpolation.

- `from` *number* - value at amount 0
- `to` *number* - value at amount 1
- `amount` *number* - 0..1 (not clamped, so it also extrapolates)

**Returns** *number*

### `Kit.math.damp(current, target, rate, dt)`

Frame-rate independent smoothing: moves `current` toward `target` by the same fraction per
second whatever the frame time, because the remaining distance decays as e^(-rate * dt).

- `current` *number*
- `target` *number*
- `rate` *number* - decay rate per second (about 5 is lazy, 20 is snappy)
- `dt` *number* - frame time in seconds

**Returns** *number* the new current value

```js
camera.position.x = damp(camera.position.x, player.x, 6, dt);
```

### `Kit.math.rand(minOrMax = 1, max)`

Uniform random number. `rand()` is 0..1, `rand(max)` is 0..max, `rand(min, max)` is min..max.

- `[minOrMax=1]` *number* - the upper bound when called with one argument, else the lower bound
- `[max]` *number* - exclusive upper bound

**Returns** *number*

### `Kit.math.randInt(min, max)`

Uniform random integer with both ends included.

- `min` *number*
- `max` *number*

**Returns** *number* integer in [min, max]

### `Kit.math.pick(items)`

Uniform random element of a list.

- `items` *T[]*

**Returns** *T* undefined when the list is empty

### `Kit.math.pickWeighted(rows, weightOf = (row) => row.weight || 1)`

Weighted random row, for spawn and loot tables. A row's chance is its weight over the total.

- `rows` *T[]*
- `[weightOf]` *function(T): number* - defaults to `row.weight`, or 1 when that is missing or 0

**Returns** *T* undefined when `rows` is empty

```js
pickWeighted(ENEMIES, (enemy) => enemy.weight)
```

### `Kit.math.shuffle(items)`

Shuffled copy of a list. The input is not changed.

- `items` *T[]*

**Returns** *T[]*

### `Kit.math.randomTools(random)`

Builds the list-randomising helpers over any source of random numbers, so the unseeded
Kit.math versions and the seeded Kit.rng versions (loot.js) share one implementation.

- `random` *function(): number* - returns a number in [0, 1)

**Returns** *RandomTools*

**Complexity** `pick` is O(1); `pickWeighted` is O(rows) time (two passes: total, then walk), O(1) space; `shuffle` is O(n) time and space

```js
const { shuffle } = Kit.math.randomTools(Kit.rng(42).next);
```

### `Kit.math.remap(value, inMin, inMax, outMin, outMax)`

Maps a value from one range onto another, clamped to the output range.

- `value` *number*
- `inMin` *number*
- `inMax` *number*
- `outMin` *number*
- `outMax` *number*

**Returns** *number*

```js
remap(speed, 0, 20, 0.8, 1.4) // engine pitch from speed
```

### `Kit.math.ease`

Easing functions: each maps progress 0..1 to eased progress, with f(0) = 0 and f(1) = 1.
The `out*` shapes start fast and settle; outBack, outBounce and outElastic overshoot or bounce.

### `Kit.math.sampleWeighted(rows, count, weightOf = (row) => (row.weight === undefined ? 1 : row.weight))`

Draws up to `count` different rows, each draw weighted: the "pick 3 of these, rarer ones less often" of offers and loot.
A row is never drawn twice, and rows whose weight is 0 or less are never drawn at all.

- `rows` *T[]* - the input is not changed
- `count` *number* - how many to draw; fewer come back when fewer rows have a positive weight
- `[weightOf]` *function(T): number* - defaults to `row.weight`, or 1 when that is missing

**Returns** *T[]* in draw order (heavier rows tend to come first)

```js
Kit.math.sampleWeighted(UPGRADES, 3, (row) => (row.rare ? 0.2 : 1))
```

### `Kit.math.rateLimiter(now)`

Makes a per-key rate limiter: `isReady(key, gap)` answers "has at least `gap` passed since this key last got a yes?"
and, when it says yes, starts that key's gap again. Sounds, toasts and hit sparks use it to stay readable in a crowd.

- `now` *function(): number* - the clock, in any unit (seconds of game time, performance.now() milliseconds)

**Returns** *function(string, number): boolean* `gap` is in the clock's unit; a gap of 0 or less always passes

```js
const isReady = Kit.math.rateLimiter(() => world.seconds);
if (isReady('hit', 0.05)) sfx.play('hit');
```

### `Kit.fmtTime(seconds)`

Formats a duration as minutes and two-digit seconds.

- `seconds` *number*

**Returns** *string*

```js
Kit.fmtTime(75) // "1:15"
```

### `Kit.fmt(value)`

Formats a number for a HUD: exact (one decimal at most) below 1000, then three significant
digits with a unit suffix.

- `value` *number*

**Returns** *string*

```js
Kit.fmt(1234) // "1.23K"
```

```js
Kit.fmt(12.5) // "12.5"
```

### `Kit.bus()`

Creates a tiny synchronous event bus.

**Returns** *Bus*

**Complexity** `on` and unsubscribe are O(1); `emit` is O(listeners of that event) time and space (it copies the listener set so listeners may subscribe or unsubscribe during delivery)

```js
const bus = Kit.bus();
const off = bus.on('hit', (damage) => console.log(damage));
bus.emit('hit', 3); off();
```

### `Kit.pool(create, onGet, onRelease)`

Creates an object pool, so things spawned repeatedly (bullets, particles, pickups) are reused
instead of allocated and garbage-collected.

- `create` *function(): \** - builds a new object when none is free
- `[onGet]` *function(\*, ...\*): void* - prepares an object for use; without it THREE objects are made visible
- `[onRelease]` *function(\*): void* - retires an object; without it THREE objects are hidden

**Returns** *Pool*

**Complexity** `get` and `release` are O(1); `each` and `clear` are O(live) time and space (they copy the live set so objects may be released during the pass). Memory is the high-water mark of live objects.

```js
const bullets = Kit.pool(() => make.sphere(0.1, 0xffee88), (bullet, origin) => bullet.position.copy(origin));
const bullet = bullets.get(player.position); bullets.release(bullet);
```

### `Kit.slotRing(capacity)`

Hands out the slots of a fixed-size buffer in a circle and remembers the stretch that may still
be in use, so a per-frame pass visits only that stretch instead of the whole buffer. Made for
short-lived things stored in pre-allocated arrays or instanced meshes (particles, trails, decals).

- `capacity` *number* - number of slots (a positive whole number)

**Returns** *SlotRing*

**Complexity** `claim` is O(1); `forEach` is O(span); `trim` is O(slots released). The window runs from the oldest unfinished slot to the newest, so a pass costs what is in use, not the capacity. O(1) space.

```js
const ring = Kit.slotRing(900);
const slot = ring.claim();                         // on spawn: write the particle into particles[slot]
ring.forEach((slot) => advance(particles[slot]));  // every frame
ring.trim((slot) => particles[slot].life <= 0);    // then let the window shrink
```

### `Kit.clock()`

Creates a game clock: timers and tweens that advance only when `update` is called, so pause,
hitstop and slow motion freeze or stretch them along with the game.

**Returns** *Clock*

**Complexity** scheduling is O(1); `update` is O(jobs) time per frame and allocates one array of the surviving jobs

```js
clock.after(2, spawnBoss);
const stop = clock.every(0.5, spawnGrunt);
await clock.tween(mesh.scale, { x: 1.4, y: 0.7 }, 0.12, 'outBack'); // target can be any object with numeric fields
```

### `Kit.store(namespace)`

Opens a persistent key-value store for one namespace (the shell gives each game its own as `ctx.save`).

- `namespace` *string* - usually the game id

**Returns** *Store*

```js
const save = Kit.store('runner');
save.set('best', Math.max(score, save.get('best', 0)));
```

### `Kit.isDevelopment()`

Is this page running for a developer (as opposed to the published page a player opens)? Development-time
warnings and checks only run when it is, so they cost a player nothing.
Set `window.KIT_DEV = true` or `false` before the kit loads to force the answer either way.

**Returns** *boolean* the forced value, else whether the page is served from this machine

```js
if (Kit.isDevelopment()) installExpensiveChecks();
```

### `Kit.warnings`

The development warnings raised so far, oldest first. Tools read this (`node tools/dev.js play` prints it).

### `Kit.warn(message)`

Tells the developer about a likely mistake that would otherwise only show up as odd behaviour. Each distinct
message is reported once, as a console warning, and only in development (see Kit.isDevelopment).

- `message` *string* - names the cause and the fix

**Returns** *boolean* true when the warning was reported, false when it was a repeat or the page is published

```js
Kit.warn(`runner: 4 stats in the top row; three fit a phone. Move one into ui.progress.`);
```

### `Kit.expectType(where, value, expected, example)`

Development check of one argument: warns (Kit.warn) when its type is obviously wrong. A kit function calls
this at its entry so the mistake is named where it was made, not three frames later.

- `where` *string* - the call as the developer wrote it, e.g. 'ui.stat(label)'
- `value` *\** - the argument received
- `expected` *string* - accepted type names separated by `|`: string, number, boolean, function, object, array, undefined (for an optional argument), null
- `example` *string* - a correct call, shown in the warning

**Returns** *boolean* whether the value has an accepted type

```js
Kit.expectType('ui.stat(label)', label, 'string', "ui.stat('Coins', 0)");
```

### `Kit.checkGame(definition, { games = Kit.games, skins = Kit.theme ? Object.keys(Kit.theme.SKINS) : null } = {})`

Checks a game definition before it is registered. Pure: it reports problems and changes nothing.

- `definition` *GameDefinition*
- `[known]` *Object* - what the definition is checked against
- `[known.games=Kit.games]` *GameDefinition[]* - the games already registered
- `[known.skins]` *?string[]* - valid `theme.skin` names; defaults to the skins of kit/theme.js when that is loaded, and null (skip the skin check) when it is not

**Returns** *{errors: string[], warnings: string[]}* each message names the cause and the fix; any error means the game cannot run

**Complexity** O(games) time for the duplicate-id scan, O(1) space

```js
Kit.checkGame({ id: 'orbit', create: 5 }).errors // ['`create` must be a function ...']
```

### `Kit.headlessOf(game)`

The headless handle of a game: its pure rules and data, for tests and tools that never open a page.

- `game` *GameDefinition \| string* - a definition, or the id of a registered game

**Returns** *?Object* the handle (`headless`, or the older `model` / `lab`), or null when the game has none

**Complexity** O(games) time when given an id (a scan of the registry), O(1) when given a definition

```js
const { progression } = Kit.headlessOf('miner');
```

### `Kit.register(definition)`

Registers a game with the menu. Each game file calls this once at load. The definition is checked first
(Kit.checkGame): a definition that cannot run throws here, at the mistake, instead of failing later.

- `definition` *GameDefinition*

**Returns** *number* how many games are now registered

```js
Kit.register({ id: 'orbit', name: 'Orbit', theme: { skin: 'neon' }, create: (ctx) => ({ update(dt, t) {} }) });
```

### Types

- **RandomTools**
  - `pick` *function(Array): \** - uniform random element (undefined for an empty list)
  - `pickWeighted` *function(Array, function(\*): number): \** - random row with chance weight / total weight
  - `shuffle` *function(Array): Array* - shuffled copy; the input is not changed
- **Bus**
  - `on` *function(string, function(\*): void): function(): boolean* - subscribe; returns an unsubscribe function
  - `emit` *function(string, \*=): void* - call every listener of the event with one payload
  - `clear` *function(): void* - drop every listener
- **Pool**
  - `live` *Set\<\*\>* - the objects currently handed out
  - `get` *function(...\*): \** - take an object (reused if one is free); extra arguments go to `onGet`
  - `release` *function(\*): void* - hand an object back; a no-op for objects that are not live
  - `each` *function(function(\*): void): void* - visit every live object (safe to release inside)
  - `clear` *function(): void* - release every live object
- **SlotRing**
  - `claim` *function(): number* - the next slot index, recycling the oldest slot once every slot has been used
  - `forEach` *function(function(number): void): void* - visit every slot in the live window, oldest first
  - `trim` *function(function(number): boolean): void* - shrink the window past the oldest slots that `isFree(slot)` reports as finished
  - `span` *number* - how many slots the live window covers (read-only)
- **Clock**
  - `after` *function(number, function(): void): function(): boolean* - run once after N game seconds; returns a cancel function
  - `every` *function(number, function(): void): function(): boolean* - run every N game seconds; returns a cancel function
  - `tween` *function(Object, Object\<string, number\>, number=, (string\|function(number): number)=): Promise\<void\>* - animate numeric properties of an object to target values; resolves when it finishes
  - `update` *function(number): void* - advance by `dt` game seconds (the shell calls this)
  - `clear` *function(): void* - drop every timer and tween
- **Store**
  - `get` *function(string, \*=): \** - the saved value, or the given default when the key was never set
  - `set` *function(string, \*): \** - save a JSON-serialisable value; returns it
- **GameInstance**
  - `update` *function(number, number): void* - called every frame with (dt, t) in game seconds
  - `[dispose]` *function(): void* - called when the game is left or restarted
  - `[debugState]` *function(): Object* - plain data describing the run right now (score, phase, positions); surfaced as `Kit.debug.state().state` to the developer overlay and to `node tools/dev.js play` predicates
- **GameDefinition**
  - `id` *string* - unique id; also the save namespace and the smoke-test name
  - `name` *string* - title on the menu
  - `tagline` *string* - one line under the title
  - `hook` *string* - the progression hook, shown on the menu card
  - `color` *number* - accent colour as a hex number
  - `order` *number* - menu position, ascending
  - `[kind='game']` *'game'\|'maker'\|'workbench'* - what the screen is (Kit.SCREEN_KINDS); a workbench is never listed for players
  - `create` *function(Object): GameInstance* - builds the game from the shell's `ctx`
  - `[theme]` *Object* - the game's HUD theme (kit/theme.js); a game without one gets a development warning
  - `[headless]` *Object* - the game's pure rules and data, usable without THREE or the DOM (tests, tools). Add `headless.progression` (`createState`, `actions`, `apply`, `summary`; see tools/sim.js) and `make sim GAME=<id>` plays the game's long arc for simulated hours. Read it with Kit.headlessOf, which also accepts the older names `model` and `lab`

## model.js

> Kit model: progression as small, pure, composable pieces.
>
>   curve    - numbers from data:               Kit.curve.of({ base: 10, growth: 1.5 })(level)
>   table    - rows with unique ids:            Kit.table(ROWS).byId('gold')
>   stats    - base values + keyed modifiers:   stats.get('damage')
>   wallet   - currencies:                      wallet.spend(25)
>   upgrades - table x curve x stats x wallet:  upgrades.buy('pick')
>   goals    - progress from game events:       goals.track('catch', 1, fish)
>   persist  - the load / save / notify plumbing the stateful models share
>
> Owns:  the data models above. Each is useful alone; `upgrades` is only the composition of the others.
> Does not own: how they are drawn (ui.shop / ui.offer / ui.checklist in ui.js), random items
>        (loot.js) or where saves are stored (any `{ get(key, fallback), set(key, value) }`, usually ctx.save).
>
> No DOM, no THREE: runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### `Kit.persist`

### `Kit.persist.load(save, key, fallback)`

Reads a model's saved state.

- `[save]` *Save* - omitted for a model that lives only in memory
- `key` *string*
- `fallback` *\** - returned when there is no save or nothing was saved under `key`

**Returns** *\**

### `Kit.persist.tracker(save, key, readState)`

Creates the "state changed" plumbing of a model: one change event and an optional save.

- `[save]` *Save*
- `[key]` *string* - where `readState()` is saved
- `[readState]` *function(): \** - returns the JSON-serialisable state to save

**Returns** *ChangeTracker*

```js
const changes = Kit.persist.tracker(save, 'wallet', () => balances);
balances.coins += 5; changes.commit('coins');
```

#### `Kit.persist.tracker().on(listener)`

#### `Kit.persist.tracker().persist()`

#### `Kit.persist.tracker().commit(payload)`

Save before notifying, so a listener that reads the save sees the new state.

### `Kit.table(rows)`

Wraps rows in a table: a copy of the array (so map / filter / indexOf still work) plus id lookup.

- `rows` *Row[]*

**Returns** *Row[] & {has: function(\*): boolean, byId: function(\*): Row}*

**Complexity** O(rows) time and space to build; `has` and `byId` are O(1) (a Map by id)

```js
Kit.table([{ id: 'gold', value: 5 }]).byId('gold').value // 5
```

### `Kit.stats(base, { limits = {} } = {})`

Creates a stat block: base values plus modifiers keyed by where they come from, so applying a
source again replaces its earlier contribution instead of stacking on it.

- `base` *Object\<string, number\>* - stat name to base value
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.limits]` *Object\<string, [number, number]\>* - stat name to [min, max]; `get` never returns a value outside it however the modifiers stack (a crit chance stays in 0..1, a cooldown never reaches 0). Use -Infinity / Infinity for an open end. Stats without a limit are unbounded, as before.

**Returns** *{base: Object\<string, number\>, get: function(string): number, mod: function(string, ?ModSpec): void, preview: function(string, string, ?ModSpec): number, clear: function(): void, on: function(function(string=): void): function(): boolean}* `preview(statName, source, spec)` is what `get(statName)` would return if `source` contributed `spec` (null = nothing) instead of its current modifiers; nothing is changed and nobody is notified.

```js
const stats = Kit.stats({ speed: 4 });
stats.mod('upg:boots', { speed: { mul: 1.5 } });
stats.get('speed'); // 6
stats.mod('upg:boots', null); // removes that source
```

```js
const stats = Kit.stats({ crit: 0.9 }, { limits: { crit: [0, 1] } });
stats.preview('crit', 'upg:luck', { crit: { add: 0.3 } }); // 1 (clamped), and stats.get('crit') is still 0.9
```

#### `Kit.stats().get(statName)`

The stat's current value.

**Complexity** O(1) time after the first read of a stat since the last mod() / clear(); that first read is O(sources). O(stats read) space for the cache. Games read stats every frame and change them rarely.

#### `Kit.stats().preview(statName, source, spec)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.stats().mod(source, spec)`

Sets (or with null removes) the modifiers one source contributes. The spec is read when stats are next
computed and then cached: to change a source's effect, call mod() again rather than editing the spec object.

#### `Kit.stats().clear()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.stats.scale(spec, level)`

Scales a per-level modifier spec to a level: adds stack linearly, muls compound.

- `spec` *ModSpec* - the effect of one level
- `level` *number*

**Returns** *ModSpec*

```js
Kit.stats.scale({ damage: { add: 2, mul: 1.1 } }, 3) // { damage: { add: 6, mul: 1.331 } }
```

### `Kit.wallet(initial = { coins: 0 }, save, key = 'wallet')`

Creates a wallet. Saved balances win over the initial ones, so `initial` is the starting
money of a fresh save.

- `[initial={ coins: 0 }]` *Object\<string, number\>* - currency name to starting balance
- `[save]` *Save* - omit for a wallet that resets every run
- `[key='wallet']` *string* - save key

**Returns** *{get: function(string=): number, can: function(number, string=): boolean, add: function(number, string=): number, spend: function(number, string=): boolean, on: function(function(string): void): function(): boolean}* `spend` returns false and changes nothing when the balance is too low; every currency argument defaults to 'coins'. Also `canAll(costs)` / `spendAll(costs)` for a price in several currencies at once (a recipe, an alloy): `costs` maps currency name to amount; `spendAll` pays every part or, when any part is short, nothing (and returns false).

```js
const wallet = Kit.wallet({ coins: 10, gems: 0 }, ctx.save);
if (wallet.spend(4)) wallet.add(1, 'gems');
```

```js
if (wallet.spendAll({ copper: 6, silver: 2 })) forgeBronze(); // all or nothing
```

### `Kit.upgrades(rows, { stats, wallet, save, key = 'upgrades', slots = {} } = {})`

Creates upgrade state over a table of rows.

- `rows` *UpgradeRow[]*
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.stats]` *ReturnType\<typeof Kit.stats\>* - receives each row's `mods`
- `[options.wallet]` *ReturnType\<typeof Kit.wallet\>* - pays for `buy`; without it everything is affordable
- `[options.save]` *Save* - persists levels
- `[options.key='upgrades']` *string* - save key
- `[options.slots]` *Object\<string, number\>* - slot name to how many different rows with that `slot` may be owned at once, e.g. `{ weapon: 3 }`; a full slot stops new rows of it from being bought or offered (owned ones still level)

**Returns** *Object* `{ table, level(id), maxed(id), cost(id), unlocked(id), canBuy(id), buy(id), grant(id), offer(n, filter), desc(id, level), preview(id, statName, level), owned(), available(), affordable(), reset(), restore(), on(listener) }`

**Complexity** `level`, `cost`, `maxed`, `canBuy`, `buy` and `grant` are O(1) per call (plus O(requires) for the unlock check); `offer`, `owned`, `available`, `affordable`, `reset` and `restore` are O(rows). O(rows) space.

```js
const upgrades = Kit.upgrades(UPGRADES, { stats, wallet, save: ctx.save });
if (upgrades.buy('pick')) sfx.play('coin');     // pays, levels up, updates stats
const choices = upgrades.offer(3);              // three random rows for a level-up screen
upgrades.on(({ id, level, row }) => redrawShop());
```

#### `Kit.upgrades().level(id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.upgrades().maxed(id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.upgrades().cost(id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.upgrades().unlocked(id)`

`requires` may be one id or a list; concat normalises both (and a missing value) to a list.

#### `Kit.upgrades().canBuy(id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.upgrades().buy(id)`

Pays and levels up. Returns false (and changes nothing) if locked, maxed or unaffordable.

#### `Kit.upgrades().slotUse(slot)`

How many rows of a slot are owned (level 1 or above). O(rows).

#### `Kit.upgrades().grant(id)`

Levels up without paying (level-up choices, milestone rewards). Ignores `requires`; false when maxed.

#### `Kit.upgrades().offer(count = 3, filter = () => true)`

Up to `count` random rows that can still be levelled, for "pick 1 of 3". Rows may carry `weight` (a number, or
`(level, row) => number` of the row's current level; default 1) to show up more or less often; 0 hides a row.
With no weights anywhere every row is equally likely, exactly as before.

#### `Kit.upgrades().desc(id, atLevel = level(id))`

A row's description text at a level (default: its current level).

#### `Kit.upgrades().preview(id, statName, atLevel = level(id) + 1)`

The value `statName` would have with this row at `atLevel` (default: one level up) and everything else as it
is now: the "next level gives" line of a shop card. Needs `stats`; changes nothing.

```js
`${fmt(stats.get('damage'))} -> ${fmt(upgrades.preview('pick', 'damage'))}`
```

#### `Kit.upgrades().owned()`

Rows at level 1 or above.

#### `Kit.upgrades().available()`

Rows that can still be levelled, in table order (shops, "what can I afford" text).

#### `Kit.upgrades().affordable()`

Rows the wallet can pay for right now.

#### `Kit.upgrades().reset()`

Sets every level back to 0 without firing onLevel.

#### `Kit.upgrades().restore()`

Re-fires onLevel for saved levels; call once after creating the things onLevel touches.

### `Kit.goals(rows, { save, key = 'goals', onDone } = {})`

Creates goal state over a table of rows.

- `rows` *GoalRow[]*
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.save]` *Save* - persists progress
- `[options.key='goals']` *string* - save key
- `[options.onDone]` *function(GoalRow): void* - fires once, when a goal reaches its target

**Returns** *Object* `{ table, done(id), progress(id), list(), allDone(), track(event, amount, payload), on(listener) }`; `list()` gives `{ row, progress, target, done }` per goal for drawing.

**Complexity** `track`, `list` and `allDone` are O(rows) time; `done` and `progress` are O(1). O(rows) space.

```js
const goals = Kit.goals([{ id: 'three', text: 'Catch 3', event: 'catch', target: 3 }], { onDone: (goal) => ui.toast(goal.text) });
goals.track('catch', 1, fish);
```

#### `Kit.goals().done(id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.goals().progress(id)`

Reported progress stops at the target, so an overshoot never draws as "4 / 3".

#### `Kit.goals().list()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.goals().allDone()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.goals().track(event, amount = 1, payload)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### Types

- **Save** - Any persistent key-value store; `ctx.save` and `Kit.store(namespace)` both fit.
  - `get` *function(string, \*=): \** - saved value, or the fallback when the key was never set
  - `set` *function(string, \*): \** - stores a JSON-serialisable value
- **ChangeTracker**
  - `on` *function(function(\*): void): function(): boolean* - subscribe to changes; returns an unsubscribe function
  - `persist` *function(): void* - write the current state to the save (no-op without a save)
  - `notify` *function(\*=): void* - tell subscribers something changed
  - `commit` *function(\*=): void* - persist, then notify
- **CurveSpec** - Plain-data description of a curve, so tables stay JSON-like: `5` constant, `[[0, 1], [10, 3]]` piecewise-linear ramp, `{ base, growth }` geometric, `{ base, step }` linear, `{ steps: [[0, 1], [3, 1.5]] }` staircase, or a function used as is.
- **Modifier**
  - `[add]` *number* - flat amount added to the base (default 0)
  - `[mul]` *number* - factor applied after the adds (default 1)
- **ModSpec** - Modifiers by stat name, e.g.
- **UpgradeRow** - Any extra fields (color, tag, icon) are yours and are passed through untouched.
  - `id` *string*
  - `[name]` *string*
  - `[desc]` *string \| function(number): string* - text, or text for a given level
  - `[max]` *number* - highest level (default: no limit)
  - `[cost]` *CurveSpec* - price of the next level as a curve of the current level; omitted = free (use grant / offer for level-up picks)
  - `[currency]` *string* - wallet currency (default 'coins')
  - `[mods]` *ModSpec \| function(number): ModSpec* - effect of ONE level (scaled by Kit.stats.scale), or a function giving the whole effect at a level; pushed into `stats` automatically
  - `[requires]` *string \| string[]* - id(s) that must be at level 1+ first: enough to describe a skill tree
  - `[onLevel]` *function(number, UpgradeRow): void* - for effects stats cannot express (spawn an orbiting blade)
  - `[requiresMax]` *string \| string[]* - id(s) that must be at their max level first: an evolution of two maxed rows
  - `[excludes]` *string \| string[]* - id(s) this row cannot be owned together with (checked both ways): an either-or choice
  - `[slot]` *string* - the limited slot the row occupies once owned (capacities come from `options.slots`)
  - `[weight]` *number \| function(number, UpgradeRow): number* - how often `offer` shows the row, relative to the others (default 1; 0 never); a function gets the row's current level, e.g. `(level) => (level ? 1 : 3)` favours new rows
- **GoalRow**
  - `id` *string*
  - `text` *string* - what the player reads
  - `event` *string* - the tracked event that advances this goal
  - `[target=1]` *number* - amount needed
  - `[when]` *function(\*): boolean* - only payloads that pass count
  - `[reward]` *\** - yours to read in onDone

## meta.js

> Kit meta: progression that outlives a single run, and synergies between the things a player collects.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.profile` (lifetime statistics, permanent unlocks, achievements, account level, run history) and
>        `Kit.synergy` (set bonuses that switch on when a build holds enough items with the right tags).
> Does not own: per-run upgrades (`Kit.upgrades`), short goals (`Kit.goals`) or currencies (`Kit.wallet`) - it
>        composes with them. Pure: no DOM, no THREE; unit-tested in tools/test-kit.js.
>
> Depth comes from three timescales. Upgrades and goals cover the session; this module covers the long arc
> (what is different on run ten) and build identity (why two players' runs differ).

### `Kit.profile({ save, achievements = [], levelCurve = { base: 100, growth: 1.5 }, onAchievement, onUnlock, onLevel, key = 'profile' } = {})`

A player's permanent record for one game.

- `options` *Object*
- `[options.save]` *{ get: Function, set: Function }* - Persistence (ctx.save). Omit for an in-memory profile.
- `[options.achievements]` *AchievementRow[]*
- `[options.levelCurve]` *\** - Kit.curve spec: levels already gained (0 for a new player) -> experience needed for the next.
- `[options.onAchievement]` *(row: AchievementRow) =\> void*
- `[options.onUnlock]` *(id: string) =\> void*
- `[options.onLevel]` *(level: number) =\> void*
- `[options.key]` *string* - Save key. Default 'profile'.

**Returns** *Object* The profile API (see the returned object).

**Complexity** count / best: O(a) where a = achievements watching that statistic. Memory: O(stats + unlocks + history).

```js
const profile = Kit.profile({ save: ctx.save, achievements: ACHIEVEMENTS, levelCurve: { base: 50, growth: 1.4 } });
profile.count('kills');                 // lifetime counter, also checks achievements
profile.best('distance', 412);          // keeps the maximum
if (profile.isUnlocked('crossbow')) ... // gate content on the long arc
profile.endRun({ time: 73, kills: 40 });
```

#### `Kit.profile().stat(statName)`

Lifetime value of a statistic (0 if never recorded).

#### `Kit.profile().count(statName, amount = 1)`

Add to a lifetime counter.

**Returns** *number* the new total.

#### `Kit.profile().best(statName, value)`

Record a personal best: keeps the larger of the stored and given value.

**Returns** *number* the best.

#### `Kit.profile().unlock(id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.profile().isUnlocked(id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.profile().unlocked()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.profile().achievements()`

Every achievement with its live progress, for a trophy screen.

#### `Kit.profile().levelProgress()`

Progress through the current account level, 0..1.

#### `Kit.profile().gainExperience(amount)`

Add account experience; may gain several levels at once.

**Returns** *number* how many levels were gained.

#### `Kit.profile().runs()`

Finished runs, newest first, capped at RUN_HISTORY_LIMIT.

#### `Kit.profile().endRun(summary)`

Record a finished run (any plain-data summary).

#### `Kit.profile().on(listener)`

Subscribe to any change.

**Returns** *() =\> void* unsubscribe.

### `Kit.synergy(rows, { stats, onChange } = {})`

Set bonuses: a synergy is active while the player's build holds at least `needs[tag]` items of every tag it names.
This is what makes two builds different: the same items are worth more together than apart.

- `rows` *SynergyRow[]*
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.stats]` *Object* - A Kit.stats instance; active synergies' `mods` are applied to it automatically.
- `[options.onChange]` *(row: SynergyRow, active: boolean) =\> void* - Called when a synergy switches on or off.

**Returns** *{ update(items: Array, tagsOf?: Function): SynergyRow[], active(): SynergyRow[], progress(): Array, countTags: Function }*

**Complexity** update: O(items x tags + rows x needs). Memory: O(rows).

```js
const synergies = Kit.synergy(SYNERGIES, { stats });
synergies.update(ownedPowers, (power) => power.tags);   // call whenever the build changes
synergies.progress();  // [{ row, have: { fire: 1 }, active: false }] for "1 more fire item" hints
```

#### `Kit.synergy().countTags(items, tagsOf = (item) => item.tags || [])`

Count how many items carry each tag.

**Returns** *Record\<string, number\>*

#### `Kit.synergy().update(items, tagsOf)`

Recompute which synergies are active for the given build.

**Returns** *SynergyRow[]* the active rows.

#### `Kit.synergy().active()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.synergy().progress()`

Every synergy with how many of each needed tag the build has, for "one more to go" hints.

### Types

- **AchievementRow**
  - `id` *string*
  - `text` *string* - Shown to the player, e.g. 'Cleanse 100 souls'.
  - `stat` *string* - Lifetime statistic it watches (a name you pass to `count` or `best`).
  - `target` *number* - Value of that statistic at which it completes.
  - `[unlocks]` *string* - Id passed to `unlock` on completion (a character, a mode, a starting item).
  - `[reward]` *\** - Anything else; handed to `onAchievement`.
- **SynergyRow**
  - `id` *string*
  - `name` *string* - e.g. 'Pyromancer'
  - `needs` *Record\<string, number\>* - Tag counts required, e.g. { fire: 2, blade: 1 }.
  - `[mods]` *Object* - Kit.stats modifier spec applied while the synergy is active.
  - `[desc]` *string*

## flow.js

> Kit flow: how things change over time.
>
>   fsm        - states with enter / update / exit:    flow.go('fishing', { spot })
>   spawnTable - "what appears now":                   table.pick(t) from rows with { from, until, weight }
>   path       - composable 2D motion (t) => { x, y }: enemies, fish, moving platforms, camera rails
>   envelope   - a number fired to a peak, held, then eased back to zero: camera pushes, recoil, glow pulses
>
> Owns:  the time-driven models above.
> Does not own: the clock that drives them (Kit.clock in core.js, the frame loop in shell.js) or
>        anything visual. A path only returns numbers; the game decides what moves along it.
>
> No DOM, no THREE: runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### `Kit.fsm(states, initial, data)`

Creates a finite state machine.

- `states` *Object\<string, FsmState\>* - state name to hooks
- `[initial]` *string* - state to enter straight away
- `[data]` *\** - passed to the initial state's `enter`

**Returns** *{state: ?string, time: number, is: function(string): boolean, go: function(string, \*=): void, update: function(number): void, on: function(function({state: string, prev: ?string}): void): function(): boolean}* `state` and `time` (seconds in the current state) are read-only.

```js
const flow = Kit.fsm({
  aim:  { update: (dt) => trackFinger(dt) },
  fly:  { enter: (shot) => launch(shot), update: (dt, t) => t > 3 && flow.go('aim') },
}, 'aim');
flow.go('fly', { power: 0.8 });
```

#### `Kit.fsm().is(stateName)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.fsm().go(nextName, enterData)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.fsm().update(dt)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.fsm().on(listener)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.spawnTable(rows, { intro = [] } = {})`

Creates a spawn table: which rows may appear at a given time, level or depth `t`.

- `rows` *SpawnRow[]*
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.intro=[]` *SpawnRow[]* - ] rows that `next` hands out first, in order, to teach patterns

**Returns** *{rows: SpawnRow[], served: number, next: function(number): ?SpawnRow, active: function(number): SpawnRow[], pick: function(number): ?SpawnRow}* `pick` is a weighted random active row (null when none is active); `next` is the intro rows in order, then `pick`; `served` counts intro rows handed out so far.

**Complexity** `active`, `pick` and `next` are O(rows) time per call; `active` allocates the filtered list

```js
const spawns = Kit.spawnTable([{ id: 'grunt' }, { id: 'brute', from: 30, weight: 0.3 }], { intro: [{ id: 'grunt' }] });
const enemyType = spawns.next(elapsedSeconds);
```

#### `Kit.spawnTable().next(t)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.spawnTable().active(t)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.spawnTable().pick(t)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.envelope({ rise = 8, fall = 6 } = {})`

Envelope: a number that is fired up to a peak, held there, then eases back to zero. One small model for camera pushes,
recoil kicks, glow pulses and anything else that reacts to an event and then relaxes. Pure: advance it with any clock
(pass real seconds to ignore slow motion).

- `[rates]` *{ rise?: number, fall?: number }* - Smoothing rates per second toward the peak and back to zero. `rise: Infinity` snaps to the peak the moment it is fired.

**Returns** *{ value: number, fire(peak?: number, hold?: number): void, update(dt: number): number }*

```js
const push = Kit.envelope({ rise: 7, fall: 7 });
push.fire(1, 0.7);                       // on impact: lean in for 0.7s
camera.fov = 55 - 2 * push.update(dt);   // every frame
```

#### `Kit.envelope().fire(to = 1, hold = 0)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.envelope().update(dt)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### Types

- **FsmState** - Every hook is optional.
  - `[enter]` *function(\*, ?string): void* - called with (data passed to go, previous state name)
  - `[update]` *function(number, number): void* - called with (dt, seconds spent in this state)
  - `[exit]` *function(string): void* - called with the next state's name
- **SpawnRow** - Any extra fields are yours (enemy type, speed, colour).
  - `[weight=1]` *number* - relative chance while active; 0 never spawns
  - `[from=0]` *number* - first t (inclusive) at which the row is active
  - `[until=Infinity]` *number* - t (exclusive) at which it stops being active
- **Point**
- **Path** - Position at time t (seconds), in unit-ish space: generators stay within -1..1 on both axes.

## lanes.js

> Kit lanes: "can a player get through this?" for anything laid out as rows of side-by-side lanes
> (endless runners, rhythm highways, falling-block dodgers, river crossings).
>
>   lanes.open       - which lanes of one row are free:         lanes.open(['wall', null, 'wall'], (cell) => cell === 'wall')  // [1]
>   lanes.reachable  - lanes of the last row a player can be in: lanes.reachable([[1], [0, 1], [0]])                            // [0]
>   lanes.isPassable - is there any way through every row:       lanes.isPassable([[0], [0, 2], [2]])                           // false
>
> Owns:  the reachability arithmetic only. A row is just the list of lane indices that are open in it.
> Does not own: what makes a lane open (the caller decides which obstacles block), timing, or anything visual.
>
> No DOM, no THREE: runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### `Kit.lanes.open(cells, isBlocked)`

Lists the open lanes of one row of cells.

- `cells` *Array\<\*\>* - one entry per lane, left to right
- `isBlocked` *function(\*, number): boolean* - called with (cell, laneIndex); true when the lane cannot be passed

**Returns** *OpenLanes*

```js
Kit.lanes.open(['block', null, 'barrier'], (cell) => cell === 'block') // [1, 2]
```

### `Kit.lanes.reachable(rows, { reach = 1, from } = {})`

Walks the rows in order and returns the lanes of the last row the player can actually be standing in.
A lane counts only if it is open AND within `reach` of a lane that was reachable in the row before, so a row
whose only open lane is too far from where the player had to be makes everything after it unreachable.

- `rows` *OpenLanes[]* - first row first
- `[options]` *LaneOptions*

**Returns** *number[]* reachable lanes of the last row (empty when the rows cannot be passed, or when there are no rows and no `from`)

```js
Kit.lanes.reachable([[1], [0, 1, 2], [0, 2]], { reach: 1 }) // [0, 2]
```

### `Kit.lanes.isPassable(rows, options)`

True when at least one route leads through every row. Checking each neighbouring pair of rows on its own is not
enough: [[0], [0, 2], [2]] has a compatible lane in every pair but no single route, and this reports it as blocked.

- `rows` *OpenLanes[]* - first row first; no rows at all is trivially passable
- `[options]` *LaneOptions*

**Returns** *boolean*

```js
const isFair = Kit.lanes.isPassable(pattern.rows.map((row) => Kit.lanes.open(row.cells, (cell) => cell === 'wall')));
```

### Types

- **OpenLanes** - The open lanes of one row, as lane indices.
- **LaneOptions**
  - `[reach=1]` *number \| function(number): number* - how many lanes a player can shift between one row and the next: a constant, or a function of the index of the row being entered (so rows that are further apart can allow more)
  - `[from]` *number[]* - lanes the player may be in before the first row (default: any lane, so every open lane of the first row counts as reached)

## scrub.js

> Kit scrub: play a CSS animation on game time instead of wall-clock time.
>
> A CSS animation normally runs on the browser's clock, so it keeps going while the game is paused, frozen by hitstop
> or in slow motion. Kit.scrub pauses the animation and moves its playhead from a game-clock tween instead, so the
> same keyframes freeze and stretch with the game.
>
> Owns:  driving one element's animation playhead from a tween function.
> Does not own: the keyframes (the stylesheet), the clock (pass ctx.tween or Kit.clock().tween), or removing the element.
>
> Touches only `element.style`, so it runs in node with a stand-in element and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### `Kit.scrub(tween, element, seconds)`

Drives `element`'s CSS animation from a game clock.

- `tween` *function(Object, Object\<string, number\>, number, string): Promise\<void\>* - a game-time tween, e.g. `ctx.tween`
- `element` *{ style: Object }* - an element whose stylesheet rule already gives it an animation
- `seconds` *number* - game seconds the whole animation should take

**Returns** *Promise\<void\>* resolves when the animation has reached its end (remove the element then)

```js
Kit.scrub(ctx.tween, toastElement, 1.2).then(() => toastElement.remove());
```

## span.js

> Kit span: one-dimensional extents (a centre and a size on one axis). Pure, no DOM, no THREE - runs in node (see tools/test-kit.js).
>
>   span.edges   - { min, max } of a span:                 span.edges({ center: 0, size: 4 })
>   span.overlap - the shared part of two spans, or null:  span.overlap(a, b)
>   span.slice   - drop a piece onto a base and cut it:    span.slice(piece, base, { tolerance })   (stackers, trimmers, timing bars)
>   span.wrap    - fold a value into [0, length):          span.wrap(scroll, 48)                    (endless scrolling layers)
>
> Owns interval arithmetic only. It knows nothing about axes, meshes or scores: a caller maps its own x or z onto a span.

### `Kit.span.between(min, max)`

Builds a span from its two ends.

- `min` *number* - lower end
- `max` *number* - upper end

**Returns** *Span*

### `Kit.span.edges(span)`

The two ends of a span.

- `span` *Span*

**Returns** *{ min: number, max: number }*

### `Kit.span.overlap(first, second)`

The part two spans share.

- `first` *Span*
- `second` *Span*

**Returns** *Span \| null* null when they do not overlap (touching ends count as no overlap)

### `Kit.span.slice(piece, base, { tolerance = 0, minKeep = 0 } = {})`

Drops `piece` onto `base` and cuts off whatever hangs over the ends of the base.
Within `tolerance` of the base centre the piece is forgiven: it snaps to the centre and keeps its full size, even if it is wider than the base.

- `piece` *Span* - the moving piece where it was released
- `base` *Span* - what it lands on
- `[options]` *{ tolerance?: number, minKeep?: number }* - tolerance: centre distance that still counts as perfect (same units as the spans); minKeep: an overlap this small or smaller counts as a miss, so a sliver never becomes a floor

**Returns** *SpanSlice*

**Complexity** O(1) time and space.

```js
span.slice({ center: 1, size: 4 }, { center: 0, size: 4 })   // kept { center: 0.5, size: 3 }, cuts [{ center: 2.5, size: 1, side: 1 }]
```

### `Kit.span.wrap(value, length)`

Folds a value into [0, length), also for negative values (plain % keeps the sign in JavaScript).

- `value` *number*
- `length` *number* - period, > 0

**Returns** *number*

### Types

- **Span** - A stretch of one axis.
- **SpanCut** - One part of a sliced piece that hangs past the base.
- **SpanSlice** - The result of dropping a piece onto a base.
  - `kept` *Span \| null* - the part that stays (null on a miss)
  - `cuts` *SpanCut[]* - the overhanging parts, low side first (empty on a perfect drop)
  - `isPerfect` *boolean* - the piece was within `tolerance` of the base centre and snapped onto it whole
  - `isMiss` *boolean* - nothing worth keeping overlapped the base
  - `keptFraction` *number* - kept size / piece size, 0..1

## blend.js

> Kit blend: in-between values from data. Pure, no DOM, no THREE - runs in node (see tools/test-kit.js). Loads after model.js (uses Kit.curve).
>
>   blend.hex      - mix two 0xRRGGBB colours:                 blend.hex(0x000000, 0xffffff, 0.5)
>   blend.hsl      - a 0xRRGGBB colour from hue / sat / light: blend.hsl(0.56, 0.66, 0.6)
>   blend.rows     - mix two table rows field by field:        blend.rows(dusk, night, 0.3, { colors: ['sky'] })
>   blend.position - where x sits in a table of thresholds:    blend.position(ZONES, { key: 'from', span: 3 })(altitude)
>   blend.along    - the two above composed:                   blend.along(ZONES, { key: 'from', span: 3, colors: ['sky'] })(altitude)
>
> Use it for anything that changes gradually between authored rows: biomes by distance, moods by time of day, difficulty looks by level.
> Owns the mixing only; what a row means (a sky, a light rig, a palette) stays with the caller.

### `Kit.blend.hex(from, to, amount)`

Mixes two colours channel by channel in sRGB (the space the hex values are authored in).

- `from` *number* - 0xRRGGBB
- `to` *number* - 0xRRGGBB
- `amount` *number* - 0 = from, 1 = to (clamped)

**Returns** *number* 0xRRGGBB

### `Kit.blend.hsl(hue, saturation, lightness)`

Converts hue / saturation / lightness to a colour, so palettes can be walked by a number (a hue per floor, per level, per team).

- `hue` *number* - 0..1 around the colour wheel (wraps)
- `saturation` *number* - 0..1
- `lightness` *number* - 0..1

**Returns** *number* 0xRRGGBB

### `Kit.blend.rows(from, to, amount, { colors = [] } = {})`

Mixes two rows into a new one: numbers are interpolated, fields named in `colors` are mixed as 0xRRGGBB,
anything else (ids, names, flags) is taken from whichever row is nearer.

- `from` *Object* - row at amount 0
- `to` *Object* - row at amount 1
- `amount` *number* - 0..1
- `[options]` *{ colors?: string[] }* - colors: field names holding 0xRRGGBB values

**Returns** *Object* a new row with every field of `from`

### `Kit.blend.position(table, { key = 'from', span = 1 } = {})`

Maps a running value (altitude, distance, time) to a fractional row index: flat while inside a row, then ramping to the next
index over `span` units starting at that row's threshold. Row 0 always starts the table, whatever its threshold says.

- `table` *Object[]* - rows in ascending threshold order
- `[options]` *{ key?: string, span?: number }* - key: the threshold field (default 'from'); span: how long each change takes (> 0)

**Returns** *(x: number) =\> number* 0 .. table.length - 1

```js
blend.position([{ from: 0 }, { from: 10 }], { span: 4 })(12)   // 0.5: half way through the change that starts at 10
```

### `Kit.blend.along(table, { key = 'from', span = 1, colors = [], ease = smooth } = {})`

A function from a running value to the blended row at that value: `position` picks the two neighbouring rows, `rows` mixes them.

- `table` *Object[]* - rows in ascending threshold order
- `[options]` *{ key?: string, span?: number, colors?: string[], ease?: (amount: number) =\> number }* - as for `position` and `rows`; ease shapes each change (default smoothstep)

**Returns** *(x: number) =\> Object*

**Complexity** building: O(rows log rows). Each call: O(rows) to find the position plus O(fields) to mix, and one new object.

```js
const lookAt = Kit.blend.along(ZONES, { key: 'from', span: 3, colors: ['skyTop', 'skyBottom'] });
stage.setSky(lookAt(floor).skyTop, lookAt(floor).skyBottom);
```

### `Kit.blend.smooth(amount)`

Smoothstep: eases a 0..1 amount in and out so a change neither starts nor stops abruptly.

## space.js

> Kit space: discrete and drawn space.
>
>   grid    - 2D cell board: get / set, neighbours, rays, flood fill, shortest path, text-level parsing, world mapping
>   line    - polylines from a finger: length, point-at-distance, resample, simplify, nearest point, lasso tests
>   history - undo / redo over plain-data snapshots
>
> Owns:  the geometry and bookkeeping of boards and drawn lines, as plain data.
> Does not own: meshes for cells or lines (make.js), turning a touch into a ground point
>        (input.ground in input.js) or continuous physics (physics.js).
>
> No DOM, no THREE: runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### `Kit.history({ max = DEFAULT_HISTORY_LIMIT } = {})`

Creates an undo / redo stack of plain-data snapshots. Push the state after every move; `undo`
and `redo` return the state to restore (a copy), or null when there is nothing to step to.

- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.max=200]` *number* - snapshots kept; the oldest is dropped beyond this

**Returns** *{push: function(\*): void, canUndo: function(): boolean, canRedo: function(): boolean, undo: function(): \*, redo: function(): \*, reset: function(\*=): void, length: number}* `length` is the number of undo steps available.

```js
const moves = Kit.history();
moves.push(board.snapshot());                      // initial state
moves.push(board.snapshot());                      // after a move
if (moves.canUndo()) board.restore(moves.undo());  // back to the initial state
```

#### `Kit.history().push(state)`

#### `Kit.history().canUndo()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.history().canRedo()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.history().undo()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.history().redo()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.history().reset(state)`

Forgets everything; with an argument, starts again from that state.

### Types

- **Cell** - Column x (0 at the left) and row y (0 at the top).
- **CellTest** - Called with (value, x, y); true when the cell passes.
- **GridOptions**
  - `[cell=1]` *number* - cell size in world units
  - `[fill=null]` *\* \| function(number, number): \** - initial value, or a function (x, y) => value. A plain object is shared by every cell; use a function to give each cell its own.
- **Point** - Polylines are arrays of these.

## hash.js

> Kit hash: a uniform spatial hash for "what is near this point?" among many moving things.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.spatialHash(cellSize)` - buckets of items by the square cell their centre falls in, rebuilt each frame.
> Does not own: what "near" means exactly (the caller tests the true distance of each candidate), the items
>        themselves, or fixed tile worlds (`Kit.grid` in kit/space.js). Pure: no DOM, no THREE; unit-tested in
>        tools/test-kit.js.
>
> Use it instead of comparing every pair: with n things spread over an area, all-pairs is O(n^2) per frame while
> insert-everything-then-query-each is O(n + n * k), k being the few things that share the queried cells.

### `Kit.spatialHash(cellSize)`

Makes a spatial hash over an unbounded plane.

- `cellSize` *number* - side of a cell in world units. Choose about the usual query diameter: much smaller and one query walks many cells, much larger and each cell holds many items.

**Returns** *SpatialHash*

**Complexity** clear O(buckets used), insert O(1), query O(cells touched + items in them); O(items + buckets) space. Nothing is allocated per frame once the buckets exist.

```js
const near = Kit.spatialHash(2);
near.clear();
enemies.forEach((enemy) => near.insert(enemy, enemy.x, enemy.z));
near.query(hero.x, hero.z, 3, (enemy) => { if (distance(enemy, hero) < 3) hit(enemy); });
```

#### `Kit.spatialHash().clear()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.spatialHash().insert(item, x, y)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.spatialHash().query(x, y, radius, visit)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.spatialHash().size()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### Types

- **SpatialHash**
  - `clear` *function(): void* - forget every item (start of a frame); keeps the buckets' memory for reuse
  - `insert` *function(\*, number, number): void* - insert(item, x, y): file an item under the cell holding (x, y)
  - `query` *function(number, number, number, function(\*): void): void* - query(x, y, radius, visit): call `visit(item)` for every item filed in a cell that the square of half-side `radius` around (x, y) touches. That is a superset of the items within `radius`: test the exact distance in `visit`. To catch big items by their edge rather than their centre, add the largest item radius to `radius`.
  - `size` *function(): number* - items currently filed

## music.js

> Kit music: pitch maths for the synth. Pure: names and numbers in, frequencies out; no DOM, no WebAudio
> (unit-tested in tools/test-kit.js).
>
>   noteToMidi  - 'A4' / 'C#5' / 'Bb3' -> MIDI note number:           Kit.music.noteToMidi('A4')        // 69
>   midiToFreq  - MIDI note number -> hertz (equal temperament):      Kit.music.midiToFreq(69)          // 440
>   noteToFreq  - note name -> hertz:                                  Kit.music.noteToFreq('C4')        // 261.63
>   midiToNote  - MIDI note number -> name, for labels:                Kit.music.midiToNote(61)          // 'C#4'
>   scale       - (degree) => hertz over a root and a named scale:     Kit.music.scale('C4', 'pentatonic')(5)   // C5
>
> Owns: note names, equal-temperament tuning and scale degrees. Does not own: making sound (pass the frequency to
> Kit.sfx.tone({ freq })), rhythm or sequencing.

### `Kit.music.SCALES`

Named scales as semitone offsets from the root within one octave. Add a row to add a scale.

### `Kit.music.noteToMidi(name)`

Convert a note name to its MIDI note number.

- `name` *string* - letter, optional '#' or 'b', then the octave: 'A4', 'C#5', 'Bb3'.

**Returns** *number* MIDI note number (C4 = 60, A4 = 69).

### `Kit.music.midiToFreq(midi)`

Frequency of a MIDI note in twelve-tone equal temperament (each semitone multiplies by 2^(1/12)).

- `midi` *number* - MIDI note number; fractions are allowed (microtones, pitch bends).

**Returns** *number* hertz.

### `Kit.music.noteToFreq(name)`

Frequency of a named note.

- `name` *string* - see noteToMidi.

**Returns** *number* hertz.

### `Kit.music.midiToNote(midi)`

Name of a MIDI note, spelled with sharps, for HUD labels.

- `midi` *number* - whole MIDI note number.

**Returns** *string* for example 'C#4'.

### `Kit.music.scale(root, kind = 'major')`

Build a scale: a function from scale degree to frequency, with the notes it uses available for labels.

- `root` *string\|number* - note name ('C4') or MIDI number of degree 0.
- `[kind='major']` *string\|number[]* - a SCALES name, or your own semitone offsets.

**Returns** *((degree: number) =\> number) & { midi: (degree: number) =\> number, noteName: (degree: number) =\> string }* call it for hertz; `.midi(degree)` and `.noteName(degree)` describe the same note.

```js
const chime = Kit.music.scale('C4', 'pentatonic');
Kit.sfx.tone({ freq: chime(3), dur: 0.4 });   // G4
chime.noteName(5);                            // 'C5' (one octave above the root)
```

## place.js

> Kit place: drag pieces from a tray onto a grid, move them, tap them, drag them off again. Builders, puzzles, decorators.
>
>   placement      - the gesture as a pure state machine: points in, one resolved action out
>                    const drag = Kit.placement({ cellAt, trayKindAt, pieceAt, canPlace });
>                    drag.begin(point); drag.move(point); const action = drag.end(point);
>   pointerOnPlane - where the pointer ray meets any plane (upright boards, tilted tables); needs THREE at call time
>
> Owns: deciding what a press-drag-release means (place / move / remove / tap / cancel) and which cell is hovered.
> Does not own: the board contents (the game applies the returned action to its own layout, e.g. a Kit.grid plus
> Kit.history), hit-testing of the tray, or any drawing of ghosts and highlights. `placement` is unit-tested in
> tools/test-kit.js; `pointerOnPlane` is the only function here that touches THREE.

### Types

- **PlaceAction** - A pointer position in whatever space the game's callbacks understand.
  - `source` *'tray'\|'board'* - where the drag started.
  - `kind` *\** - the tray kind picked up, or the kind of the board piece.
  - `piece` *\** - the board piece being moved (null for a tray pick).
  - `from` *PlaceCell\|null* - the cell the board piece came from (null for a tray pick).
  - `held` *PlaceHeld*
  - `point` *PlacePoint*
  - `cell` *PlaceCell\|null* - the cell under the pointer, or null when off the board.
  - `isValid` *boolean* - true when releasing here would place or move the piece.
  - `isDragging` *boolean* - false until the pointer has travelled further than tapSlop.
  - `type` *'place'\|'move'\|'remove'\|'tap'\|'cancel'*
  - `held` *PlaceHeld*
  - `cell` *PlaceCell\|null* - the target cell for place / move, the piece's own cell for tap, else null.

## puzzle.js

> Kit puzzle: the rule-free parts of a turn-based board puzzle. Pure, no THREE; runs in node (see tools/test-kit.js).
>
>   beam   - light travelling over a Kit.grid:   Kit.beam.trace(grid, emitters, { optics, lights }) -> { segments, lit, passes }
>   search - fewest-moves solution of any puzzle: Kit.search.shortest({ start, keyOf, movesOf, isGoal }) -> { moves, explored }
>            how hard that was:                   Kit.search.difficulty(solution)
>            generate-and-verify:                 Kit.search.generate({ propose, solve, accept }) -> { puzzle, solution }
>   levels - progress through a list of levels:   Kit.levels(rows, { save }).complete('fork', 4, ['tidy']) -> { stars, isBest, newMedals }
>
> Owns: how light moves between cells and what the stock optics do to it, state-space search, star maths, unlock order.
> Does not own: what a level contains, which moves a game allows, or how anything is drawn. The one function that touches
> the DOM is Kit.levels.select, and it only does so through the ctx.ui object passed to it.

### `Kit.beam.trace(grid, emitters, options = {})`

Follow light from its emitters across a grid until every ray is absorbed, leaves the board or repeats itself.
Cells whose kind has no optic are transparent, so targets may sit in the path of a ray.

- `grid` *object* - - a Kit.grid
- `emitters` *Beam[]* - - rays to start with (light begins in the cell after the emitter's own)
- `[options]` *object*
- `[options.optics]` *Object\<string, Optic\>* - - optic per cell kind; kinds not listed let light through
- `[options.kindOf]` *(cell: \*) =\> string* - - how to read a cell's kind (default `cell.kind`)
- `[options.lights]` *(cell: \*, color: \*) =\> boolean* - - does light of this colour light this cell? (default: nothing is a target)

**Returns** *{segments: BeamSegment[], lit: {x: number, y: number, color: \*, distance: number}[], passes: {x: number, y: number, color: \*, distance: number}[]}* `lit` lists each lit cell once, with the cells travelled from the emitter when light first reached it. `passes` lists every distinct (cell, colour) pair that lit a cell, for targets that need several colours at once.

**Complexity** O(cells x headings x colours) time and space: each (cell, heading, colour) ray is walked at most once, and a walk is at most max(width, height) cells. A 6 x 7 board with three colours traces in a few microseconds.

```js
const grid = Kit.grid.parse(['>./', '..*'], { '/': { kind: 'mirror', slant: 1 }, '*': { kind: 'target' } });
  Kit.beam.trace(grid, [{ x: 0, y: 0, dir: 0, color: 'white' }], { optics: Kit.beam.optics, lights: (cell) => !!cell && cell.kind === 'target' });
```

### `Kit.beam.reflect(dir, slant)`

Direction of light after bouncing off a diagonal mirror.

- `dir` *number* - - incoming DIRS4 index
- `slant` *number* - - 0 for a "/" mirror (rises to the east), 1 for a "\" mirror (falls to the east)

**Returns** *number* outgoing DIRS4 index

```js
Kit.beam.reflect(0, 0) // 3: light heading east leaves a "/" mirror heading north

With rows growing downward, "/" swaps east with north and south with west (0<->3, 1<->2, i.e. 3 - dir);
"\" swaps east with south and west with north (0<->1, 2<->3, i.e. flip the lowest bit).
```

### `Kit.beam.optics`

Stock optics, keyed by the names a game is likely to use as `cell.kind`. Compose a game's own table from these.
mirror and splitter read `cell.slant` (see reflect); filter reads `cell.color`; oneWay reads `cell.dir`.

### `Kit.search.shortest({ start, keyOf, movesOf, isGoal, limit = DEFAULT_SEARCH_LIMIT })`

Breadth-first search for the shortest list of moves that reaches a goal. Works for any puzzle whose states can be
given a string key: sliding blocks, light routing, sokoban, lights-out.

- `problem` *object*
- `problem.start` *\** - - the state to search from (treated as immutable)
- `problem.keyOf` *(state: \*) =\> string* - - equal states must give equal keys
- `problem.movesOf` *(state: \*) =\> {move: \*, state: \*}[]* - - every legal move from a state and the state it leads to
- `problem.isGoal` *(state: \*) =\> boolean*
- `[problem.limit]` *number* - - give up after exploring this many distinct states

**Returns** *{moves: \*[], explored: number} \| null* the moves in order (empty when `start` is already a goal), or null when no goal is reachable within the limit

**Complexity** O(states x moves per state) time, O(states) space, both capped by `limit`: breadth-first search visits every state closer than the goal. Keep `movesOf` tight (list only moves that can matter) before raising the limit.

```js
Kit.search.shortest({ start: 1, keyOf: String, isGoal: (n) => n === 10,
    movesOf: (n) => [{ move: '+1', state: n + 1 }, { move: 'x2', state: n * 2 }] }).moves // ['+1', 'x2', '+1', 'x2']
```

### `Kit.search.difficulty(solution)`

How hard a solved puzzle is, as one number: the moves a perfect player needs plus the base-2 logarithm of the states
a blind search had to look at (how much there is to consider). A one-tap tutorial rates about 2, a bed with a
dozen interacting pieces about 15.

- `solution` *{moves: \*[], explored: number}* - - a result of shortest()

**Returns** *number*

```js
Kit.search.difficulty({ moves: ['a', 'b', 'c'], explored: 64 }) // 9
```

### `Kit.search.generate({ propose, solve, accept = () => true, attempts = DEFAULT_GENERATE_ATTEMPTS })`

Generate-and-verify: propose puzzles until one is proven solvable and good enough. Nothing unverified ever comes back.

- `recipe` *object*
- `recipe.propose` *(attempt: number) =\> \** - - a candidate puzzle for this attempt (0, 1, 2, ...), or null to skip it
- `recipe.solve` *(puzzle: \*) =\> ({moves: \*[], explored: number} \| null)* - - usually a call to shortest()
- `[recipe.accept]` *(solution: object, puzzle: \*) =\> boolean* - - is this one good enough? Default: any puzzle that needs a move
- `[recipe.attempts]` *number*

**Returns** *{puzzle: \*, solution: object, attempt: number, isAccepted: boolean} \| null* the first accepted candidate; failing that the hardest one that needs at least one move (by difficulty()) with isAccepted false; null when every candidate was unsolvable or already solved

**Complexity** O(attempts x cost of solve). With shortest() as the solver that is attempts x its state limit at worst.

```js
Kit.search.generate({ propose: (attempt) => scramble(rng), solve: (board) => Kit.search.shortest(problemFor(board)),
    accept: (solution) => solution.moves.length >= 4 })
```

### Types

- **Beam** - A ray of light leaving a cell.
  - `x` *number* - - column of the cell the light leaves from
  - `y` *number* - - row of the cell the light leaves from
  - `dir` *number* - - index into Kit.grid.DIRS4 (0 east, 1 south, 2 west, 3 north)
  - `color` *\** - - opaque colour value; only filters and the `lights` test give it meaning
- **BeamSegment** - One straight run of light, in the order the tracer found it.
  - `from` *{x: number, y: number}* - - cell the run leaves from (an emitter or the optic that produced it)
  - `to` *{x: number, y: number}* - - last cell the run reaches: the optic that stopped it, or the last cell on the board
  - `dir` *number* - - index into Kit.grid.DIRS4
  - `color` *\** - - colour carried along the run
  - `start` *number* - - cells travelled from the emitter before this run begins (for animating in route order)
  - `length` *number* - - cells covered by the run, `from` to `to`
  - `leaves` *boolean* - - true when the run ended by leaving the board rather than at an optic
- **LevelRow** - A level as the progress model sees it; games add their own fields (rows, tips, ...).
  - `id` *string*
  - `name` *string* - - shown in the level-select sheet
  - `par` *number* - - the score (moves, strokes, seconds: lower is better) that earns every star

## route.js

> Kit route: drawn routes and the things that travel along them. Pure, no DOM, no THREE - runs in node (see tools/test-kit.js).
>
>   route  - a finger stroke becomes a clean polyline between anchors:   Kit.route.capture(stroke, { anchors, snapRadius })
>   follow - a position travelling along a polyline, ping-pong or loop:  Kit.follow.advance(follower, distance, track)
>
> Owns: stroke clean-up, anchor snapping, stop detection, ribbon outlines and follower stepping.
> Does not own: the polyline maths itself (Kit.line in space.js), rendering, input, or what a stop means to a game.
> Points are { x, y } exactly like Kit.line (use the ground plane's x / z as x / y).

### `Kit.route.snap(point, anchors, radius)`

The anchor closest to a point, if any is within reach.

- `point` *Point*
- `anchors` *Anchor[]*
- `radius` *number* - maximum snapping distance, world units

**Returns** *Anchor\|null*

**Complexity** O(anchors) time, O(1) space

### `Kit.route.smooth(points, passes = 1)`

Round the corners of a polyline without moving its ends.

- `points` *Point[]*
- `[passes=1]` *number* - each pass doubles the point count and halves the sharpness of every corner

**Returns** *Point[]*

### `Kit.route.clean(stroke, { tolerance = 0.2, smoothPasses = 2, step = 0.3 } = {})`

Turn a shaky stroke into an evenly spaced, gently curved polyline: drop jitter, round corners, resample.

- `stroke` *Point[]*
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.tolerance=0.2]` *number* - jitter smaller than this (world units) is removed
- `[options.smoothPasses=2]` *number* - corner-rounding passes
- `[options.step=0.3]` *number* - spacing of the output points, world units

**Returns** *Point[]*

### `Kit.route.stops(points, anchors, radius)`

Every anchor a polyline passes within `radius` of, ordered along the line.

- `points` *Point[]*
- `anchors` *Anchor[]*
- `radius` *number* - world units

**Returns** *Stop[]*

**Complexity** O(anchors x points) time, O(anchors) space

### `Kit.route.capture(stroke, options)`

Capture a drag as a route between anchors: both ends snap to an anchor, the route is pinned to every anchor the stroke
passes on the way, each section is cleaned, and the result is refused (with a reason the game can show) when it is
too short, starts or ends nowhere, or crosses blocked ground.

- `stroke` *Point[]* - raw drag samples in world units, in the order they were drawn
- `options` *Object*
- `options.anchors` *Anchor[]*
- `options.snapRadius` *number* - how close an end must be to an anchor, world units
- `[options.stopRadius=snapRadius]` *number* - how close the stroke must pass an anchor to call there
- `[options.isBlocked]` *(point: Point) =\> boolean* - true where a route may not go (land, walls)
- `[options.minLength=0]` *number* - shortest allowed route, world units
- `[options.minLoopLength=minLength]` *number* - shortest allowed route that returns to its own start
- `[options.tolerance]` *number*
- `[options.smoothPasses]` *number*
- `[options.step]` *number* - see Kit.route.clean

**Returns** *CaptureResult*

**Complexity** O(points x anchors) time, O(points) space

```js
const route = Kit.route.capture(stroke, { anchors: docks, snapRadius: 1, isBlocked: isLand });
  if (route.ok) launchBoat(route.points, route.stops); else ui.toast(REASONS[route.reason]);
```

### `Kit.route.ribbon(points, width)`

The two edges of a ribbon of the given width centred on a polyline, for building a flat mesh strip.

- `points` *Point[]*
- `width` *number* - world units

**Returns** *{ left: Point, right: Point }[]* one pair per input point, left / right of the direction of travel

**Complexity** O(points) time and space

### `Kit.follow.start(along = 0, direction = 1)`

A follower at the start of its track (or anywhere on it).

- `[along=0]` *number* - distance from the start, world units
- `[direction=1]` *1\|-1* - 1 = toward the end

**Returns** *Follower*

### `Kit.follow.advance(follower, distance, track)`

Move a follower along its track. It stops on the first mark it reaches (so the caller can dwell there) and reports it;
otherwise it travels the whole distance, turning round (pingpong) or wrapping (loop) at the ends.

- `follower` *Follower*
- `distance` *number* - how far to travel this step, world units (speed * dt)
- `track` *Track*

**Returns** *Advance*

```js
const moved = Kit.follow.advance(boat.follower, speed * dt, { length, mode: 'pingpong', marks: stops.map((stop) => stop.along) });
  if (moved.hit >= 0) unloadAt(stops[moved.hit]);
```

### `Kit.follow.pose(points, follower, isLoop = false)`

World position and heading of a follower on a polyline.

- `points` *Point[]*
- `follower` *Follower*
- `[isLoop=false]` *boolean* - wrap `along` instead of clamping it

**Returns** *{ x: number, y: number, tx: number, ty: number }* position plus unit heading (already reversed on the way back)

### Types

- **Point**
  - `x` *number*
  - `y` *number*
- **Anchor** - A named place a route can start, end or call at (a dock, a station, a socket).
  - `id` *string\|number*
  - `x` *number*
  - `y` *number*
- **Stop** - Where a route passes an anchor.
  - `id` *string\|number* - the anchor's id
  - `along` *number* - distance from the start of the route, in world units
- **CaptureResult**
  - `ok` *boolean*
  - `[reason]` *string* - when not ok: 'too-short' | 'no-start' | 'no-end' | 'blocked'
  - `[points]` *Point[]* - when ok: the cleaned polyline, first and last point exactly on their anchors
  - `[length]` *number* - when ok: length of `points` in world units
  - `[isLoop]` *boolean* - when ok: the stroke came back to the anchor it started from
  - `[stops]` *Stop[]* - when ok: every anchor the route calls at, ordered by distance along it
- **Follower** - A position on a track: how far along it is and which way it is heading.
  - `along` *number* - distance from the start of the track, world units
  - `direction` *1\|-1* - 1 = toward the end, -1 = back toward the start
- **Track**
  - `length` *number* - total length, world units
  - `[mode]` *'pingpong'\|'loop'* - pingpong (default) turns round at each end; loop wraps from the end to the start
  - `[marks]` *number[]* - distances along the track where the follower must stop (stations, docks)
- **Advance**
  - `along` *number*
  - `direction` *1\|-1*
  - `hit` *number* - index into track.marks of the mark it stopped on, or -1
  - `left` *number* - distance it did not travel because it stopped on a mark

## timer.js

> Kit timer: countdowns as plain data. Pure, no DOM, no THREE - runs in node (see tools/test-kit.js).
>
>   Kit.timer.start(30)            a countdown: { duration, left }
>   Kit.timer.tick(timer, dt)      a new timer with dt less on it (never below zero)
>   Kit.timer.fraction(timer)      1 when fresh, 0 when expired (patience rings, cooldown wipes)
>   Kit.timer.tickAll(items, dt)   tick the timer on every item and split them into { running, expired }
>   Kit.timer.cycle(timer, dt)     a repeating timer: how many times it fired in dt, remainder carried over
>
> Owns: the arithmetic of patience, cooldowns and repeating production. Does not own: scheduling callbacks (ctx.after /
> ctx.every do that) or what happens on expiry - timers live inside a game's own state and the game decides.

### `Kit.timer.start(duration)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

- `duration` *number* - seconds

**Returns** *Timer*

### `Kit.timer.tick(timer, dt)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

- `timer` *Timer*
- `dt` *number* - seconds elapsed

**Returns** *Timer* a new timer; the input is not changed

### `Kit.timer.fraction(timer)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

- `timer` *Timer*

**Returns** *number* remaining share, 1 (fresh) down to 0 (expired)

### `Kit.timer.isExpired(timer)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

- `timer` *Timer*

**Returns** *boolean*

### `Kit.timer.tickAll(items, dt, key = 'timer')`

Tick the timer carried by each item and separate the ones that ran out.

- `items` *Item[]* - objects holding a Timer under `key`
- `dt` *number* - seconds elapsed
- `[key='timer']` *string*

**Returns** *{ running: Item[], expired: Item[] }* copies with ticked timers, in their original order

**Complexity** O(items) time and space

```js
const { running, expired } = Kit.timer.tickAll(orders, dt);
  expired.forEach(loseHeart);
```

### `Kit.timer.cycle(timer, dt)`

Advance a repeating timer. Large steps fire several times; the overshoot is carried so the rhythm never drifts.

- `timer` *Timer*
- `dt` *number* - seconds elapsed

**Returns** *{ timer: Timer, fired: number }*

```js
const { timer, fired } = Kit.timer.cycle(dock.production, dt);   // fired crates were made during dt
```

### Types

- **Timer**
  - `duration` *number* - seconds it started with
  - `left` *number* - seconds remaining, 0 when expired

## loot.js

> Kit loot: randomness with structure.
>
>   rng         - seedable random source (same seed, same run): next / range / int / chance / pick / weighted / shuffle / fork
>   stats.power, stats.merge - modifier-spec algebra used to build an item's stats
>   loot        - item generator: base x rarity x affixes -> plain-data item whose `mods` plug straight into Kit.stats
>   inventory   - a bag plus equipment slots; equipping pushes the item's mods into Kit.stats
>
> Owns:  seeded randomness, item rolling and the bag / equipment model.
> Does not own: unseeded helpers (Kit.math in core.js), the stat block the mods feed (Kit.stats in
>        model.js) or any inventory screen (ui.panel in ui.js).
>
> No DOM, no THREE: runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### `Kit.rng(seed = randomSeed())`

Creates a seedable random source (mulberry32): the same seed always gives the same sequence.

- `[seed]` *number* - 32-bit integer; omitted = a random one, readable back from `rng.seed`

**Returns** *Rng*

**Complexity** `next`, `range`, `int`, `chance` and `pick` are O(1); `weighted` is O(rows); `shuffle` is O(n) time and space

```js
const rng = Kit.rng(42);
const lootRng = rng.fork();
rng.int(1, 6); rng.pick(['red', 'green']); rng.weighted(ENEMIES);
```

#### `Kit.rng().next()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.rng().range(low, high)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.rng().int(low, high)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.rng().chance(probability)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.rng().weighted(rows, weightOf = weightOrOne)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.rng().fork()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.stats.power(spec, strength)`

Scales the strength of a modifier spec: adds scale directly, and a mul's bonus (its distance
from 1) scales, so strength 2 turns "x1.1" into "x1.2" and strength 0 into "x1".

- `spec` *Object\<string, {add?: number, mul?: number}\>*
- `strength` *number* - multiplier (1 leaves the spec as it is)

**Returns** *Object\<string, {add: number, mul: number}\>*

```js
Kit.stats.power({ damage: { add: 4, mul: 1.2 } }, 2) // { damage: { add: 8, mul: 1.4 } }
```

### `Kit.stats.merge(specs)`

Combines several modifier specs into one: adds sum, muls multiply.

- `specs` *Array\<Object\<string, {add?: number, mul?: number}\>\>*

**Returns** *Object\<string, {add: number, mul: number}\>* one entry per stat named in any spec ({} for no specs)

```js
Kit.stats.merge([{ damage: { add: 4 } }, { damage: { mul: 1.2 } }]) // { damage: { add: 4, mul: 1.2 } }
```

### `Kit.loot({ rarities, bases, affixes = [], power = DEFAULT_POWER_CURVE, rng = Kit.rng() })`

Creates an item generator.

- `definition` *Object*
- `definition.rarities` *RarityRow[]* - ordered common -> best
- `definition.bases` *BaseRow[]*
- `[definition.affixes=[]` *AffixRow[]* - ]
- `[definition.power]` *\** - curve spec (see Kit.curve.of): level -> strength multiplier (default 1 + 0.15 per level)
- `[definition.rng]` *Rng* - defaults to a randomly seeded one

**Returns** *{rarities: RarityRow[], bases: BaseRow[], affixes: AffixRow[], rng: Rng, roll: function({level?: number, luck?: number, slot?: string, rarity?: string}=): LootItem, describe: function(LootItem, Object\<string, string\>=): string[]}*
