own position, turn, stretch and HDR colour. Use it when the things are plain objects in an array (enemies, shots,
pickups) that come and go; use make.instanced for a fixed set of slots you move by index.

- `geometry` *THREE.BufferGeometry* - shared shape, e.g. `make.prism(6, 1, 0.1, null, { parent: null }).geometry`
- `capacity` *number* - most instances drawn in one frame
- `[options]` *{ y?: number, material?: THREE.Material, parent?: THREE.Object3D }* - y: height of the plane the instances lie on (default 0); material: defaults to white unlit, so `color` is the colour seen

**Returns** *Batch*

```js
const shots = make.batch(make.box(1, 0.1, 1, null, { parent: null }).geometry, 160, { y: 0.3 });
shots.begin(); bullets.forEach((bullet) => shots.put(bullet.x, bullet.z, bullet.angle, 0.2, 1, SHOT_COLOR, 2.5)); shots.end();
```

#### `Kit.make().canvasTexture(width, height, draw, options = {})`

A texture you paint with canvas 2D.

- `width` *number* - pixels
- `height` *number* - pixels
- `draw` *function(CanvasRenderingContext2D, number, number): void* - called once with (context, width, height)
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.repeat]` *[number, number]* - tile it [across, down]
- `[options.pixel]` *boolean* - nearest-neighbour filtering, for crisp pixel art
- `[options.anisotropy]` *number* - e.g. 8 keeps a ground texture sharp at a grazing angle (roads, floors running to the horizon)

**Returns** *THREE.CanvasTexture*

```js
make.canvasTexture(256, 256, (context, width, height) => { context.fillRect(0, 0, width / 2, height / 2); }, { repeat: [4, 4], pixel: true })
```

#### `Kit.make().mesh(geometry, hexOrMaterial, options = {})`

Builds a mesh from any geometry; the base of every shape helper.

- `geometry` *THREE.BufferGeometry*
- `hexOrMaterial` *number \| THREE.Material*
- `[options]` *MeshOptions*

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh* casting and receiving shadows unless `shadow: false`

#### `Kit.make().box(width, height, depth, hexOrMaterial, options)`

Box of width x height x depth (world units).

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh*

#### `Kit.make().sphere(radius, hexOrMaterial, options)`

Sphere of a radius.

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh*

#### `Kit.make().cyl(radiusTop, radiusBottom, height, hexOrMaterial, options = {})`

Cylinder (or cone frustum) with top and bottom radii; `options.seg` sets the radial segments.

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh*

#### `Kit.make().cone(radius, height, hexOrMaterial, options = {})`

Cone; `options.seg` sets the radial segments.

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh*

#### `Kit.make().ico(radius, detail, hexOrMaterial, options = {})`

Low-poly rock / gem: an icosahedron (detail 0 = 20 faces), flat-shaded unless overridden.

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh*

#### `Kit.make().prism(sides, radius, height, hexOrMaterial, options = {})`

Flat n-sided shape lying in the ground plane (triangle, diamond, hexagon ...) for top-down 2D looks.

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh*

#### `Kit.make().group(options = {})`

An empty group, for moving several meshes as one.

- `[options]` *{at?: number[], parent?: THREE.Object3D}*

**Returns** *THREE.Group*

#### `Kit.make().ground(size = DEFAULT_GROUND_SIZE, hexOrMaterial = DEFAULT_GROUND_COLOR, options = {})`

A flat ground plane at y = 0 that receives shadows.

- `[size=60]` *number* - side length, world units
- `[hexOrMaterial=0x2a3350]` *number \| THREE.Material*
- `[options]` *MeshOptions & {grid?: number, cells?: number, gridOpacity?: number}* - `grid`: overlay a subtle grid of this colour; `cells`: grid divisions (default one per 2 units); `gridOpacity` (default 0.25)

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh*

#### `Kit.make().stage(options = {})`

One call for the sky gradient, fog, a hemisphere light and a shadow-casting sun.

- `[options]` *StageOptions*

**Returns** *{sun: THREE.DirectionalLight, hemi: THREE.HemisphereLight, follow: function({x: number, y: number, z: number}): void, setSky: function(number, number, number=): void}* `follow(point)` keeps shadows centred on a moving focus (call it each frame or when the focus moves); `setSky(top, bottom, fogColor = bottom)` repaints the sky at any time, e.g. when blending between altitude zones

```js
const stage = make.stage({ sky: [0x86c5ff, 0xe8f4ff], fog: [30, 90], sun: [-6, 12, 7] });
stage.follow(player.position);
```

#### `Kit.make().glow(hex = 0xffffff, size = 1, options = {})`

An additive soft glow sprite: a cheap halo for pickups, projectiles and lamps. It is
depth-tested by default, so one at floor level is cut by the floor: lift it or pass `depthTest: false`.

- `[hex=0xffffff]` *number* - 0xRRGGBB
- `[size=1]` *number* - diameter, world units
- `[options]` *{opacity?: number, depthTest?: boolean, at?: number[], parent?: (THREE.Object3D\|null)}* - opacity default 0.9

**Returns** *THREE.Sprite*

#### `Kit.make().frame(hex = 0xffffff, options = {})`

A flat square outline lying in the ground plane, 1 x 1 on the outside: scale x / z to fit any footprint.
Landing markers, selection boxes, tile highlights; fx.frame animates one as a pulse.

- `[hex]` *number* - 0xRRGGBB
- `[options]` *{ thickness?: number, bright?: number, opacity?: number, at?: number[], parent?: THREE.Object3D \| null }* - thickness: border width as a fraction of the side (default 0.06); bright > 1 blooms; opacity 0..1; at / parent as for mesh()

**Returns** *THREE.Mesh* unlit and double-sided with its own material, so it is safe to recolour or fade

```js
const marker = make.frame(0xffd27a, { bright: 1.6, at: [0, 0.02, 0] });
marker.scale.set(4, 1, 4);
```

#### `Kit.make().dots(count, options = {})`

A field of screen-sized points that can scroll along y for ever: two copies stacked one `height` apart, wrapped with Kit.span.wrap.
Stars, snow, dust motes, speed streaks. Keep the visible window no taller than `height`.

- `count` *number* - points per copy
- `[options]` *{ width?: number, height?: number, depth?: number[], color?: number, size?: number, bright?: number, parent?: THREE.Object3D }* - width / height: extent of one copy in the parent's units; depth: [near, far] distance behind the parent's origin (-z); color: 0xRRGGBB; size: point size in pixels; bright > 1 blooms

**Returns** *{ group: THREE.Group, material: THREE.PointsMaterial, scroll(offset: number): void, fade(weight: number): void }* scroll: shift the field up by `offset` units (any value, it wraps); fade: 0..1 opacity, hidden near 0

```js
const stars = make.dots(170, { depth: [46, 70], size: 2.2, bright: 1.8, parent: skyGroup });
stars.scroll(-altitude * 0.25); stars.fade(look.stars);
```

### Types

- **MaterialOptions**
  - `[rough=0.6]` *number* - roughness 0 (mirror) .. 1 (matt)
  - `[metal=0]` *number* - metalness 0..1
  - `[emissive=0x000000]` *number* - self-lit colour, 0xRRGGBB
  - `[emissiveIntensity=1]` *number* - above 1 the emissive colour blooms
  - `[opacity=1]` *number* - below 1 the material becomes transparent
  - `[flat=false]` *boolean* - faceted (flat-shaded) look
  - `[fresh=false]` *boolean* - make an uncached copy, for a material you will change per object
- **UnlitOptions**
  - `[opacity=1]` *number*
  - `[additive=false]` *boolean* - add to what is behind (light beams, neon)
  - `[bright=1]` *number* - above 1 pushes the colour past the bloom threshold so it glows
  - `[fresh=false]` *boolean* - make an uncached copy
- **MeshOptions** - Options every shape helper takes: the material options (used when the colour argument is a hex), `at: [x, y, z]`, `parent` (default: the scene; null = do not add anywhere), `shadow: false` to neither cast nor receive, and `seg` for the radial segments of cyl / cone.
- **StageOptions**
  - `[sky]` *[number, number]* - [top, bottom] gradient colours, 0xRRGGBB
  - `[fog]` *[number, number]* - [near, far] distances in world units; omitted = no fog
  - `[fogColor]` *number* - defaults to the sky's bottom colour, so the horizon blends
  - `[sun]` *[number, number, number]* - sun position relative to the focus point
  - `[sunColor]` *number*
  - `[sunIntensity=1.25]` *number*
  - `[skyLight]` *number* - hemisphere light colour from above
  - `[groundLight]` *number* - hemisphere light colour from below
  - `[ambient=0.7]` *number* - hemisphere light intensity
  - `[shadow=16]` *number* - half-width of the shadowed area round the focus, world units
- **Batch**
  - `mesh` *THREE.InstancedMesh* - the one mesh (and draw call) behind every instance
  - `begin` *function(): void* - start a frame: forget last frame's instances
  - `put` *function(number, number, number, number, number, THREE.Color, number=): void* - put(x, z, rotationY, scaleX, scaleZ, color, bright = 1): add one instance on the batch's plane; `color` is a linear THREE.Color (make.color) and is not kept; bright > 1 blooms. Calls past the capacity are dropped.
  - `end` *function(): void* - finish the frame: show exactly what was put since begin()

## theme.js

> Kit theme: the customisation layer that lets each game's HUD look native to that game.
>
> Owns: design tokens (CSS custom properties), skins (named starting points), font loading, per-game scoped CSS,
>       and applying / clearing all of that when a game starts or exits.
> Does not own: the HUD components themselves (kit/ui.js builds them and consults `theme.components` for overrides)
>       or the base stylesheet (index.html, which reads every token listed in TOKEN_DEFAULTS).
>
> A game opts in from its registration:
>
>   Kit.register({
>     id: 'cozy',
>     theme: {
>       skin: 'paper',                                   // start from a skin (see SKINS)...
>       fonts: ['Pixelify Sans:wght@500;700'],           // ...load Google Fonts families...
>       tokens: { '--display': '"Pixelify Sans", monospace', '--accent': '#e2725b' },   // ...override tokens...
>       layout: { stats: 'top-center', actions: 'bottom' },                            // ...place the HUD regions...
>       css: '& .stat { transform: rotate(-1.5deg); }',  // ...add CSS scoped to this game ("&" is the game's root)...
>       components: { stat: ({ label, value, h }) => customElement },                  // ...or rebuild a component.
>     },
>     create(ctx) { ... },
>   });
>
> Everything is optional: a game with no `theme` gets the default "glass" skin.

### `Kit.theme.SKINS`

Skins: complete looks a game can start from. Each is just a Theme, so a game's own theme merges over it.
Add a skin by adding a row; nothing else needs to change.

### `Kit.theme.TOKEN_DEFAULTS`

Every token the base stylesheet reads, with the value used when neither the skin nor the game sets it.
Colours are any CSS colour; sizes are CSS lengths.

### `Kit.theme.resolve(gameTheme = {})`

Merge a game's theme over its skin. Tokens and components merge key by key; fonts concatenate; css concatenates.

- `[gameTheme]` *Theme*

**Returns** *Required\<Theme\>* A complete theme with every field present.

### `Kit.theme.apply(gameId, gameTheme)`

Apply a game's theme to the page: tokens on <body>, skin / game / layout as data attributes for CSS to key on,
fonts requested, scoped CSS installed. Returns the resolved theme so kit/ui.js can read `components`.

- `gameId` *string* - The game's registered id; scoped CSS only matches while this game is active.
- `[gameTheme]` *Theme*

**Returns** *Required\<Theme\>*

### `Kit.theme.clear()`

Remove every trace of the active theme (called when a game exits to the menu or restarts).

### `Kit.theme.loadFonts(families)`

Ask the browser to load Google Fonts families that have not been requested yet.
Google Fonts is the only font host the published page's security policy allows.

- `families` *string[]* - css2-form family specs, e.g. 'Fredoka:wght@500;700'.

### Types

- **Theme**
  - `[skin]` *string* - Name of a skin in SKINS to start from. Default 'glass'.
  - `[fonts]` *string[]* - Google Fonts families in css2 form, e.g. 'Pixelify Sans:wght@500;700'.
  - `[tokens]` *Record\<string, string\>* - Token overrides; keys are the names in TOKEN_DEFAULTS (or your own).
  - `[layout]` *{ stats?: 'top-right'\|'top-center'\|'top-left', actions?: 'bottom'\|'bottom-right' }*
  - `[css]` *string* - Extra CSS. "&" is replaced by a selector matching only this game's HUD.
  - `[components]` *Record\<string, Function\>* - Component builders that replace the kit's (see kit/ui.js).

## ui.js

> Kit ui: the HUD, built from a few primitives.
>
> Owns:  HUD widgets (stat, pips, progress, actions, toast, hint, el), modals that pause the game
>        (choose, banner, panel) and the adapters that draw a Kit model (shop and offer for
>        Kit.upgrades, checklist for Kit.goals). Everything it adds is removed by `dispose()`,
>        which the shell calls when the game exits or restarts.
>        It is also the compatibility layer over the component library in kit/ui/: every call above keeps its
>        name, its return value and its markup (game themes style `.stat`, `.action`, `#modal .modal-card`,
>        `.sheet-body` ...), while the modal host is now a native <dialog>, the sheet body is the kit's scroll
>        pane, and `mount` / `dialog` / `html` open the library to a game. New views should be components.
> Does not own: how the HUD looks (the base stylesheet in index.html and the game's theme, see
>        theme.js; a theme may also rebuild a component through `theme.components`), the models
>        themselves (model.js) or canvas input (input.js).
>
> Needs the DOM, so it is exercised by tools/smoke.js rather than unit tests.

### `Kit.ui(ctx)`

Creates the HUD for a game. The shell does this and exposes it as `ctx.ui`.

- `ctx` *Object* - the shell context (uses paused, tween, theme)

**Returns** *Object* `{ stat, pips, progress, actions, choose, banner, panel, toast, hint, el, shop, offer, checklist, h, dispose }`

```js
const score = ui.stat('Score', 0); score.set(1250); score.pulse();
const hint = ui.hint('Drag to move'); input.onDrag(() => hint.hide());
ui.banner({ title: 'Run over', stats: [['Score', 1250]] }).then(() => ctx.restart());
```

#### `Kit.ui().stat(label, value = 0)`

A labelled value in the top row.

- `label` *string* - keep it short: the row must fit a 390px phone
- `[value=0]` *number\|string* - numbers are formatted with Kit.fmt

**Returns** *{el: HTMLElement, set: function((number\|string)): void, pulse: function(): void}* `pulse` plays a brief pop

#### `Kit.ui().progress(label = '', color)`

A slim bar under the stats row.
A theme may rebuild the bar (theme.components.progress({ fill, label })) as long as it places both live elements;
`fill` is sized as a percentage of its parent, so wrap it in a track element to inset the bar.

- `[label='']` *string*
- `[color]` *string* - CSS colour of the fill

**Returns** *{el: HTMLElement, set: function(number): void, label: function(string): void, color: function(string): void}* `set(fraction)` takes 0..1 (clamped); `color(css)` repaints the fill at any time (a boss bar that takes each boss's colour, an HP bar that reddens)

```js
const xp = ui.progress('XP', '#7cf'); xp.set(0.4); xp.label('Level 2');
```

#### `Kit.ui().actions(specs)`

The bottom button bar (shops, abilities).

- `specs` *ActionSpec[]*

**Returns** *{el: function(string): ?HTMLButtonElement, set: function(string, {label?: string, sub?: string, disabled?: boolean, hidden?: boolean}): void}* `set` patches one button (unknown ids are ignored); `el(id)` is the button itself (or null), for a themed button that carries extra parts the game keeps up to date

```js
const bar = ui.actions([{ id: 'dash', label: 'Dash', sub: 'ready', onClick: () => player.dash() }]);
bar.set('dash', { sub: '2s', disabled: true });
```

#### `Kit.ui().choose({ title = 'Choose', sub, options })`

"Pick one" modal (level-up choices, route forks).

- `spec` *{title?: string, sub?: string, options: ChoiceOption[]}* - title defaults to 'Choose'

**Returns** *Promise\<number\>* index of the picked option

```js
const index = await ui.choose({ title: 'Level up', options: [{ title: 'Haste', desc: '+10% speed', tag: 'New' }] });
```

#### `Kit.ui().banner({ title, text, stats: rows, button: buttonLabel = 'Play again' })`

End-of-run / level-clear modal with one button.

- `spec` *{title: string, text?: string, stats?: Array\<[string, (number\|string)]\>, button?: string}* - `stats` are [label, value] lines; `button` is the button's label (default 'Play again')

**Returns** *Promise\<void\>* resolves when the button is pressed

```js
ui.banner({ title: 'Run over', text: 'The mine caved in', stats: [['Depth', 42]] }).then(() => ctx.restart());
```

#### `Kit.ui().toast(text, ms = DEFAULT_TOAST_MS, { gameTime = false } = {})`

A brief centred callout ("Wave 3").

- `text` *string*
- `[ms=1300]` *number* - how long it stays, milliseconds
- `[options]` *{gameTime?: boolean}* - `gameTime: true` plays it on the game clock (Kit.scrub), so it freezes while the game is paused or in hitstop and stretches in slow motion; the default is wall-clock time

#### `Kit.ui().hint(text)`

A persistent instruction near the bottom; hide it after the player's first input.

- `text` *string*

**Returns** *{el: HTMLElement, hide: function(): void, show: function(): void, set: function(string): void}*

#### `Kit.ui().el(tag = 'div', className, text)`

Escape hatch: adds your own element to the HUD layer (pointer events are off unless you turn them on).

- `[tag='div']` *string*
- `[className]` *string*
- `[text]` *string*

**Returns** *HTMLElement*

#### `Kit.ui().pips(label, { total = 0, filled = total } = {})`

A stat drawn as a row of tokens instead of a number: hearts, ammo, shots in a rack, flags left to capture.
Markup: div.stat.pips[data-pips="<label slug>"] > span (label) + div.pip-row > i.pip (with .on on the filled ones).
The default look is small dots. Restyle `.pip` / `.pip.on` from the game's theme css (key on data-pips to style each
row differently), or rebuild the box with theme.components.pips({ label, row, h }): keep `row`, set() fills it.

- `label` *string*
- `[initial]` *{ total?: number, filled?: number }*

**Returns** *{ el: HTMLElement, set(filled: number, total?: number): void, pulse(): void }*

```js
const lives = ui.pips('Lives', { total: 3 }); lives.set(2); lives.pulse();
```

#### `Kit.ui().panel({ title, build, closeLabel = 'Close' })`

A full-height scrollable sheet for inventories, skill trees and shops.

- `spec` *{title: string, build: (body: HTMLElement, close: (value?: any) =\> void) =\> void, closeLabel?: string}* - `build(body, close)` fills `body` with your own elements; call `close(value)` to dismiss

**Returns** *Promise\<\*\>* the value passed to close (undefined from the Close button)

```js
ui.panel({ title: 'Backpack', build: (body, close) => inventory.bag.forEach((item) => body.append(ui.h('div', 'item', item.name))) });
```

#### `Kit.ui().shop(upgrades, { wallet, ids, onBuy, describe = false, sound = 'up' } = {})`

A bottom bar bound to a Kit.upgrades model: buying, labels, costs and disabling are automatic.

- `upgrades` *ReturnType\<typeof Kit.upgrades\>*
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.wallet]` *ReturnType\<typeof Kit.wallet\>* - redraw when the balance changes
- `[options.ids]` *string[]* - show only these rows, in this order (default: the whole table)
- `[options.onBuy]` *function(Object, number): void* - called with (row, new level) after a purchase
- `[options.describe=false]` *boolean* - show each row's desc(next level) before the price
- `[options.sound='up']` *?string* - Kit.sfx name played on a purchase, or null when the game plays its own in onBuy

**Returns** *{refresh: function(): void, bar: ReturnType\<typeof actions\>}*

```js
ui.shop(upgrades, { wallet, describe: true });
```

#### `Kit.ui().offer(upgrades, { n = 3, title = 'Level up', sub, filter, rows: givenRows } = {})`

"Pick 1 of n" from a Kit.upgrades model; the pick is granted.

- `upgrades` *ReturnType\<typeof Kit.upgrades\>*
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.n=3]` *number* - how many to offer
- `[options.title='Level up']` *string*
- `[options.sub]` *string*
- `[options.filter]` *function(Object): boolean* - passed to upgrades.offer
- `[options.rows]` *Object[]* - a hand you rolled yourself (a guaranteed slot, a reroll, a saved offer) instead of upgrades.offer(n, filter); rows that are maxed by then are dropped

**Returns** *Promise\<?Object\>* the picked UpgradeRow, or null when nothing could be offered

```js
const picked = await ui.offer(upgrades, { n: 3, title: 'Level 4' });
```

#### `Kit.ui().checklist(goals, { hideWhenDone = false } = {})`

A compact goal list bound to a Kit.goals model.

- `goals` *ReturnType\<typeof Kit.goals\>*
- `[options]` *{hideWhenDone?: boolean}* - `hideWhenDone: true` takes the list out of view once every goal is done (and brings it back if progress is ever reset)

**Returns** *{el: HTMLElement, refresh: function(): void}*

#### `Kit.ui().h(tag, className, text)`

Makes an element.

- `tag` *string*
- `[className]` *?string*
- `[text]` *string\|number* - text content (never parsed as HTML)

**Returns** *HTMLElement*

```js
ui.h('div', 'card-title', 'Backpack')
```

#### `Kit.ui().mount(view, slot = 'layer')`

Mounts a component view (kit/ui/) into the HUD; it is removed with the rest of the HUD when the game ends.

- `view` *\** - a template built with `ui.html` from Kit.ui components
- `[slot='layer']` *'layer'\|'stats'\|'actions'* - the HUD region: the middle layer, the top stats row or the bottom bar

**Returns** *{el: HTMLElement, dispose: function(): void}*

```js
const { html, signal, Meter } = Kit.ui; const heat = signal(0);
ctx.ui.mount(html`<${Meter} label="Heat" value=${heat} />`);   // then just: heat.value = 0.7
```

#### `Kit.ui().dialog(spec)`

A component dialog or sheet (Kit.ui.openDialog) that pauses the game while it is open.

- `spec` *Object* - see Kit.ui.openDialog: `{ title, body, actions, variant: 'dialog' | 'sheet', dismissable }`

**Returns** *Promise\<\*\>* the value passed to `close(value)`; undefined when dismissed

```js
const pick = await ctx.ui.dialog({ title: 'Bait', variant: 'sheet', body: (close) => html`<${List} label="Bait" items=${baits} onSelect=${close} />` });
```

#### `Kit.ui().dispose()`

Removes everything this HUD added and closes any open modal, dialog or notice.

### `Kit.ui.h`

The element builder is also available without a HUD instance (the shell builds the menu with it).

### Types

- **ActionSpec**
  - `id` *string*
  - `label` *string* - main line of the button
  - `[sub]` *string* - small second line (a price, a cooldown)
  - `[onClick]` *function(string): void* - called with the id
- **ChoiceOption**
  - `title` *string*
  - `[desc]` *string*
  - `[tag]` *string* - small badge ("New", "Lv 3")
  - `[color]` *string* - CSS colour of the option's accent

## ui/logic.js

> Kit ui logic: the decisions the components make, as pure functions.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.uiLogic`: the notice queue (what is showing, what waits), roving focus (which item an arrow key
>        lands on), selection (single and multiple), text search over rows, fraction clamping and where a popover goes.
> Does not own: any element or event (the component files beside this one apply these decisions to the DOM).
>
> Pure: runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js. It loads before kit/ui.js and needs nothing.

### `Kit.uiLogic.createQueue(limit = 1)`

An empty notice queue.

- `[limit=1]` *number* - how many notices may be on screen together

**Returns** *NoticeQueue*

### `Kit.uiLogic.enqueue(queue, notice)`

Adds a notice: it shows at once if a slot is free, otherwise it waits its turn.

- `queue` *NoticeQueue*
- `notice` *Object* - anything with a unique `id`

**Returns** *NoticeQueue* a new queue; the input is not changed

**Complexity** O(showing + waiting) time and space (the lists are copied)

### `Kit.uiLogic.dismiss(queue, id)`

Removes a notice by id (shown or still waiting) and promotes the longest-waiting notice into any freed slot.

- `queue` *NoticeQueue*
- `id` *string\|number*

**Returns** *NoticeQueue* a new queue

**Complexity** O(showing + waiting) time and space

### `Kit.uiLogic.rovingIndex(key, index, count, isDisabled = () => false)`

Where focus goes when a key is pressed in a tab list, list or grid: arrows step and wrap around, Home and
End jump, and disabled items are skipped.

- `key` *string* - a KeyboardEvent.key
- `index` *number* - the focused item
- `count` *number* - how many items there are
- `[isDisabled]` *function(number): boolean* - items focus must skip

**Returns** *number* the new index, or -1 when the key is not a focus key (or every other item is disabled)

**Complexity** O(count) time in the worst case (a run of disabled items), O(1) space

```js
rovingIndex('ArrowRight', 2, 3) // 0: wraps to the first tab
```

### `Kit.uiLogic.toggleSelection(selected, id, multiple = false)`

The selection after an item is picked. Single selection replaces; multiple selection toggles.

- `selected` *\*\|Array* - the current selection: one id (or null), or an array of ids when `multiple`
- `id` *\** - the picked item
- `[multiple=false]` *boolean*

**Returns** *\*\|Array* the new selection, in the same shape

```js
toggleSelection(['a'], 'b', true) // ['a', 'b'];  toggleSelection('a', 'b') // 'b'
```

### `Kit.uiLogic.isSelected(selected, id)`

Is this id part of the selection (either shape)?

### `Kit.uiLogic.searchRows(rows, query, fields)`

Rows whose text contains every word of a query, ignoring case. An empty query keeps every row.

- `rows` *Object[]*
- `query` *string* - what the person typed
- `fields` *string[]* - the row properties to search

**Returns** *Object[]* matching rows, in their original order

**Complexity** O(rows x words x text length) time, O(rows) space

```js
searchRows(games, 'neon run', ['name', 'tagline'])
```

### `Kit.uiLogic.clampFraction(value)`

A meter's value as a fraction 0..1; anything that is not a finite number counts as empty.

### `Kit.uiLogic.placePanel(anchor, panel, viewport)`

Where a panel goes: under its anchor (above when there is no room below), kept on screen sideways.
Pure geometry: boxes in, a position out.

- `anchor` *{left: number, top: number, bottom: number, width: number}* - the button's box
- `panel` *{width: number, height: number}* - the panel's size
- `viewport` *{width: number, height: number}*

**Returns** *{left: number, top: number}* viewport coordinates of the panel's top-left corner

### Types

- **NoticeQueue**
  - `showing` *Object[]* - notices on screen, oldest first (at most `limit`)
  - `waiting` *Object[]* - notices held back until a slot frees, in arrival order
  - `limit` *number* - how many may show at once

## ui/core.js

> Kit ui core: the foundation every component in kit/ui/ stands on.
>
> Owns:  the template tag (`Kit.ui.html`, htm bound to Preact), reactive state (`signal`, `computed`, `effect`,
>        `batch`, straight from @preact/signals; `useSignal`, `useComputed`, `useRef`, `useEffect` for component
>        authors), `bind` (a Kit model in, a signal out), `mount` (a view into the
>        page, with a dispose handle), the one stylesheet the components share (`css`), and small helpers
>        (`read`, `classes`, `uid`).
> Does not own: any component (one file per concern beside this one), the theme tokens (kit/theme.js) or the
>        legacy HUD calls (kit/ui.js, which now leans on these pieces).
>
> Why Preact + htm + signals: see "UI components" in DECISIONS.md. In short: templates with no build step, the
> same component functions work under JSX later, and a signal read in a template updates only the text node or
> attribute that reads it, so a HUD bound to game state costs O(values that changed) per frame, not a re-render.
>
> A component is a plain function from props to a template. Any prop may be a plain value or a signal:
>
>   const { html, signal, mount, Meter } = Kit.ui;
>   const heat = signal(0.2);
>   mount(html`<${Meter} label="Heat" value=${heat} />`, ctx.ui.el('div'));
>   heat.value = 0.8;        // the bar moves; nothing else is touched
>
> Needs the DOM; exercised by tools/test-ui.js (in a browser) and the component gallery (Kit.play('gallery')).

## ui/scroll.js

> Kit ui scroll: the scrollable pane.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.ScrollPane` (the component) and `Kit.ui.scrollable` (the same behaviour for an element you
>        already have): native scrolling with momentum, overscroll kept inside the pane, optional scroll snap,
>        fade edges that show there is more, keyboard scrolling, and safe-area padding at the screen's edge.
> Does not own: what is inside the pane, or its height (give it one: `fill` inside a flex column, or a max-height).
>
> Why native: the browser's scroller is the only one that gets momentum, rubber-banding, accessibility and
> 120 Hz for free. Nothing here listens to touch events; the one listener is a passive `scroll` that keeps the
> fade edges honest. tools/touch-scroll.js proves a pane moves under a real finger drag.

### Types

- **ScrollOptions**
  - `[axis='y']` *'y'\|'x'* - the direction that scrolls
  - `[snap=false]` *false\|'start'\|'center'* - snap children to the pane's start or centre when a scroll ends
  - `[fade=true]` *boolean* - fade the edge(s) beyond which more content lies
  - `[safe=false]` *boolean* - pad the far end by the phone's safe-area inset (for a pane that reaches the screen edge)
  - `[label]` *string* - accessible name; a pane that has one is announced as a region

## ui/button.js

> Kit ui button: the one pressable control.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.Button`: a native <button> with variants (default, primary, quiet, danger) and the states a
>        button can be in: pressed (a toggle), disabled, busy.
> Does not own: what a press does. Menus of buttons are a `Cluster` (layout.js); a row of exclusive choices is `Tabs`.

## ui/layout.js

> Kit ui layout: arranging things, and saying there is nothing to arrange.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.Stack` (a column that can become a row when its own container is wide enough; a container
>        query, so it responds to the space it is given, not to the screen), `Kit.ui.Cluster` (a row that wraps),
>        and `Kit.ui.Empty` (the empty state of a list, search or inventory).
> Does not own: scrolling (scroll.js) or surfaces (collection.js).

## ui/meter.js

> Kit ui meter: numbers a player reads at a glance.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.Meter` (a level: health, heat, fuel), `Kit.ui.Progress` (work toward an end, or "working" when
>        the end is unknown), `Kit.ui.Stat` (a labelled value) and `Kit.ui.Pips` (a count drawn as tokens).
> Does not own: the legacy HUD's `ui.stat` / `ui.progress` / `ui.pips` (kit/ui.js keeps those calls and their
>        markup, which game themes style); these are the same ideas as components for new views.
>
> Each takes a plain value or a signal. With a signal only the text node or the one style property that shows
> the value is written when it changes: O(1) per changed value, no re-render.

## ui/tabs.js

> Kit ui tabs: one of several views at a time.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.Tabs`: a tab list (arrow keys, Home and End move between tabs; only the selected tab is in the
>        Tab order) and the panel of the selected tab.
> Does not own: what the panels hold. A panel that should scroll wraps its content in a ScrollPane.

### Types

- **TabSpec**
  - `id` *string*
  - `label` *string*
  - `content` *\*\|function(): \** - the panel: a template, or a function returning one (built only while selected)
  - `[disabled]` *boolean*

## ui/collection.js

> Kit ui collection: things in a row, a column or a grid, and the surface each one sits on.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.Card` (a surface with a title, text, a mark and a tag; a real <button> when it can be pressed),
>        `Kit.ui.List` and `Kit.ui.Grid` (items with single or multiple selection, a listbox to assistive
>        technology: arrows move, Home and End jump, Enter or Space selects).
> Does not own: scrolling (wrap a long list in a ScrollPane) or where the items come from.

### Types

- **CollectionProps**
  - `items` *Object[]* - rows; each needs a unique `id` (or pass `idOf`)
  - `[render]` *function(Object, boolean): \** - draws one item given (item, isSelected); default: its `label` or `name`
  - `[selected]` *\*\|Array\|Object* - the selection: an id (single) or array of ids (multiple), plain or a signal you own
  - `[multiple=false]` *boolean* - toggle items in and out of the selection instead of replacing it
  - `[onSelect]` *function(\*\|Array, Object): void* - called with (new selection, the item that was picked)
  - `[idOf]` *function(Object): \** - how to read an item's id (default: `item.id`)
  - `[isDisabled]` *function(Object): boolean* - items that cannot be picked
  - `label` *string* - accessible name of the collection

## ui/field.js

> Kit ui field: the controls a person sets a value with.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.TextField` (a labelled text or search input), `Kit.ui.Toggle` (an on / off switch) and
>        `Kit.ui.Slider` (a number in a range). All three are native inputs, so the keyboard, the on-screen
>        keyboard, autofill and assistive technology already know them.
> Does not own: validation rules (pass `error` to show the message your rule produced).
>
> `value` / `checked` may be a signal you own: the control writes to it as the person changes it, and shows it
> when you change it. Key presses inside a control never reach the game underneath.

## ui/dialog.js

> Kit ui dialog: something that takes over until it is dealt with.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.Dialog` (a centred card) and `Kit.ui.Sheet` (the same thing rising from the bottom edge, for
>        long content on a phone), both on the native <dialog>: the browser traps focus inside, makes the rest
>        of the page inert, closes on Escape and gives focus back to whatever had it. `Kit.ui.openDialog` shows
>        one without writing a component and returns a promise of its result.
> Does not own: pausing the game (ctx.ui.dialog does that for a running game) or the legacy modal (`ui.choose`,
>        `ui.banner`, `ui.panel` in kit/ui.js, which share the native element but keep their own markup).
>
> The body is a ScrollPane, so content taller than the screen scrolls inside the dialog and never behind it.

## ui/notice.js

> Kit ui notice: telling the player something without stopping them.
>
> Owns:  the toast queue (`Kit.ui.notify`: short messages that stack, a few at a time, and leave by themselves)
>        and the banner queue (`Kit.ui.announce`: one message at a time across the top that stays until it is
>        dismissed or acted on, the next one following it). Both are live regions, so screen readers speak them.
> Does not own: the legacy centred callout `ui.toast` (kit/ui.js, an animation themed per game) or modal
>        results (`ui.banner`). The queue's rules are pure (kit/ui/logic.js) and unit-tested.

## ui/popover.js

> Kit ui popover: a little more, on request, next to the thing it is about.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.ui.Popover` (a button that opens a floating panel: tap or Enter opens it, a tap elsewhere or
>        Escape closes it; the native popover API, so it sits above everything, dialogs included) and
>        `Kit.ui.Tooltip` (a short description shown on hover, on keyboard focus and on a touch press).
> Does not own: menus of commands (use a Sheet with a List on a phone) or modal questions (dialog.js).

## views.js

> Kit views: build-crafting screens generated from kit models. Model in, element out; each view re-renders itself when
> its model changes. Put the element anywhere (usually inside ui.panel) and call dispose() when it is closed.
>
>   tree      - skill tree from a Kit.upgrades model whose rows carry `row` / `col` / `requires`   Kit.views.tree(upgrades, { wallet })
>   inventory - bag and equipment from Kit.inventory + Kit.loot, with compare, equip and salvage    Kit.views.inventory(inventory, { loot })
>   roll      - slot-style reel over a weighted table that stops on a winner chosen beforehand      Kit.views.roll({ entries, winner })
>   progressList - any list of titled entries with a counter and a progress bar                     Kit.views.progressList(readEntries, models)
>   deeds     - achievements of a Kit.profile with live progress                                    Kit.views.deeds(profile)
>   synergies - set bonuses of a Kit.synergy with the tags still missing                            Kit.views.synergies(synergy)
>
> Owns: the markup, the default look and the taps of those screens.
> Does not own: the decisions they show (kit/craft.js, unit-tested), the models (kit/model.js, kit/loot.js), or the
>               modal they sit in (ui.panel).
>
> Theming: every colour, font and radius comes from the theme tokens (kit/theme.js) or from the view tokens in
> VIEW_TOKEN_DEFAULTS below, so a game restyles a view from `theme.tokens` and `theme.css` alone. The class names are a
> stable contract for scoped CSS:
>   .kv-tree  .kv-tree-heads  .kv-tree-lines line[data-active]  .kv-node[data-status][data-owned]  .kv-node-mark
>   .kv-inventory  .kv-slot  .kv-item (its rarity colour is the CSS variable --c)  .kv-compare .is-better / .is-worse
>   .kv-roll  .kv-reel  .kv-reel-row (colour --c)  .kv-roll[data-landed]  .kv-roll-result
>   .kv-list  .kv-entry[data-done]  .kv-entry-bar (its fill is the CSS variable --progress, 0..1)

### `Kit.views.VIEW_TOKEN_DEFAULTS`

Tokens only the views read. A game overrides them like any theme token (`theme.tokens`).

### `Kit.views.tree(upgrades, options = {})`

Skill tree generated from a Kit.upgrades model. Rows are placed by their `row` (tier, 0 at the top) and `col`
fields, lines join each node to its `requires`, and a tap buys. Every node shows its name, what the next level
does and its price; its `data-status` (see Kit.craft.nodeStatus) drives the locked / available / owned looks.

- `upgrades` *Object* - a Kit.upgrades model (rows need integer `row` and `col`)
- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.wallet]` *Object* - the Kit.wallet the tree spends from; shows the balance and re-renders on change
- `[options.currency]` *string* - which wallet currency to show (default: the wallet's default)
- `[options.pointsLabel]` *string* - text before the balance (default 'Points')
- `[options.heads]` *string[]* - a title per column (branch names)
- `[options.anchor]` *{ x: number, y: number }* - where lines attach in a cell, 0..1 (default the centre); move it when your CSS draws a node's mark (.kv-node-mark) away from the centre
- `[options.onBuy]` *(row: Object, level: number) =\> void* - called after a successful purchase

**Returns** *{ element: HTMLElement, refresh: () =\> void, dispose: () =\> void }*

**Complexity** render: O(rows + edges) elements, rebuilt on every model change (a tree is a few dozen nodes).

```js
const view = Kit.views.tree(tree, { wallet, currency: 'souls', heads: ['Reaper', 'Warden', 'Occult'] });
ui.panel({ title: 'Soul Tree', build: (body) => body.append(view.element) }).then(view.dispose);
```

### `Kit.views.inventory(inventoryModel, options)`

Inventory generated from a Kit.inventory and the Kit.loot that rolled its items: the worn item per slot, then every
bag item as a card in its rarity colour with its describe lines, a compare against what is worn, and Equip /
Salvage buttons.

- `inventory` *Object* - a Kit.inventory
- `options` *Object*
- `options.loot` *Object* - the Kit.loot generator (for describe)
- `[options.labels]` *Record\<string, string\>* - stat name -> display name, for describe and compare lines
- `[options.compareStats]` *string[]* - stats to compare (default: none, which hides the compare line)
- `[options.percent]` *string[]* - see Kit.craft.compareLines
- `[options.lowerIsBetter]` *string[]* - see Kit.craft.compareLines
- `[options.slotLabels]` *Record\<string, string\>* - slot id -> display name
- `[options.tag]` *(item: Object) =\> string* - small text beside an item's name (rarity, level, "Cursed")
- `[options.decorate]` *(card: HTMLElement, item: Object) =\> void* - last word on each bag card: add data attributes or badges your scoped CSS keys on (for example card.dataset.cursed)
- `[options.onEquip]` *(item: Object) =\> void* - called after an item is equipped
- `[options.salvage]` *{ label: (item: Object) =\> string, onSalvage: (item: Object) =\> void }* - adds a Salvage button; the view removes the item from the bag, then calls onSalvage(item) for the payout

**Returns** *{ element: HTMLElement, refresh: () =\> void, dispose: () =\> void }*

**Complexity** render: O(items x compareStats), rebuilt on every inventory change (a bag is a few dozen items).

```js
const view = Kit.views.inventory(inventory, { loot, labels: STAT_LABELS, compareStats: ['damage', 'maxHp'],
  salvage: { label: (item) => `Salvage +${soulsFor(item)}`, onSalvage: (item) => wallet.add(soulsFor(item), 'souls') } });
```

### `Kit.views.roll(options)`

Slot-style roll: a reel of weighted entries spins and stops on `winner`, then a result line and a "take" button
appear. The outcome is decided by the caller before the view is made; the view only reveals it. Declining is the
host's job (for example the close button of ui.panel), so the caller can word and price it.

- `options` *Object*
- `options.entries` *RollEntry[]* - the weighted table the reel shows
- `options.winner` *RollEntry* - the entry to stop on (one of `entries`)
- `[options.describe]` *(entry: RollEntry) =\> string* - result text once landed
- `[options.takeLabel]` *(entry: RollEntry) =\> string* - text of the take button (default 'Take')
- `[options.onLand]` *(entry: RollEntry) =\> void* - called when the reel stops
- `options.onTake` *(entry: RollEntry) =\> void* - called when the take button is pressed
- `[options.spinMs]` *number* - spin duration in milliseconds
- `[options.rng]` *Object* - a Kit.rng for the filler rows

**Returns** *{ element: HTMLElement, dispose: () =\> void }* dispose cancels a spin still in progress

**Complexity** O(strip length) elements once (about 27 rows); the spin itself is one CSS transition.

```js
ui.panel({ title: 'Absorb', closeLabel: 'Bank it: +3 souls', build: (body, close) =>
  body.append(Kit.views.roll({ entries: pool, winner, onTake: () => close('take') }).element) });
```

#### `Kit.views.roll().dispose()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.views.progressList(readEntries, models)`

A list of progress entries (a title, a counter, a detail line and a thin bar each) that re-renders when a model
changes. `deeds` and `synergies` are this with a mapping; use it directly for any other list (a bestiary, quests).

- `readEntries` *() =\> { title: string, counter: string, detail?: string, progress: number, isDone: boolean }[]* - the entries to show; progress is 0..1
- `models` *Object[]* - things with on(listener) to re-render on

**Returns** *{ element: HTMLElement, refresh: () =\> void, dispose: () =\> void }*

**Complexity** render: O(entries), rebuilt on every model change.

### `Kit.views.deeds(profile, options = {})`

Achievements of a Kit.profile: each with its text, progress toward its target and what it unlocks.

- `profile` *Object* - a Kit.profile
- `[options]` *{ describeUnlock?: (unlockId: string) =\> string }* - describeUnlock: words for a row's `unlocks` id (default: the id itself)

**Returns** *{ element: HTMLElement, refresh: () =\> void, dispose: () =\> void }*

**Complexity** render: O(achievements).

```js
ui.panel({ title: 'Deeds', build: (body) => body.append(Kit.views.deeds(profile).element) });
```

### `Kit.views.synergies(synergy, options = {})`

Set bonuses of a Kit.synergy: each with its description and, tag by tag, how much of what it needs the build
holds. Call refresh() after synergy.update(...) if the view stays open while the build changes.

- `synergy` *Object* - a Kit.synergy
- `[options]` *{ tagLabels?: Record\<string, string\> }* - tagLabels: tag id -> display name

**Returns** *{ element: HTMLElement, refresh: () =\> void, dispose: () =\> void }*

**Complexity** render: O(rows x needs).

```js
body.append(Kit.views.synergies(synergies, { tagLabels: { blight: 'Blight' } }).element);
```

### Types

- **RollEntry**
  - `label` *string* - what the row shows
  - `[sub]` *string* - small text under the label (the source, the odds)
  - `[color]` *string* - CSS colour of the label
  - `[weight]` *number* - how often it appears among the filler rows (default 1)

## post.js

> Kit post: the post-processing chain. HDR render -> bloom -> one final pass that applies
> exposure, vignette, ACES tone mapping and sRGB output.
>
> Owns:  building that chain, its per-scene tuning (`ctx.post.bloom`, `ctx.post.vignette`,
>        `ctx.post.exposure`) and switching it off and on.
> Does not own: when to render or resize (shell.js calls `render` and `setSize`), deciding that
>        the device is too slow for it (the shell's quality governor calls `disable`), or what
>        glows (anything emissive or built with `bright` above 1 in make.js / fx.js).
>
> Where HDR render targets or the three.js post-processing addons are missing, every method is a
> no-op and `render` draws the scene directly, so callers never need to check.
> Needs WebGL, so it is exercised by tools/smoke.js rather than unit tests.

### `Kit.post(renderer)`

Creates the post-processing chain for a renderer. The shell does this once and exposes it as `ctx.post`.

- `renderer` *THREE.WebGLRenderer*

**Returns** *Post*

```js
ctx.post.bloom(0.6, 0.4, 0.9); // a neon game opts in to visible glow
ctx.post.vignette(0.3);
```

#### `Kit.post().bloom(strength = DEFAULTS.strength, radius = DEFAULTS.radius, threshold = DEFAULTS.threshold)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.post().vignette(amount = DEFAULTS.vignette)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.post().exposure(amount = DEFAULTS.exposure)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.post().reset()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.post().setSize(width, height, pixelRatio)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.post().render(scene, camera)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.post().disable()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.post().enable()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### Types

- **Post**
  - `active` *boolean* - is the chain in use? (false on the fallback and after disable())
  - `bloom` *function(number=, number=, number=): void* - bloom(strength, radius, threshold): emissive and tone-map-exempt colours brighter than `threshold` glow; strength 0 turns bloom off for the scene
  - `vignette` *function(number=): number* - edge darkening 0..1 (default 0.18)
  - `exposure` *function(number=): number* - brightness multiplier (default 1)
  - `reset` *function(): void* - back to the defaults; the shell calls this between games
  - `setSize` *function(number, number, number): void* - (width, height, pixelRatio) in CSS pixels
  - `render` *function(THREE.Scene, THREE.Camera): void* - draw one frame
  - `disable` *function(): void* - render directly from now on (the quality governor's first step)
  - `enable` *function(): void* - use the chain again

## ballistics.js

> Kit ballistics: the maths of thrown things. Pure: plain { x, y, z } objects in, plain data out; no DOM, THREE or cannon
> (unit-tested in tools/test-kit.js).
>
>   direction   - unit aim vector from a yaw and a loft angle:    Kit.ballistics.direction(0.2, 0.5)
>   trace       - the flight path a physics body will follow:     Kit.ballistics.trace({ origin, velocity, acceleration, stopWhen })
>   sphereTouchesBox - cheap obstacle test for a trace:           Kit.ballistics.sphereTouchesBox(point, radius, box)
>   blastKick   - velocity change an explosion gives a point:     Kit.ballistics.blastKick(centre, point, { radius, kick, lift })
>
> Owns: aim geometry, trajectory prediction and explosion falloff. Does not own: bodies, collisions or rendering
> (Kit.physics applies blastKick to cannon bodies; the game decides how to draw a trace).

### `Kit.ballistics.direction(yaw, loft)`

Unit vector for an aim given as two angles. Yaw 0 looks down -z (away from a camera on +z), like the kit's stages.

- `yaw` *number* - Radians around the vertical axis; positive turns toward +x.
- `loft` *number* - Radians above the horizon.

**Returns** *Vector* Unit direction.

### `Kit.ballistics.advance({ position, velocity }, acceleration, step)`

One semi-implicit Euler step (velocity first, then position): the integrator cannon.js and most game physics use, so
a trace built from it lands exactly where an undamped body will.

- `state` *{ position: Vector, velocity: Vector }*
- `acceleration` *Vector* - Units per second squared (gravity plus any wind).
- `step` *number* - Seconds.

**Returns** *{ position: Vector, velocity: Vector }* The next state (a new object).

### `Kit.ballistics.trace({ origin, velocity, acceleration = { x: 0, y: -20, z: 0 }, step = 1 / 60, maxSteps = 120, stopWhen = () => false })`

Predicts a flight path by stepping the launch state until `stopWhen` says the point has hit something.

- `options` *object*
- `options.origin` *Vector* - Launch point.
- `options.velocity` *Vector* - Launch velocity, units per second.
- `[options.acceleration]` *Vector* - Constant acceleration; defaults to gravity of -20 on y (the Kit.physics default).
- `[options.step]` *number* - Seconds per step; match the physics step (1/60) for an exact preview.
- `[options.maxSteps]` *number* - Upper bound on steps, so a shot into the sky still ends.
- `[options.stopWhen]` *(point: Vector, index: number) =\> boolean* - True when the point is inside an obstacle.

**Returns** *{ points: Vector[], end: Vector, isBlocked: boolean }* `points` are the free-flight positions after each step, `end` is the blocked point (or the last free one when nothing was hit), `isBlocked` says which.

**Complexity** O(maxSteps) calls of `stopWhen` and O(maxSteps) points of memory; with an obstacle list the caller's `stopWhen` makes it O(maxSteps x obstacles).

```js
const flight = Kit.ballistics.trace({ origin, velocity, stopWhen: (point) => point.y < ballRadius });
flight.points.filter((_, index) => index % 3 === 0).forEach(placeDot);
```

### `Kit.ballistics.sphereTouchesBox(point, radius, box)`

Whether a sphere overlaps an axis-aligned box, tested as "centre inside the box grown by the radius". That rounds the
box corners outward slightly, which is the right bias for a preview: it never draws a path through a corner.

- `point` *Vector* - Sphere centre.
- `radius` *number* - Sphere radius.
- `box` *Box*

**Returns** *boolean*

**Complexity** O(1): three axis comparisons.

### `Kit.ballistics.blastKick(centre, point, { radius, kick, lift = 0 })`

Velocity change an explosion gives something at `point`: pushed straight away from the centre, strongest at the centre
and fading linearly to nothing at `radius`, plus an optional upward share so debris arcs instead of sliding.

- `centre` *Vector* - Where the blast is.
- `point` *Vector* - Where the affected thing is.
- `blast` *object*
- `blast.radius` *number* - Reach in world units; at or beyond it nothing happens.
- `blast.kick` *number* - Speed change at the centre, units per second.
- `[blast.lift]` *number* - Extra upward speed as a fraction of the falloff-scaled kick (0.3 = 30%).

**Returns** *Vector \| null* The velocity to add, or null when the point is out of reach.

### Types

- **Box** - A point, velocity or acceleration in world units (metres, seconds).

## loadout.js

> Kit loadout: a fixed row of slots filled from a collection under a point budget.
>
> Owns:  which id sits in which slot, what the picks cost in total and whether a swap still fits the budget.
> Does not own: the collection (pass the ids you offer), what an id means, or any view (the game draws the slots).
> Pure: no DOM, no THREE; unit-tested in tools/test-kit.js.
>
> This is the "decision with a trade-off" model: three shots and five points buy one bomb and two plain balls, or two
> splitters and nothing else. The same shape fits ability bars, squad pickers and deck slots.

### `Kit.loadout({ slots, budget, costOf, initial = [] })`

Create a loadout.

- `options` *LoadoutOptions*

**Returns** *{ picks(): (string\|null)[], filled(): string[], spent(): number, left(): number, canPlace(slot: number, id: string\|null): boolean, place(slot: number, id: string\|null): boolean, cycle(slot: number, ids: string[]): string\|null, on(listener: Function): Function }*

**Complexity** every call is O(slots); `cycle` is O(slots x ids). Memory: O(slots).

```js
const loadout = Kit.loadout({ slots: 3, budget: 5, costOf: (id) => BALLS.byId(id).cost, initial: ['heavy', 'heavy', 'heavy'] });
loadout.place(0, 'bomb');             // true: 3 + 1 + 1 = 5 fits
loadout.cycle(1, ['heavy', 'bomb']);  // 'heavy': a second bomb would cost 7, so the slot keeps the pick that fits
loadout.filled();                     // ['bomb', 'heavy', 'heavy'], the order the game uses them
```

#### `Kit.loadout().picks()`

Every slot in order, `null` where empty.

#### `Kit.loadout().filled()`

The non-empty picks in slot order.

#### `Kit.loadout().spent()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.loadout().left()`

Budget still unspent.

#### `Kit.loadout().canPlace(slot, id)`

A swap frees what the slot held before it charges the new pick, so replacing a dear pick with a cheap one always fits.

#### `Kit.loadout().place(slot, id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.loadout().cycle(slot, ids)`

Step a slot to the next id in `ids` that fits the budget, wrapping round: the one-tap way to edit a loadout.

- `slot` *number* - Slot index, from 0.
- `ids` *string[]* - The ids on offer, in the order to step through.

**Returns** *string\|null* the slot's pick afterwards (unchanged when nothing else fits).

#### `Kit.loadout().on(listener)`

Subscribe to changes.

- `listener` *(change: { slot: number, id: string\|null }) =\> void*

**Returns** *() =\> void* unsubscribe.

### Types

- **LoadoutOptions**
  - `slots` *number* - How many slots there are (a positive whole number).
  - `budget` *number* - The most the picks may cost in total.
  - `costOf` *(id: string) =\> number* - Cost of one id (an empty slot is free).
  - `[initial]` *(string\|null)[]* - Starting picks, slot by slot; missing entries are empty.

## physics.js

> Kit physics: a thin wrapper over cannon.js (global CANNON).
>
> Owns:  one physics world with sensible defaults (fixed 60 Hz timestep, sleeping on), rigid
>        bodies paired with the meshes that show them, mesh <- body syncing after each step,
>        impact callbacks, static scenery, bounds, pushes and blasts.
> Does not own: the meshes themselves (make.js), projectile and blast maths as plain data
>        (ballistics.js) or the frame loop: the game calls `phys.step(dt)` from its update.
>
> Needs cannon.js, so it is exercised by tools/smoke.js rather than unit tests.
>
> @example
> const phys = Kit.physics({ gravity: -20 });
> const body = phys.box(mesh, [width, height, depth], { mass: 1 });   // or phys.sphere(mesh, radius, { mass }) / phys.add(mesh, body)
> phys.onHit(body, (speed, other, event) => speed > 4 && sfx.play('thud'));
> // in update(dt): phys.step(dt);

### `Kit.physics({ gravity = -20, friction = 0.5, restitution = 0.15 } = {})`

Creates a physics world.

- `[options]` *Object*
- `[options.gravity=-20]` *number* - acceleration along y, units / s^2 (negative is down)
- `[options.friction=0.5]` *number* - default contact friction
- `[options.restitution=0.15]` *number* - default bounciness, 0..1

**Returns** *Object* `{ world, pairs, add(mesh, body), box(mesh, size, options), sphere(mesh, radius, options), remove(body), onHit(body, listener), step(dt), fixed(size, { at }), floor(height), bounds(body), push(body, velocityChange), blast(centre, options, bodies), wakeWhere(shouldWake, bodies) }`; `world` is the CANNON.World and `pairs` the live list of `{ mesh, body }` being synced

#### `Kit.physics().add(mesh, body)`

Adds a body you built yourself to the world, optionally tied to a mesh.

- `mesh` *?THREE.Object3D* - follows the body after every step; null for a body with nothing to show
- `body` *CANNON.Body*

**Returns** *CANNON.Body*

#### `Kit.physics().box(mesh, [width, height, depth], options = {})`

Adds a box body for a mesh.

- `mesh` *THREE.Object3D*
- `size` *[number, number, number]* - full width, height and depth
- `[options]` *BodyOptions*

**Returns** *CANNON.Body*

#### `Kit.physics().sphere(mesh, radius, options = {})`

Adds a sphere body for a mesh.

- `mesh` *THREE.Object3D*
- `radius` *number*
- `[options]` *BodyOptions*

**Returns** *CANNON.Body*

#### `Kit.physics().remove(body)`

Takes a body out of the world and stops syncing its mesh (the mesh itself is left alone).

- `body` *CANNON.Body*

#### `Kit.physics().onHit(body, listener)`

Calls back whenever a body starts touching another.

- `body` *CANNON.Body*
- `listener` *function(number, CANNON.Body, Object): void* - gets (impact speed along the contact normal in units / s, the other body, the raw cannon event)

#### `Kit.physics().step(dt)`

Advances the simulation and moves every paired mesh. Call once per frame from the game's update.

- `dt` *number* - frame time in seconds

#### `Kit.physics().fixed([width, height, depth], { at: [x, y, z] })`

Immovable box (platforms, walls). It has no mesh to sync: draw it yourself with make.box at the same place.

- `size` *[number, number, number]* - Full width, height and depth.
- `options` *{ at: [number, number, number] }* - Centre position.

**Returns** *CANNON.Body*

#### `Kit.physics().floor(height = 0)`

Infinite horizontal floor facing up.

- `[height]` *number* - World y of the surface.

**Returns** *CANNON.Body*

#### `Kit.physics().bounds(body)`

A body's current world-space bounding box as plain data (the Box shape Kit.ballistics.sphereTouchesBox takes).

- `body` *CANNON.Body*

**Returns** *{ min: { x: number, y: number, z: number }, max: { x: number, y: number, z: number } }*

#### `Kit.physics().push(body, velocityChange)`

Wake a body and add to its velocity (a mass-independent shove; sleeping bodies ignore velocity until woken).

- `body` *CANNON.Body*
- `velocityChange` *{ x: number, y: number, z: number }* - Units per second.

**Returns** *CANNON.Body*

#### `Kit.physics().blast(centre, options, bodies = world.bodies)`

Explosion: pushes every dynamic body within reach away from the centre (falloff from Kit.ballistics.blastKick).

- `centre` *{ x: number, y: number, z: number }*
- `options` *{ radius: number, kick: number, lift?: number, spin?: number }* - `spin` is the largest random tumble (radians per second per axis) given to each pushed body; the rest is passed to blastKick.
- `[bodies]` *CANNON.Body[]* - Candidates; defaults to every body in the world.

**Returns** *CANNON.Body[]* The bodies that were pushed, so the caller can break, score or light them.

```js
phys.blast(bomb.position, { radius: 3, kick: 16, lift: 0.3, spin: 6 }).forEach((body) => shatter(body));
```

#### `Kit.physics().wakeWhere(shouldWake, bodies = world.bodies)`

Wake the sleeping bodies a predicate picks. Cannon only wakes sleepers on contact with a moving body, so anything
resting on a body you removed or teleported hangs in the air until woken by hand.

- `shouldWake` *(body: CANNON.Body) =\> boolean*
- `[bodies]` *CANNON.Body[]* - Candidates; defaults to every body in the world.

#### `Kit.physics().surface(body, name)`

Give a body a named surface. On its own this changes nothing: declare how two surfaces meet with `contact`.

- `body` *CANNON.Body*
- `name` *string* - Any label, e.g. 'rubber', 'ice', 'bumper'.

**Returns** *CANNON.Body*

#### `Kit.physics().contact(nameA, nameB, { friction = world.defaultContactMaterial.friction, restitution = world.defaultContactMaterial.restitution } = {})`

Declare how two named surfaces behave when they touch. Pairs you do not declare, and any contact where one body
has no surface, keep the world's default friction and restitution.

- `nameA` *string*
- `nameB` *string* - May equal nameA.
- `[options]` *{ friction?: number, restitution?: number }* - Each defaults to the world default. restitution 0 = no bounce, 1 = loses no speed.

```js
phys.surface(ball, 'ball'); phys.surface(pad, 'bumper');
phys.contact('ball', 'bumper', { restitution: 0.85 });   // balls spring off pads, everything else is unchanged
```

### Types

- **BodyOptions**
  - `[mass=1]` *number* - 0 makes the body immovable
  - `[at]` *[number, number, number]* - starting position (default: the mesh's position)
  - `[damping=0.01]` *number* - linear damping 0..1 (spheres only)

## bodies.js

> Kit bodies: the pieces Kit.physics (kit/physics.js) needs for built contraptions and 2D-in-3D games. Each helper takes
> the `phys` object that Kit.physics() returns, so it composes with everything already there.
>
>   compound   - one static or kinematic body from several rotated boxes and discs:   Kit.physics.compound(phys, mesh, shapes, { at, angle })
>   stepRules  - per-body rules enforced after every physics step:                    Kit.physics.stepRules(phys, [keepInPlane('z'), limitSpeed(14)])
>   keepInPlane / limitSpeed - the two rules a flat marble, pinball or platform game needs
>   layers     - named collision groups:                                              layers.assign(body, 'marble', ['part'])
>   sensor     - a body that reports contacts but pushes nothing (triggers, portals, boost pads)
>   surfaces   - named materials with their own bounce and grip against the scenery:    surfaces.assign(ballBody, 'rubber')
>   retire     - remove a body safely, even from inside a collision callback
>
> Owns: body construction beyond a single axis-aligned box, planar and speed constraints, contact filtering and safe removal.
> Does not own: the world, stepping or mesh sync (kit/physics.js), or what a contact means (the game's onHit handlers).
> Needs the CANNON global (cannon.js 0.6.2). Unit-tested against real cannon in tools/test-kit.js.

### Types

- **CompoundShape** - One collider of a compound body, in the body's own frame.
  - `[box]` *number[]* - full size [width, height, depth] in world units. Give either `box` or `disc`.
  - `[disc]` *number* - radius of a round collider (a sphere: in a planar game it behaves as a disc).
  - `[at]` *number[]* - offset [x, y, z] from the body origin; defaults to the origin.
  - `[angle]` *number* - rotation in radians about the body's turn axis; defaults to 0.
- **SurfaceRow** - How one kind of moving thing meets the scenery.
  - `friction` *number* - 0 = ice, 1 = sandpaper.
  - `restitution` *number* - 0 = dead stop, 1 = keeps all its bounce.

## touch.js

> Kit touch: the pure models behind finger-skill moments. No DOM, no THREE - runs in node (see tools/test-kit.js).
>
>   trace  - follow a moving Kit.path target: catch meter, line tension, streak, accuracy   trace.step(state, dt, pointer)
>   stroke - a drawn polyline with a minimum step and a length budget (ropes, routes, ink)  stroke.add(point)
>   lasso  - which targets a drawn loop encloses                                            lasso(points, targets)
>   zone   - a sweet spot with graded bands around it                                       zone(0.6, [0.07, 0.18]).grade(v)
>   sweep  - a needle that swings 0..1 over time (stop-in-the-zone meters)                  sweep(0.5)(t)
>   walker - tap-to-walk: a point that walks to a target at a speed, inside a constraint    walker.goTo(p); walker.step(dt)
>
> Owns: the rules and numbers of these interactions. Does not own: input events, drawing or sound. Feed a model pointer
> positions and dt, then draw what it returns. A hold-and-release gauge is a zone plus any Kit.curve ramp of the held time.

### `Kit.touch.trace(options)`

A trace-accuracy meter over a moving target: the model of "keep your finger on the swimming fish".
The state is plain data and every function returns a new state, so a fight can be simulated, replayed or tested.

- `options` *TraceOptions*

**Returns** *{ start: (pathTime?: number) =\> TraceState, drift: (state: TraceState, dt: number) =\> TraceState, step: (state: TraceState, dt: number, pointer: Point \| null) =\> TraceState, accuracy: (state: TraceState) =\> number }*

```js
const trace = Kit.touch.trace({ target: Kit.path.lissajous(1, 2), tolerance: 0.3, fillSeconds: 4 });
  let fight = trace.start();
  fight = trace.step(fight, dt, isTouching ? { x, y } : null);   // every frame; drift() while the player gets ready
  if (fight.outcome === 'won') award(trace.accuracy(fight));
```

#### `Kit.touch.trace().start(pathTime = 0)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.touch.trace().drift(state, dt)`

Advance the target only: the path keeps moving but nothing is scored (count-ins, grace periods).

- `state` *TraceState*
- `dt` *number* - Seconds.

**Returns** *TraceState*

#### `Kit.touch.trace().step(state, dt, pointer)`

Advance the target and score this slice of time against the pointer.

- `state` *TraceState*
- `dt` *number* - Seconds.
- `pointer` *Point \| null* - Where the player is pointing, in the target's units; null when not touching.

**Returns** *TraceState*

#### `Kit.touch.trace().accuracy(state)`

Share of the scored time spent on target (0 before anything was scored).

- `state` *TraceState*

**Returns** *number* 0..1

### `Kit.touch.stroke({ minStep = 0, maxLength = Infinity } = {})`

A polyline drawn by a finger, with a minimum spacing between points and a total length budget.

- `[options]` *{ minStep?: number, maxLength?: number }* - minStep: points closer than this to the last one are skipped (same units as the points); maxLength: the budget, after which add() refuses.

**Returns** *{ points: Point[], length: number, add: (point: Point) =\> boolean, isSpent: () =\> boolean, remaining: () =\> number, reset: () =\> void }* remaining() is the unused share of the budget, 1..0.

```js
const rope = Kit.touch.stroke({ minStep: 0.15, maxLength: 7 });
  if (rope.add(pointer)) redraw(rope.points);
  if (rope.isSpent()) closeTheNet();
```

#### `Kit.touch.stroke().add(point)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.touch.stroke().isSpent()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.touch.stroke().remaining()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.touch.stroke().reset()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.touch.lasso(points, targets, { closeTolerance = Infinity, at = (target) => target } = {})`

Score a drawn loop: which targets does it enclose? The loop is shut by joining its two ends, so the player does
not have to land exactly on the starting point; closeTolerance says how big a gap that join may bridge.

- `points` *Point[]* - The drawn line, in the same space as the targets.
- `targets` *T[]* - Anything with a position.
- `[options]` *{ closeTolerance?: number, at?: (target: T) =\> Point }* - closeTolerance: largest end-to-end gap still treated as a loop (default: any); at: where a target is (default: the target itself).

**Returns** *{ isClosed: boolean, inside: T[], centre: Point }* centre is the mean of the drawn points.

**Complexity** O(p x t) time for p drawn points and t targets (one point-in-polygon test per target), O(t) space.

```js
const { inside } = Kit.touch.lasso(rope.points, fish, { closeTolerance: 1.5, at: (f) => f.position });
```

### `Kit.touch.zone(centre, halfWidths)`

A sweet spot on a 0..1 gauge with graded bands around it (watering to the right level, stopping a needle).

- `centre` *number* - Where the sweet spot is.
- `halfWidths` *number[]* - Half-width of each band, best (narrowest) first, e.g. [0.07, 0.18].

**Returns** *{ centre: number, bands: { from: number, to: number, grade: number }[], grade: (value: number) =\> number }* grade(value) is halfWidths.length + 1 inside the best band, one less per band outwards, and 1 outside all.

**Complexity** grade: O(b) for b bands (two or three in practice).

```js
const sweet = Kit.touch.zone(0.6, [0.07, 0.18]);
  sweet.grade(0.62) === 3;  sweet.grade(0.75) === 2;  sweet.grade(0.1) === 1;
```

#### `Kit.touch.zone().grade(value)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.touch.sweep(cyclesPerSecond, phase = 0)`

A needle that swings smoothly between 0 and 1: the moving part of a stop-in-the-zone meter.

- `cyclesPerSecond` *number* - Full there-and-back swings per second.
- `[phase=0]` *number* - Starting offset in cycles (0 starts mid-gauge, moving up).

**Returns** *(seconds: number) =\> number* Needle position 0..1 at a time.

### `Kit.touch.walker({ at, speed, constrain = (point) => point, arriveWithin = 0.06, stuckSeconds = 0.35 })`

Tap-to-walk movement on a plane: a point that walks toward a target at a fixed speed and gives up when blocked.

- `options` *Object*
- `options.at` *Point* - Starting position.
- `options.speed` *number* - Units per second.
- `[options.constrain]` *(point: Point) =\> Point* - Pulls a point back into the walkable area (default: anywhere).
- `[options.arriveWithin=0.06]` *number* - Distance at which the walker counts as having arrived.
- `[options.stuckSeconds=0.35]` *number* - Seconds of being held back by `constrain` before it stops trying.

**Returns** *{ position: Point, target: Point, goTo: (point: Point) =\> void, stop: () =\> void, step: (dt: number) =\> { isMoving: boolean, heading: number, hasArrived: boolean } }* heading is the sign of the x movement this step (-1, 0, 1), for sprite facing; hasArrived is true only on the step that reaches the target.

```js
const walker = Kit.touch.walker({ at: { x: 0, y: 0 }, speed: 4, constrain: keepOnShore });
  walker.goTo(tappedPoint);
  const { isMoving, heading } = walker.step(dt);   // then copy walker.position onto the sprite
```

#### `Kit.touch.walker().goTo(point)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.touch.walker().stop()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.touch.walker().step(dt)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### Types

- **TraceState**
  - `target` *(time: number) =\> Point* - Kit.path the player has to follow.
  - `tolerance` *number* - Largest pointer-to-target distance that still counts as on target (path units).
  - `fillSeconds` *number* - Seconds on target, at boost 1, that fill the meter from empty.
  - `[drainRate=0.5]` *number* - Meter lost per second off target, as a fraction of the fill rate.
  - `[strainRate=0.4]` *number* - Tension gained per second while touching but off target (tension runs 0..1).
  - `[looseRate=strainRate]` *number* - Tension gained per second while not touching at all.
  - `[recoverRate=0.2]` *number* - Tension shed per second on target.
  - `[smoothing=Infinity]` *number* - Rate (1/s) at which the reported target chases its path point. A finite rate turns the teleports of a darting path into quick, readable bursts.
  - `[boost]` *(streakSeconds: number) =\> number* - Fill-rate multiplier from the unbroken seconds on target.
  - `[pace]` *(state: TraceState) =\> number* - Path-time multiplier, read every step (tiring, panicking targets).
  - `pathTime` *number* - Time fed to the target path (warped by pace).
  - `target` *Point* - Where the target is now (after smoothing).
  - `elapsed` *number* - Scored seconds so far.
  - `onSeconds` *number* - Scored seconds spent on target.
  - `streak` *number* - Unbroken seconds on target right now.
  - `progress` *number* - The catch meter, 0..1.
  - `tension` *number* - The fail meter, 0..1.
  - `isOnTarget` *boolean* - Was the pointer within tolerance on the last step?
  - `outcome` *null \| 'won' \| 'lost'* - 'won' when progress reaches 1, 'lost' when tension reaches 1.

## pixel.js

> Kit pixel: pixel-paper sprites. Pixel art is written as data (rows of characters + a palette, optionally several frames),
> baked into a crisp nearest-filtered texture with an off-white paper border, and stood up in the scene as a paper cut-out.
>
>   const ART = Kit.pixel.sheet(PALETTE, { hero: { frames: [[...rows], [...rows]], anims: { walk: [1, 0] } }, tree: [...rows] });
>   const paper = Kit.pixel.use(ctx, { unit: 1 / 16, lean: 0.3, shadow: true, tint });      // per-game defaults
>   const hero = paper.sprite(ART.hero, { at: [x, y, z] });
>   hero.play('walk', 9);  hero.face(-1);  hero.hop(0.3);  hero.squash();   // frames, paper flip, springy feedback
>   paper.update(dt);                                                       // once per frame, drives every sprite
>
> Owns: sprite definitions as data (sheet / recolor / tile / label / cells), baking them to textures, the paper sprite
> with its frame, flip, hop and squash animation, and true-colour unlit materials (paper.flat) for backdrops and water.
> Does not own: movement, game rules or the art itself.
>
> Colour: sprites are unlit and sRGB-correct. While the kit's post pass is on they pre-invert its ACES curve, so palette
> colours reach the screen as written; paper whites then sit near 2.3 in HDR, so use ctx.post.bloom(0), or a bloom
> threshold of 2.5 or more, to keep them matte.

### `Kit.pixel.use(ctx, defaults = {})`

Per-game sprite factory: shares materials, the blob shadow and one update loop between its sprites.

- `ctx` *Object* - The game ctx (scene, post).
- `[defaults]` *SpriteOptions* - Defaults for every sprite: { unit: 1 / 16, lean: 0, shadow: true, tint: white }.

**Returns** *{ sprite: (def: SpriteDef, options?: SpriteOptions) =\> PaperSprite, flat: (map?: Object \| null, options?: Object) =\> Object, tint: Object, update: (dt: number) =\> void }*

**Complexity** update: O(s) per frame for s visible sprites, with no allocation. Sprites of one definition share a texture and a material, so draw state changes scale with definitions, not sprites.

#### `Kit.pixel.use().sprite(def, options = {})`

Stand a paper cut-out up in the scene.

- `def` *SpriteDef*
- `[options]` *SpriteOptions*

**Returns** *PaperSprite*

#### `Kit.pixel.use().flat(map = null, { color = 0xffffff, opacity = 1, additive = false, alphaTest = 0 } = {})`

An unlit, double-sided material with the same true-colour handling as the sprites (backdrops, water, rings).

- `[map]` *Object \| null* - THREE.Texture or null for a plain colour.
- `[options]` *{ color?: number \| Object, opacity?: number, additive?: boolean, alphaTest?: number }* - color: 0xRRGGBB, or a THREE.Color instance that stays shared (mutate it to recolour every user).

**Returns** *Object* THREE.MeshBasicMaterial

#### `Kit.pixel.use().update(dt)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.pixel.sheet(pal, table)`

Turn a table of art into named definitions that share one palette.

- `pal` *Object\<string, number\>*
- `table` *Object\<string, string[] \| Partial\<SpriteDef\>\>* - name -> rows, or a partial definition.

**Returns** *Object\<string, SpriteDef\>*

**Complexity** O(n) for n entries; the rows themselves are shared, not copied.

### `Kit.pixel.texture(def)`

The baked texture of a definition (cached per definition object).

- `def` *SpriteDef*

**Returns** *Baked*

### `Kit.pixel.canvas(def, { frame = 0, scale = 4 } = {})`

One frame as a plain canvas scaled by whole pixels, for HUD cards and shop icons.

- `def` *SpriteDef*
- `[options]` *{ frame?: number, scale?: number }* - scale: screen pixels per art pixel (default 4).

**Returns** *HTMLCanvasElement*

### `Kit.pixel.cells(def, frame = 0, border = borderOf(def))`

Every pixel to paint for one frame: the art, plus a paper border around its silhouette.

- `def` *SpriteDef*
- `[frame=0]` *number*
- `[border]` *number* - Border width in pixels (default: the definition's own).

**Returns** *Cell[]* Cells may have negative coordinates: the border sits outside the art's box.

**Complexity** O(w x h x b^2) time for a w x h frame with border b (each empty pixel looks at its (2b+1)^2 neighbours), O(w x h) space. Runs once per definition at bake time, never per frame.

```js
Kit.pixel.cells({ rows: ['r'], pal: { r: 0xff0000 } });   // 1 red cell + 8 paper cells around it
```

### `Kit.pixel.label(text, { ink = LABEL_INK, fill = PAPER } = {})`

A paper tag with text in the 3x5 pixel font (capitals, digits and ! ? + - . only; anything else is a space).

- `text` *string*
- `[colors]` *{ ink?: number, fill?: number }* - Text and background colours, 0xRRGGBB.

**Returns** *SpriteDef*

**Complexity** O(characters).

### `Kit.pixel.recolor(def, swaps)`

A copy of a definition with some palette colours swapped (blossom tree from a green tree).

- `def` *SpriteDef*
- `swaps` *Object\<string, number\>* - character -> new 0xRRGGBB.

**Returns** *SpriteDef*

### `Kit.pixel.tile(def, count)`

A single-frame definition made of `count` copies of a sprite's first frame side by side (fence runs, wave strips).

- `def` *SpriteDef*
- `count` *number*

**Returns** *SpriteDef*

**Complexity** O(rows x count) characters.

### `Kit.pixel.PAPER`

Paper colour of the border around every cut-out, and the darker tone used directly under the art (a cut edge).

### Types

- **Baked** - One sprite as data.
  - `[name]` *string* - For warnings.
  - `[rows]` *string[]* - One frame: strings of palette characters ('.' and ' ' are transparent).
  - `[frames]` *string[][]* - Several frames (instead of rows); shorter frames are bottom-aligned.
  - `pal` *Object\<string, number\>* - Palette: character -> 0xRRGGBB.
  - `[anims]` *Object\<string, number[]\>* - Named frame sequences for sprite.play(name).
  - `[mirror]` *boolean* - Rows give the left half; the right half is its mirror image.
  - `[border=1]` *number* - Paper border width in pixels.
  - `[wrap]` *boolean* - Seamless repeating tile for surfaces: no border, no margin.
  - `canvas` *HTMLCanvasElement* - All frames side by side.
  - `texture` *Object* - THREE.CanvasTexture over the canvas (nearest-filtered, sRGB).
  - `frameCount` *number*
  - `artWidth` *number* - Width of the art alone, in pixels.
  - `artHeight` *number*
  - `frameWidth` *number* - Width of one frame including border and margin, in pixels.
  - `frameHeight` *number*
  - `padding` *number* - Border plus a one-pixel margin on every side (0 for wrap tiles).
- **PaperSprite**
  - `[at]` *number[]* - [x, y, z] of the sprite's foot (or centre, with anchor 'center').
  - `[unit]` *number* - World units per art pixel.
  - `[lean]` *number* - Backward tilt in radians, so upright paper faces a tilted camera.
  - `[shadow]` *boolean* - Soft blob shadow on the ground under it.
  - `[tint]` *Object* - THREE.Color multiplied into the art; a shared instance recolours every user at once.
  - `[bright=1]` *number* - Extra brightness multiplier.
  - `[anchor='bottom']` *'bottom' \| 'center'*
  - `[parent]` *Object* - THREE.Object3D to add it to (default: the scene).
  - `root` *Object* - THREE.Group: move this.
  - `pivot` *Object* - THREE.Group inside root: rotate or scale this for your own effects.
  - `mesh` *Object* - The textured plane.
  - `shadow` *Object \| null*
  - `def` *SpriteDef* - What it is showing now.
  - `width` *number* - World width of the art.
  - `height` *number*
  - `facing` *number* - +1 (as drawn) or -1 (flipped).
  - `set` *(def: SpriteDef) =\> PaperSprite* - Show another definition.
  - `frame` *(index: number) =\> PaperSprite* - Show one frame (wraps).
  - `play` *(name: string \| number[], framesPerSecond?: number) =\> PaperSprite* - Loop a named anim or a frame list.
  - `stop` *(index?: number) =\> PaperSprite* - Stop animating and show a frame.
  - `show` *(isVisible: boolean) =\> PaperSprite*
  - `face` *(direction: number, isInstant?: boolean) =\> PaperSprite* - Paper flip toward the sign of `direction`.
