  - `[base]` *string* - set by `sidedDials`: the id both sides share (they are drawn from it, so a rolled body is symmetric until someone unlinks it)
  - `[side]` *('left'\|'right')* - set by `sidedDials`
  - `[twin]` *string* - set by `sidedDials`: the id of the other side
- **DialSet**
  - `ids` *string[]* - dial index -> id
  - `count` *number*
  - `min` *Float32Array* - per dial
  - `max` *Float32Array* - per dial
  - `defaults` *Float32Array* - per dial
  - `opDial` *Int16Array* - per operation: which dial
  - `opBone` *Int16Array* - per operation: which bone
  - `opChannel` *Uint8Array* - per operation: a `CHANNELS` value
  - `opAmount` *Float32Array* - per operation
  - `shapeOps` *Array\<{dial: number, bone: number, path: Array\<string\|number\>, amount: number, mode: string}\>* - per shape operation: which dial changes which number of which bone's shape
  - `featureOps` *Array\<{dial: number, feature: string, path: Array\<string\|number\>, amount: number, mode: string, side: ?string}\>* - per feature operation: resolved against the features of an assembled body by kit/body-surface.js
  - `unused` *string[]* - ids of dials that touch no bone of this plan (often a misspelt role)
  - `indexOf` *function(string): number* - id -> dial index; throws naming the known dials

## body-parts.js

> Kit body parts: things that plug into sockets. A part is a small body plan of its own (a hat, a sword, a pair
> of wings, a gem) that says which socket types it fits and may expose sockets for further parts, so a gem sits
> on a sword that sits in a hand. Props are parts; there is no second mechanism for them.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.part` (compile a part), `Kit.body.assemble` (plan + fills -> a new plan with the parts' bones,
>        sockets, surface features and regions appended, type-checked), `Kit.body.canAttach` and
>        `Kit.body.openSockets`.
> Does not own: the plan format, kinematics or capabilities (kit/body-plan.js: an assembled body IS a plan, so
>        all of that works on it unchanged), nor which parts an individual wears (kit/body-recipe.js).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### Types

- **PartSpec**
  - `id` *string* - name of the part ('sword')
  - `fits` *string[]* - socket types this part plugs into (['grip'])
  - `[anchor]` *string* - name of one of the part's own sockets: the point of the part that sits on the host socket (a sword's grip). It is used up by attaching, so it is not a socket of the assembled body. Default: the part's origin.
  - `bones` *Object[]* - the part's bones, as `BoneSpec` rows of kit/body-plan.js; may be empty for a part that is only `features` and / or `regions` (a pair of eyes, a printed shirt)
  - `[sockets]` *Object[]* - the part's own sockets, as `SocketSpec` rows; other parts attach here
  - `[features]` *Object[]* - surface features (kit/body-surface.js `FeatureSpec`); `bone` names one of the part's bones, a bone of the wearer (any name the part does not have), or is omitted (or '@host') for the bone of the socket the part sits in
  - `[regions]` *Object[]* - painted regions (kit/body-surface.js `RegionSpec`); `roles` select bones of the whole wearer, `bones` names bones as a feature's `bone` does
  - `[label]` *string* - what an editor calls the part (default: worked out from the id)
  - `[icon]` *string* - the name of an icon an editor shows for it
  - `[capabilities]` *string[]* - declared capabilities the part grants its wearer (such as 'hover')
- **Part**
  - `id` *string*
  - `spec` *PartSpec* - the data it was compiled from
  - `fits` *ReadonlyArray\<string\>* - socket type names it plugs into
  - `fitTypes` *Uint16Array* - the same as type ids (`Kit.body.socketType`)
  - `anchorPosition` *ReadonlyArray\<number\>* - [x, y, z] of the anchor in the part's own space at rest
  - `plan` *Object* - the part alone as a `BodyPlan` (a sword lying on the ground poses and draws like a body)

## body-surface.js

> Kit body surface: what is drawn ON a body's bones rather than as bones. Surface features (eyes, brows, a nose,
> a mouth, ears, freckles, a scar) are placed on a bone's shape by azimuth and elevation, alone or as a mirrored
> pair, with a flat stamp (frames of outlines: open, half, closed) and / or a small solid; regions (a sleeve, a
> hairline, stripes, a belly patch) are predicate trees over a bone's along / around coordinates that paint the
> bone with another colour channel. Both are data on a plan or a part; this file lays them out for a bake.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.surfaceLayout(plan)` (validate and index a plan's features and regions; cached per plan) and
>        `Kit.body.surfaceOf(...)` (one individual's placements and painted layers, with `regionAt`), plus the
>        `FeatureSpec` and `RegionSpec` shapes and `FRAME_SETS`; and the figure renderer's two hooks for a bake,
>        `Kit.body.FIGURE_HOOKS` (`featuresOf`: a bone's face as one stamps piece plus feature solids, kept per
>        placed list; `regionMapOf`: a bone's regions baked to a small map, kept per outfit).
> Does not own: the maths of placing on a solid (kit/surface.js), the predicates (kit/region.js), how features
>        and regions travel with parts (kit/body-parts.js), or drawing them (renderers; kit/body-debug.js marks them).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### Types

- **StampLayer**
  - `outline` *number[][]* - [[u, v], ...] in the stamp's own square, -1 to 1 each way (`Kit.surface.ellipse` draws one); u runs across (towards the body's left on a front-facing stamp), v up
  - `paint` *{channel: string, shade: number}* - a colour channel of the species and a shade of its ramp (0 is the darkest); the shade is clamped to the ramp
  - `[least]` *number* - the fewest target texels the layer's smaller side may cover: a renderer grows a layer that would be smaller (a glint, a pupil), so it survives a low-resolution target
  - `[drop]` *number* - the layer is left out while its smaller side covers fewer target texels than this: detail (the white of an eye, a pupil) that would only be noise on a small sprite. Not with `least`.
  - `[fixed]` *boolean* - the layer is not mirrored on the right copy of a pair: a glint sits on the lit side of both eyes
- **FeatureSpec**
  - `id` *string* - what dials and animators call it ('eye', 'mouth'); several features may share an id
  - `[bone]` *string* - the bone it sits on (a part's features default to the bone of the socket it is in)
  - `[azimuth=0]` *number* - degrees about the bone's +Y from its +Z (the front) towards +X (its left)
  - `[elevation=0]` *number* - degrees above the middle of the bone's shape
  - `[spacing]` *number* - degrees between the two copies of a mirrored pair (left at +spacing/2); omit for one copy
  - `[size=0.03]` *(number\|number[])* - metres across the stamp, or [width, height]
  - `[rotation=0]` *number* - degrees about the surface normal (the right copy of a pair turns the other way)
  - `[lift=0]` *number* - metres out along the normal (a nose tip stands proud)
  - `[frames]` *Object\<string, StampLayer[]\>* - frame name -> the stamp's layers, bottom first
  - `[rest]` *string* - the frame shown when nothing animates it (default: the first frame)
  - `[shape]` *Object* - a solid in the feature's own frame, metres at the authored size (`Kit.body.SHAPES` row: +X across, +Y along `up`, +Z out along the normal), for a nose or an ear that has a silhouette. A capsule, lathe or extrude states `end` [x, y, z], the far end of its long axis (a nose runs down: [0, -0.03, 0.01])
  - `[paint]` *string* - the colour channel of the solid (default 'skin')
  - `[glow]` *boolean* - draw it as a glow (eyes): a renderer leaves it out of outlines
  - `[under=0]` *number* - stamps of a feature with a higher `under` are drawn beneath the others on its bone whatever order the parts were attached in: blush and freckles go under the eyes
  - `[outlined=true]` *boolean* - false for a solid that should not get an outline of its own (a nose inside the face's silhouette), while still shading and inking as part of its bone
  - `[part]` *string* - set by kit/body-parts.js: the part that brought it
- **RegionSpec**
  - `id` *string* - what it is ('sleeve', 'hairline')
  - `[roles]` *string[]* - paint every bone of the body carrying ALL of these roles (never a prop's bone)
  - `[except]` *string[]* - ...but not a bone carrying any of these (['hand'] keeps a sleeve off the hand)
  - `[bones]` *string[]* - paint these bones by name
  - `where` *Object* - a `Kit.region` predicate tree over the bone's along / around coordinates
  - `paint` *string* - the colour channel it paints with
  - `[layer=0]` *number* - stacking order: higher covers lower (skin marks 1, clothes 2, outerwear 3, hair 4)
  - `[ink=true]` *boolean* - false for a pattern inside a garment (stripes, stipple): a renderer draws no line round it
  - `[raise=0]` *number* - metres the painted area stands proud of the bone's surface: a renderer draws it on a shell of the bone's own (dialled) shape, so cloth and hair have thickness and follow every dial
  - `[occludes=false]` *boolean* - the area hides face stamps beneath it (hair over a brow); a beard or stubble leaves it false so the mouth still shows
  - `[part]` *string* - set by kit/body-parts.js: the part that brought it
- **FeaturePlacement** - One copy of a feature on an individual: everything a renderer needs to stamp or build it.
  - `id` *string*
  - `bone` *number* - bone index in the assembled plan
  - `side` *?string* - 'left' or 'right' for a copy of a pair, else null
  - `flip` *boolean* - true for the right copy: mirror the stamp's u (and the solid's x)
  - `point` *number[]* - [x, y, z] on the bone's (dialled) shape, in the bone's space, lifted by `lift`
  - `normal` *number[]* - unit, out of the surface
  - `up` *number[]* - unit, in the tangent plane; across = up x normal
  - `size` *number[]* - [width, height] metres
  - `rotation` *number* - radians about the normal
  - `frames` *Object\<string, Array\<{outline: number[][], ramp: number, shade: number}\>\>* - frame name -> layers with their paint resolved to (index into the bake's `ramps`, shade); ramp -1 when the species lacks the channel
  - `rest` *?string*
  - `shape` *?Object*
  - `scale` *number[]* - [across, up] the solid's stretch: this copy's size over the authored size (dials)
  - `ramp` *number* - the solid's ramp index (-1 when none)
  - `part` *?string* - the part it came from, null for the plan's own
  - `glow` *boolean* - the feature asked to be drawn as a glow (eyes): never outlined
  - `outlined` *boolean* - false when the solid should get no outline of its own
  - `lift` *number* - metres `point` stands off the surface (already applied to it)

## body-recipe.js

> Kit body recipe: an individual as three small things, `{ species, seed, overrides }`, and everything needed to
> turn that into a body and back: a catalogue of species (a `Kit.lineage` whose definitions name a plan, dials,
> colour channels and weighted socket fills), seed derivation, validation, a compact binary form, a canonical
> hash, species tables that roll recipes, and a bake cache.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.catalog(spec)` and what it returns: `resolve` (recipe -> individual), `describe`, `validate`,
>        `normalize`, `encode` / `decode`, `hash`, `table` (weighted species -> recipes) and `bakery` (individual
>        -> assembled plan + proportioned rest pose, cached by hash); the roll rules (`Kit.body.rolls`: harmony
>        schemes, followers, contrast and rooms as pure functions) with `clashes` and `reconcile`.
> Does not own: the inheritance fold (kit/lineage.js), the seed derivation and content hash (kit/seeded.js),
>        the byte format's primitives (kit/pack.js), plans, dials and kinematics (kit/body-plan.js), attaching
>        (kit/body-parts.js), instances (kit/body-store.js) or anything that moves or draws a body. This file
>        is the composition of those: it gives their generic pieces the shape of a body.
>
> Every field's value is `Kit.seeded` hash(seed, field name), not the next number of a stream, so a field never
> depends on how many fields were read before it: add a dial to a species next month and every existing seed
> keeps its old values for every old field.
>
> Roll rules. A species may also state how its fields go together (`SpeciesDefinition.colorWeights`, `follow`,
> `harmony`, `contrast`, `occupies`): then a field's hash is still its own, but what it chooses among is narrowed
> by the fields rolled before it, so a seed rolls an outfit, not fourteen unrelated colours. The rules are part
> of the catalogue: a seed gives the same individual for as long as the catalogue's tables are unchanged.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### Types

- **ColorFact** - What the rules know of a ramp: its own colour (its middle shade) in OKLCH, plus the same in OKLab.
  - `lightness` *number* - 0..1
  - `chroma` *number* - 0..about 0.4
  - `hue` *number* - degrees
  - `greenRed` *number* - OKLab a
  - `blueYellow` *number* - OKLab b
- **Recipe**
  - `species` *string* - id of a species of the catalogue (a node with a `code`)
  - `seed` *number* - unsigned 32-bit whole number; every field not overridden is derived from it
  - `[overrides]` *Object\<string, (number\|string\|null)\>* - field key -> value. 'dial:<id>' takes a number in the dial's range (clamped and snapped to its steps); 'color:<channel>' the index of a ramp in the channel's palette; 'fill:<socket path>' a part id, or null / 'none' for empty.
- **Individual** - A recipe with every field worked out: what renderers, bakes and editors read.
  - `species` *string*
  - `seed` *number*
  - `dials` *Float32Array* - one value per dial of the species, in `kind.dialIds` order
  - `colors` *Uint8Array* - one ramp index per colour channel, in `kind.colorIds` order: an index into the channel's palette, never a colour, because the shaders downstream are palette-indexed
  - `fills` *Object\<string, string\>* - socket path -> part id, filled sockets only, parents before children
- **SpeciesDefinition** - The shape a catalogue expects of a lineage node's folded values (every key optional except on a species).
  - `[code]` *number* - stable whole number that names the species in the binary form. A node with a code is a species and can be instantiated; a node without one is abstract (an archetype). Never reuse a code.
  - `[plan]` *string* - id of a body plan of the catalogue
  - `[dials]` *Object\<string, {min: number, max: number, default?: number, shape?: string, affects: Object[]}\>* - dial id -> a `DialRow` of kit/body-plan.js without its id, plus `shape` (a `Kit.seeded.SHAPES` name, default 'bell')
  - `[colors]` *Object\<string, string\>* - colour channel -> the name of a palette of the catalogue ({ skin: 'skinTones' }); an individual stores which ramp of that palette it uses
  - `[fills]` *Object\<string, Object\<string, number\>\>* - socket path -> { part id or 'none': weight }
  - `[colorWeights]` *Object\<string, Object\<string, number\>\>* - colour channel -> { ramp id: weight } for the ramps that are not rolled at weight 1 ({ hair: { teal: 0.3 } }); 0 keeps a ramp for editors only
  - `[follow]` *Object\<string, FollowRule\>* - colour channel -> the channel it follows: it takes the ramp of its own palette nearest the followed colour (plus an offset), whatever sets that colour, unless overridden
  - `[harmony]` *HarmonyRule* - the channels whose colours are drawn from one scheme per roll
  - `[contrast]` *ContrastRule[]* - pairs of channels whose colours must stand apart
  - `[occupies]` *Object\<string, string[]\>* - part id -> the rooms it takes up on a body ('crown', 'back'). Two parts that occupy the same room are never rolled together; an override may still combine them
- **FollowRule**
  - `channel` *string* - the channel followed (it may not itself follow another)
  - `[lightness]` *number* - OKLCH lightness added to the followed colour before the nearest ramp is sought
  - `[chroma]` *number* - OKLCH chroma added
  - `[hue]` *number* - degrees added
  - `[among=1]` *number* - the follower picks, by its own seeded field, among this many nearest ramps
- **HarmonyRule**
  - `schemes` *Object\<string, number\>* - scheme id (a key of `Kit.body.rolls.SCHEMES`) -> weight
  - `channels` *string[]* - the channels drawn from the scheme. The first of them (in the species' channel order) to take a colour that is not a neutral sets the key hue
  - `[tolerance=26]` *number* - degrees a colour may sit from a scheme hue
  - `[neutralChroma=0.05]` *number* - OKLCH chroma below which a colour is a neutral
- **ContrastRule**
  - `between` *string[]* - two channels
  - `[lightness]` *number* - least OKLCH lightness gap that satisfies the rule
  - `[chroma]` *number* - least OKLCH chroma gap that satisfies it instead
  - `[hue]` *number* - least hue gap, degrees, that satisfies it instead, between two colours that are not neutrals. State at least one of the three: garments that meet want value or saturation apart (two hues of one value vibrate), while skin against cloth may also be told apart by hue
- **SpeciesKind** - A species compiled for fast derivation.
  - `id` *string*
  - `code` *number*
  - `definition` *Object* - the folded `SpeciesDefinition` (frozen)
  - `plan` *Object* - the bare `BodyPlan`
  - `dialIds` *string[]*
  - `dialRows` *Object[]* - `DialRow`s, for compiling against an assembled plan
  - `dialMin` *number[]*
  - `dialMax` *number[]*
  - `dialDefaults` *number[]*
  - `dialDefaultSteps` *number[]* - per dial: the step that decodes to the default exactly
  - `unusedDials` *string[]* - dials that touch no bone of the bare plan
  - `colorIds` *string[]*
  - `paletteNames` *string[]* - per colour channel: the catalogue palette it draws from
  - `palettes` *Object[][]* - per colour channel: that palette's ramps (`Ramp` rows of kit/palette.js)
  - `fillPaths` *string[]* - declared socket paths, parents before children
  - `fillFields` *Array\<{path: string, hash: number, options: Array\<{part: ?string, weight: number}\>}\>* - the declared fills compiled: the socket path, its field id and its weighted options
  - `dialDraws` *Array\<function(number): number\>* - per dial: seed -> a number in [0, 1) (`Kit.seeded.field`)
  - `colorDraws` *Array\<function(number): number\>* - per colour channel: seed -> a number in [0, 1)
  - `keyByHash` *Map\<number, string\>* - field id -> override key, for decoding
  - `rolls` *Object* - the species' colour rules compiled (`compileColorRules`): per-channel colour facts and weights, followers, the harmony rule and the contrast rules; `active` is false when it states none
  - `rooms` *?{flags: Object, all: number, taken: Object\<string, {requirement: Object, mask: number}\>}* - the rooms its parts occupy as a `Kit.flags` set, the mask of all of them free and, per part that occupies any, the requirement "my rooms are free" with the mask it takes; null when no part occupies a room
- **RecipeProblem**
  - `code` *string* - one of 'unknown-species', 'abstract-species', 'bad-seed', 'bad-overrides', 'unknown-field', 'bad-value', 'out-of-range', 'bad-color', 'unknown-part', 'no-such-socket', 'part-does-not-fit', 'socket-required'; and from `clashes`: 'contrast', 'harmony', 'occupied'
  - `[field]` *string* - the override key at fault
  - `message` *string* - what is wrong and how to fix it
- **Bake** - Everything a renderer needs for one distinct look, built once and shared by every instance that has it.
  - `hash` *number* - the canonical hash of the individual (the cache key)
  - `species` *string*
  - `plan` *Object* - the assembled `BodyPlan`: bare plan plus the parts worn
  - `rest` *Object* - the proportioned rest pose (a `JointTransforms`); copy it, never write it
  - `shapes` *ReadonlyArray\<?Object\>* - 1 per bone of `plan`: its shape row with the shape dials applied (null for the default capsule), from `Kit.body.shapesWithDials`
  - `surface` *Object* - the individual's surface features (placed) and painted regions, with `regionAt(bone, along, angle, normalX, normalY, normalZ)` -> index in `ramps` or -1 (kit/body-surface.js)
  - `colors` *Readonly\<Object\<string, Object\>\>* - channel -> the `Ramp` the individual uses for it
  - `boneRamp` *Int16Array* - 1 per bone of `plan`: the index in `ramps` of the ramp the bone is painted with, or -1 when the bone names no paint channel or the species has no such channel
  - `ramps` *ReadonlyArray\<Object\>* - the individual's ramps, one per colour channel of the species
  - `individual` *Individual*
- **CatalogSpec**
  - `plans` *Object\<string, Object\>* - plan id -> `BodyPlanSpec` without its id
  - `[parts]` *Object\<string, Object\>* - part id -> `PartSpec` without its id
  - `nodes` *Object\<string, Object\>* - lineage nodes (kit/lineage.js) whose `set` follows `SpeciesDefinition`
  - `[traits]` *Object\<string, Object\>* - lineage traits; two traits of one node may not fill the same socket
  - `[palettes]` *Object\<string, Object[]\>* - palette name -> its ramps, each { id, shades: [dark ... light] } (`Kit.palette.ramp` builds one from a hue)

## body-edit.js

> Kit body edit: everything a character editor needs from a catalogue, with no editor in it. A schema that
> lists every dial, colour channel and socket of a recipe as data (so the editor's controls are generated, not
> written), edits as pure functions from a recipe to a new recipe, and a report of what changed between two
> bakes (so a renderer updates only that).
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.editor(catalog)` and what it returns: `schema`, `setDial`, `setSocket`, `setColor`,
>        `randomize`, `mirror` and `changes`.
> Does not own: any interface (a creator screen builds its sliders and lists from `schema` with kit/ui/),
>        validation rules, derivation or baking (kit/body-recipe.js: every edit goes through `normalize`, so an
>        edit can only produce a recipe the catalogue accepts), or undo (`Kit.history` in kit/space.js holds
>        recipes as they are plain data).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### Types

- **DialControl**
  - `key` *string* - the override key, 'dial:<id>'
  - `id` *string*
  - `label` *string*
  - `group` *string* - 'head', 'arms', 'legs', 'wings', 'tail', 'fins' or 'body' (or the dial's own `group`)
  - `min` *number*
  - `max` *number*
  - `step` *number* - the smallest change that makes a difference
  - `default` *number*
  - `value` *number* - the individual's current value
  - `affects` *Array\<{channel: string, bones: string[], param?: string, feature?: string}\>* - what it changes: per bone channel, per shape path (`channel: 'shape'`, `param`) or per feature parameter (`channel: 'feature'`, `feature`, `param`), with the bones of the bare plan it touches
  - `mirror` *?string* - the key of the dial that does the same on the other side, when there is one
  - `side` *?string* - 'left' or 'right' for a sided dial, else null
  - `base` *?string* - for a dial made by `sided: true`, the id both sides share ('eyeSize'); `setDial` with it moves both
  - `linked` *?boolean* - for such a dial: do both sides hold the same value (show one slider)?
  - `order` *number* - where it sorts within its group (from the species' `controls` rows, else list order)
  - `hint` *?string* - a sentence for a tooltip, from `controls`
  - `icon` *?string* - an icon name, from `controls`
- **ColorControl**
  - `key` *string* - the override key, 'color:<channel>'
  - `id` *string* - the channel
  - `label` *string*
  - `palette` *string* - the catalogue palette the channel draws from
  - `ramps` *Object[]* - the choices: `Ramp` rows { id, shades: [dark ... light] }
  - `value` *number* - the index of the ramp the individual uses
  - `group` *string* - from `controls`, else 'colors'
  - `order` *number*
  - `hint` *?string*
  - `icon` *?string*
- **SocketControl**
  - `key` *string* - the override key, 'fill:<path>'
  - `path` *string*
  - `label` *string*
  - `group` *string* - from the roles of the bone the socket (or its outermost parent socket) rides on
  - `type` *string* - the socket type
  - `part` *?string* - what is in it now
  - `options` *string[]* - ids of every catalogue part that fits
  - `allowsEmpty` *boolean* - false for a required socket
  - `mirror` *?string* - the key of the same socket on the other side, when there is one
  - `side` *?string* - 'left' or 'right' for a sided socket (its own bone's side, or its parent socket's)
  - `choices` *Array\<{id: ?string, label: string, icon: ?string, hint: ?string}\>* - what the socket can hold, as a list a picker shows: 'none' (id null) first when it may be empty, then every option with the part's own `label`, `icon` and `hint`
  - `order` *number*
  - `hint` *?string*
  - `icon` *?string*

## body-store.js

> Kit body store: many body instances as columns - where each is, which way it faces, how far through its
> movement cycle it is, how much detail it deserves and which recipe it wears.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.store(capacity)`: the body schema over `Kit.columns`, a `spawn` that places the instance, and
>        `assignDetail` (level of detail by distance from an eye).
> Does not own: slots, the free list and generations (kit/columns.js), what a recipe index points at (the caller
>        keeps the list of recipes or bakes), moving instances (locomotion writes the columns), posing or
>        drawing them, or finding neighbours (`Kit.spatialHash`).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

## body-samples.js

> Kit body samples: three deliberately different body plans (a biped, a quadruped, a fish), four parts (a
> one-bone sword with grip and gem sockets, a ruby, a hat, a pair of wings) and a small family of species over
> them, all as data. They are the proof that one abstraction covers different shapes, the fixtures of the unit
> tests and the bench, and a starting point to copy when writing a real catalogue.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.samples.PLANS`, `PARTS`, `PALETTES`, `NODES`, `TRAITS` and `CATALOG` (the five together,
>        ready for `Kit.body.catalog`).
> Does not own: any behaviour. There is no code here beyond freezing the tables.
>
> A bone's `girth` is its mass data (kit/dynamics.js reads it) and is lean; what is drawn is its `shape` row, a
> stylised solid stated beside it (`drawn`, `flattened`, `fin` below), so every plan is presentable as it stands.
>
> Conventions (kit/body-plan.js): metres, +Y up, +Z forward, +X the body's left. Every plan starts with a
> `ground` bone at the origin, so a `size` dial on the root scales the whole body about the point it stands on.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

## body-wardrobe.js

> Kit body wardrobe: an original, generic humanoid catalogue for a character creator, as data. One body plan (a
> person with clavicles and hip bones so shoulder and hip width are real dials, and a lathe head whose profile
> dials reshape), face features as surface-only parts (eyes with blink frames, brows, noses, mouths with talk
> frames, ears, marks), hair as parts that bring a painted hairline and, for most styles, a silhouette, clothing
> by slot as painted regions with a few silhouette bones (skirts, coats, cloaks, hoods), accessories and held
> items on sockets, and three variants of the base species: stout, lanky and the horned, tailed hornkin.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.wardrobe.CATALOG` (ready for `Kit.body.catalog`) and its parts: `PLANS`, `PARTS`, `PALETTES`,
>        `NODES`, `TRAITS`, and `SLOTS` (socket -> the group and label a creator lists it under).
> Does not own: any behaviour (kit/body-*.js), any screen (a creator builds its controls from `editor.schema`),
>        or any rendering (renderers read the bake: shapes, features, regions, ramps).
>
> Conventions (kit/body-plan.js): metres, +Y up, +Z forward, +X the body's left. Region `s` runs along a bone
> from its joint (0) to its end (1): down an arm or leg, up the spine and the head (chin 0, crown 1). Feature
> azimuth turns from the front towards the body's left; elevation rises from the middle of the head.
>
> Nothing here copies an existing product: names, shapes and outfits are generic descriptions.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

## body-debug.js

> Kit body debug: a stick figure of any posed body plan on a 2D canvas, with its sockets marked. It exists so a
> person can see that a plan, a pose, an attachment or a dial does what the numbers say, and as the smallest
> possible example of a renderer: project the world transforms, draw the bones.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.debug.project` (orthographic projection of a world point for a view angle),
>        `Kit.body.debug.draw` (bones as lines, bone ends, joints, socket markers) and
>        `Kit.body.debug.drawSurface` (a bake's dialled shapes as outlines, its painted regions as tinted spans
>        along each bone, and its surface features as marks), enough to check shapes, faces and outfits by eye.
> Does not own: real rendering (solids, shading, stamps: the pixel and 3D renderers read the same bake), the
>        canvas or its size, or posing.
>
> Runs in node too: `draw` only calls the 2D-context methods listed in CONTEXT_METHODS, so a test passes a
> recorder object instead of a canvas.

### Types

- **DebugView**
  - `centerX` *number* - canvas x of the world origin's vertical axis, pixels
  - `groundY` *number* - canvas y of world height 0, pixels
  - `scale` *number* - pixels per metre
  - `[turn=0]` *number* - radians the camera is turned about +Y: 0 looks at the body's front, a quarter turn looks at its side
  - `[tilt=0]` *number* - radians the camera looks down by: 0 is level (the ground is a line), a little tilt opens the ground plane up so footprints and what stands over them can be told apart

## body-gallery.js

> Kit body gallery: the `bodies` screen (`Kit.play('bodies')`). Six cells, each a different thing made by the
> same code: a biped, a winged variant, a quadruped, a fish, a sword on its own, and a stream of individuals
> rolled from a species table. The biped's right arm reaches for a moving target by inverse kinematics and the
> quadruped's tail follows a simulated chain, so the kinematics and dynamics rungs can be seen working.
>
> Owns:  the screen: its layout, what each cell shows, and the two demonstrations.
> Does not own: anything it shows. Plans, parts and species are kit/body-samples.js; posing is `Kit.rig`,
>        `Kit.reach` and `Kit.dynamics`; drawing is `Kit.body.debug`. It is a debug view, not a renderer.
>
> It is registered like a game (near the end of the menu), so the shell gives it a HUD, a back button and
> clean-up, and the smoke and play tools reach it like any other.

## look-settings.js

> Kit look settings: the look as data and small pure functions. What a tone band is, how dither thresholds are
> laid out, how a figure sits on a texel grid, and the three presets with the knobs each exposes.
>
> Owns:  the band lookup, the light value and the ordered-dither tables the shaders mirror (`bandOf`, `lightOf`,
>        `edgesFor`, `bayerAt`); the grid
>        helpers of pixel mode (`snapToTexel`, `stepYaw`, `texelScale`); smooth outline normals (`weldNormals`);
>        the lights and how kinds of surface answer them (`LIGHTING`, `RESPONSES`); and the presets and knobs
>        (`PRESETS`, `KNOBS`, `settings`).
> Does not own: colour (kit/palette.js: ramps, tones and the packed atlas), textures, materials or passes
>        (look-toon.js, look-ink.js), or the frame (look-view.js).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js.

### `Kit.look.BAND_EDGES`

Band edges on the light value n.L (-1 facing away .. 1 facing the light), by tone count. A pixel's tone is
the number of edges at or below its light. The edges are placed by what they leave of a ball under the key
light of `LIGHTING`: about an eighth of its disc in highlight, a fifth in shadow (a crescent, with the deeper
tones of higher counts nested inside it) and the rest, well over half, in the ramp's own colour: two or
three clean tones on a large form. The second-lightest band (the ramp's own colour) always contains
`LIGHTING.fillCap`, which is what lets the fill light lift a face to its own colour and no further.
tools/test-looks.js measures both.

### `Kit.look.LIGHTING`

The lights of the look, in view space (x right, y up, z toward the viewer), so they keep their place on
screen as the camera or a figure turns: a figure is lit the same from all eight directions.
 - `key`: from the upper left and well in front, so what faces the viewer is lit and the shadow is a
   crescent down the right and under overhangs, never a half.
 - `fill`: almost the viewer's own direction. It never brightens past `fillCap` (a light value inside the
   ramp's own colour band), so it can lift a shadow off a surface but cannot add a highlight. How much of it
   a surface takes is the surface's response (`RESPONSES`).
 - `back`: from behind, to the right and a little below. With `bounce` on, shadowed surface that faces it
   (the far-side silhouette) is lifted one tone, which keeps a figure's dark side off a dark backdrop.
 - `rim`: with `rim` on, lit surface seen edge-on is lifted one tone.

### `Kit.look.RESPONSES`

Responses by name (`LookPart.response`). `form` is everything by default: key light only. `face` is bare
skin that carries features: wherever it is turned toward the viewer the fill holds it at its own colour, so
eyes, brows and a mouth always sit on a readable tone; only the last sliver before the far silhouette can
take the shadow, which keeps the head round. A face is never dithered: a checker of two tones across a
cheek reads as stubble.

### `Kit.look.DITHER_TEXEL_FADE`

How much of a look's dither a frame draws, by the world size of a texel. Dither trades resolution for an
extra apparent tone, which pays on a figure large enough to hold the pattern; on a small sprite a 4 x 4
checker along every band edge is speckle. It is whole while a texel is finer than `full` world units (a
person about 140 texels tall or more) and gone at `none` (about 70 texels tall).

### `Kit.look.ditherAt(dither, texelWorld)`

The dither strength to draw with.

- `dither` *number* - the look's dither strength (`LookSettings.dither`)
- `texelWorld` *number* - world units one target texel covers; 0 when unknown (a perspective camera): no fade

**Returns** *number* 0..dither

```js
Kit.look.ditherAt(0.07, 0.018) // 0.035: half way through the fade
```

### `Kit.look.lightOf(normal, response = RESPONSES.form)`

The light value of a surface: the brighter of the key light's n.L and what the surface's response takes of
the capped fill light. The fragment shader's `lightOf` is this function.

- `normal` *number[]* - unit normal in view space [right, up, toward the viewer]
- `[response]` *LightResponse* - a row of `RESPONSES` (default: a form)

**Returns** *number* -1..1; pass it to `bandOf`

```js
Kit.look.bandOf(Kit.look.lightOf([0, 0, 1], Kit.look.RESPONSES.face), Kit.look.edgesFor(3)) // 1: a face seen head-on is its own colour
```

### `Kit.look.BAYER_4`

The 4 x 4 ordered-dither matrix (thresholds 0..15), row by row.

### `Kit.look.PRESETS`

The three looks, as data. `label` and `about` are for an editor; the rest are LookSettings.

### `Kit.look.KNOBS`

The settings an editor can offer, in display order. Adding a knob is adding a row.

### `Kit.look.bandOf(light, edges)`

The tone of a light value: how many band edges lie at or below it.

- `light` *number* - n.L, -1..1 (plus any dither offset)
- `edges` *number[]* - ascending band edges

**Returns** *number* tone index, 0 (darkest) .. edges.length

**Complexity** O(edges)

```js
Kit.look.bandOf(-0.05, [-0.05, 0.62]) // 1: a value on an edge belongs to the lighter band
```

### `Kit.look.edgesFor(toneCount)`

The default band edges for a tone count, padded to the shader's fixed length with an unreachable edge.

- `toneCount` *number* - 2..6

**Returns** *number[]* five numbers: `toneCount - 1` real edges, then padding

### `Kit.look.bayerAt(column, row)`

The ordered-dither threshold of a pixel, 0..1, repeating every 4 pixels both ways.

- `column` *number* - pixel x (any integer)
- `row` *number* - pixel y (any integer)

**Returns** *number* one of 0, 1/16 .. 15/16

```js
const adjusted = light + (Kit.look.bayerAt(x, y) - 0.5) * 0.12;
```

### `Kit.look.snapToTexel(value, texelSize)`

Rounds a coordinate to the texel grid, so a sprite moves by whole texels and keeps one shape while it moves.

- `value` *number* - a coordinate along a screen axis, world units
- `texelSize` *number* - world units one texel covers

**Returns** *number* the nearest multiple of texelSize

### `Kit.look.stepYaw(angle, steps)`

Rounds a turn to one of `steps` facings, like the 8 or 16 drawn directions of a sprite sheet.

- `angle` *number* - radians
- `steps` *number* - facings per full turn; 0 leaves the angle alone

**Returns** *number* radians

### `Kit.look.texelScale(bufferHeight, rows)`

How many screen pixels one texel covers when a view is drawn at about `rows` texels tall: always a whole
number, because a fractional upscale makes texels of uneven size.

- `bufferHeight` *number* - height of the screen buffer, device pixels
- `rows` *number* - wanted internal height in texels; 0 means native resolution

**Returns** *number* whole pixels per texel, 1 or more

### `Kit.look.weldNormals(positions, normals, tolerance = 1e-4)`

Averages the normals of vertices that share a position. An inverted-hull outline pushes vertices along
their normals; where a mesh has hard edges (a box, a cylinder's cap) the copies of a corner would part
and open a gap in the line, so the hull uses these welded normals instead.

- `positions` *ArrayLike\<number\>* - x, y, z per vertex
- `normals` *ArrayLike\<number\>* - x, y, z per vertex
- `[tolerance=1e-4]` *number* - positions closer than this (per axis) count as the same point

**Returns** *Float32Array* x, y, z per vertex, unit length

**Complexity** O(vertices) time and space (one hash lookup per vertex)

### `Kit.look.settings(presetId, overrides = {})`

Resolves a preset plus overrides into complete settings.

- `presetId` *string* - a key of PRESETS
- `[overrides]` *Partial\<LookSettings\>* - knob values that differ from the preset

**Returns** *LookSettings & {preset: string, isIndexed: boolean}* `isIndexed`: the view draws tone indices into a target and resolves colour afterwards (needed for screen-space ink and for any resolution below native)

```js
Kit.look.settings('pixel', { toneCount: 5 }).isIndexed // true
```

### Types

- **LightResponse** - How a kind of surface answers the light.
  - `fillGain` *number* - how strongly the fill light follows the surface turning to the viewer
  - `fillBias` *number* - added to that; the fill's light value is `fillGain * (n . fill) + fillBias`, capped at `LIGHTING.fillCap`, and a fragment takes the brighter of key and fill
  - `dither` *number* - share of the look's dither the surface takes, 0..1
- **LookSettings** - Everything that decides how a view draws.
  - `toneCount` *number* - tones per ramp, 2..6 (the band count)
  - `dither` *number* - strength of the ordered dither on the light value, 0 (off) .. 0.3; a frame draws less of it as its texels grow against the figures (`ditherAt`), and none on faces or tightly curved forms
  - `ditherSpace` *'screen'\|'anchored'\|'object'* - what the dither pattern is fixed to: the screen, the figure's snapped screen position, or the surface itself
  - `softness` *number* - half-width of the blend across a band edge, in light units; 0 is a hard edge. Only drawn at native resolution without screen-space ink (tone indices cannot be blended)
  - `outline` *'none'\|'hull'\|'ink'* - no lines, an inverted hull per mesh, or screen-space ink over an index target
  - `inkWidth` *number* - hull line width, CSS pixels (screen-space ink is always one texel)
  - `pixelRows` *number* - internal height in texels; 0 is native resolution
  - `snap` *boolean* - move figures by whole texels when drawn below native resolution
  - `yawSteps` *number* - facings per turn a caller should round a figure's turn to (see stepYaw); 0 is continuous
  - `rim` *boolean* - lift grazing, lit edges one tone
  - `bounce` *boolean* - lift the shadowed far-side silhouette one tone (a back light)
- **LookKnob**
  - `id` *string* - the LookSettings field it edits
  - `label` *string*
  - `type` *'choice'\|'range'\|'toggle'*
  - `[options]` *Array\<{value: \*, label: string}\>* - for a choice
  - `[min]` *number* - for a range
  - `[max]` *number* - for a range
  - `[step]` *number* - for a range

## look-toon.js

> Kit look toon: the materials of the stylised look. A toon material turns n.L into a tone band and looks the
> colour up in the palette texture; a hull material draws the mesh's outline in its ramp's ink colour.
>
> Owns:  the palette texture over a `Kit.palette.atlas`; the GLSL chunks (bands, the lights, ordered dither, accents, palette
>        lookup, hull extrusion, region maps and their boundary lines, stamps) as named strings; `Kit.look.shading`: the uniforms every material of one look
>        shares, `dress(mesh, part)` to put a mesh in the look, and `apply(settings, frame)` to change the look
>        of every dressed mesh at once.
> Does not own: colours (kit/palette.js), screen-space ink and low-resolution targets (look-ink.js), or
>        when anything is drawn (look-view.js).
>
> Why a ShaderMaterial and not a patched MeshToonMaterial: the palette contract needs the fragment's last
> word (a tone index or a table colour, never lit RGB, never tone mapped), and the standard material's
> lighting, fog and tone-mapping chunks would all have to be cut back out through string patches that break
> with the next three.js release. The cost: no scene lights, shadows or fog; the look has its own lights (a key, a
> capped fill and a back light: Kit.look.LIGHTING), fixed in view space.
>
> GLSL here is WebGL1-safe (constant loop bounds, no integer bit operations, no MRT); three.js converts it for
> WebGL2. Skinned and instanced meshes are supported through three.js's own vertex chunks; morph targets are not.
> Needs THREE, so it is exercised in the browser (Kit.play('look')) rather than by unit tests.

### Types

- **LookPart** - What a mesh is, to the look.
  - `ramp` *number* - row of the palette atlas (`block.rows.skin`)
  - `[inkGroup=1]` *number* - 1..255. Screen-space ink draws a line where two groups meet; 0 means the part is never outlined and never drawn over by ink (eyes, glows)
  - `[shiny]` *boolean* - takes the lightest tone where it mirrors the light (hair, metal, gems)
  - `[emissive]` *boolean* - always the lightest tone; its ink group defaults to 0
  - `[tone]` *number* - a fixed position along the ramp, 0 (its deepest colour) .. 1 (lightest), instead of shading; it is drawn with the nearest tone the ramp has (`Kit.palette.columnAt`)
  - `[response='form']` *string* - a key of `Kit.look.RESPONSES`: how the part answers the light. 'face' is for skin that carries features (a head): the fill light keeps what is turned to the viewer at the ramp's own colour, and it is never dithered. Where a region map paints over the mesh (hair on a scalp) and on its raised shells, the part answers as a form again
  - `[responseShare]` *boolean* - the geometry has a float `responseShare` attribute, 0..1 per vertex: how much of `response` the surface takes there, the rest being a form's (one skin that is a head and a body)
  - `[anchor]` *{value: THREE.Vector2}* - from `shading.follow(root)`: the figure this part's dither moves with
  - `[crowdBones]` *THREE.Texture* - an InstancedMesh of one skinned surface: a float texture with a row of bone matrices per instance (4 texels a bone), which bend the geometry's `skinIndex`/`skinWeight` before the instance matrix places it (WebGL2)
  - `[instanceRamp]` *boolean* - an InstancedMesh whose geometry has a float `instanceRamp` attribute: a row offset per instance
  - `[inkClass=0]` *number* - 0..15. Where two classes meet on one surface (a sleeve and the skin below it), a line is drawn on the lower one, even inside one ink group. Parts that all keep the default never get one
  - `[regions]` *LookRegions* - the mesh is painted from a baked region map instead of one ramp
  - `[blockRow]` *number* - with `regions` or `stamps`: the atlas row of the figure's ramp slot 0 (slots are rows from there)
  - `[shell]` *number* - with `regions`: 0 (default) draws the whole mesh; k draws only the texels raised to shell k
  - `[stamps]` *boolean* - the geometry has `stampPaint` and `stampCentre` attributes (kit/figure-shapes.js `stamps`): each vertex names its slot and tone, and it is never outlined
  - `[outlined=true]` *boolean* - false: no hull outline of its own, though it keeps its ink group
- **LookRegions**
  - `texture` *THREE.Texture* - a `RegionMap` (kit/region.js) as RGBA bytes, nearest-filtered, wrapping around
  - `width` *number* - texels around
  - `height` *number* - texels along
  - `end` *number[]* - [x, y, z] the far end of the mesh's long axis in its own space (s is 1 there)
  - `front` *number* - the axis (0 = x, 1 = y, 2 = z) the around-angle is measured from
  - `side` *number* - the axis it grows towards
- **DressedMesh**
  - `mesh` *THREE.Mesh*
  - `toon` *THREE.Material*
  - `hull` *THREE.Material*
  - `setRamp` *function(number): void* - point both materials at another atlas row (O(1))
- **LookFrame** - What `apply` must know about the target being drawn into.
  - `width` *number* - target width, device pixels (or texels)
  - `height` *number* - target height
  - `lineWidth` *number* - hull line width in pixels of that target
  - `[depthRange]` *number[]* - [near, far] view depths packed into the index target's alpha

## look-ink.js

> Kit look ink: the indexed pipeline. A scene of dressed meshes is drawn as (ramp, tone, ink group, depth)
> into a small target, a second pass turns that into colour and adds one-texel ink lines, and a last pass
> copies the result to the screen with whole-number scaling.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.look.inkPass`: the two render targets and their sizes, the resolve shader (palette lookup plus
>        silhouette and inner ink) and the upscale shader.
> Does not own: what writes the index target (the materials in look-toon.js, with LOOK_INDEX_OUTPUT), the
>        settings (look-settings.js), or the frame around it: clearing, cameras, snapping (look-view.js).
>
> Why one RGBA8 target and no depth texture or MRT: ramp, tone and ink group are a byte each, and the view
> depth fits the fourth byte because its range is fitted round the figures. So the pipeline needs nothing
> beyond WebGL1, one colour attachment and five texture reads per texel. What it gives up: normal-based
> crease lines (there is no normal target), and anti-aliasing (indices cannot be averaged).
> Needs THREE and WebGL, so it is exercised in the browser (Kit.play('look')) rather than by unit tests.

### Types

- **InkPass**
  - `resize` *function(number, number, number): void* - (bufferWidth, bufferHeight, scale): size the targets for a screen buffer in device pixels drawn at `scale` pixels per texel
  - `draw` *function(THREE.WebGLRenderer, THREE.Scene, THREE.Camera, Object): void* - index pass, resolve pass, then the upscale onto whatever target is current. Leaves the renderer's clear colour at transparent black
  - `size` *function(): {width: number, height: number, scale: number}* - the targets' size in texels and the scale
  - `readTexels` *function(THREE.WebGLRenderer): Uint8Array* - the resolved colour target as RGBA bytes, bottom row first
  - `dispose` *function(): void*

## look-view.js

> Kit look view: the thin composition that draws a scene of dressed meshes in a chosen look. It joins the
> palette atlas, the shading and the indexed pipeline, and decides per frame which path a look needs.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.look.view`: a scene for the figures, the current settings (preset plus knob overrides), the
>        choice between the direct path (colour straight to the screen, hull outlines, anti-aliased) and the
>        indexed path (look-ink.js), texel snapping and dither anchoring of figures, the depth range fitted
>        round them, and the cheaper fallback for slow devices (`degrade`).
> Does not own: the pieces it composes (kit/palette.js, look-settings.js, look-toon.js, look-ink.js), the camera, animation
>        (a caller rounds a turn with Kit.look.stepYaw), or the background behind the figures.
>
> Colour: the view writes palette bytes straight to the screen, so it must be drawn outside the kit's HDR
> post chain (a game does that by returning `render` from create(); see kit/shell.js).
> Needs THREE and WebGL, so it is exercised in the browser (Kit.play('look')) rather than by unit tests.

### Types

- **LookView**
  - `scene` *THREE.Scene* - add figures here (through `add`); only dressed meshes belong in it
  - `isWebGL2` *boolean* - whether the renderer is WebGL2 (a sculpted crowd needs it)
  - `atlas` *ReturnType\<typeof Kit.palette.atlas\>* - the palette table; `atlas.load(palette)` gives a figure its rows
  - `shading` *Object* - from Kit.look.shading: `shading.dress(mesh, part)`
  - `add` *function(THREE.Object3D): Object* - put a figure's root in the scene; returns its dither anchor (pass as part.anchor)
  - `remove` *function(THREE.Object3D): void* - take a figure's root out of the scene and drop its dither anchor
  - `use` *function(string, Object=): Object* - switch to a preset, with optional knob overrides; returns the settings
  - `set` *function(string, \*): Object* - change one knob; returns the settings
  - `settings` *function(): Object* - the settings in force (after any degrade)
  - `render` *function(THREE.Camera, {clearColor?: number}=): void* - draw one frame onto the current target
  - `degrade` *function(): boolean* - drop to the cheaper variant of the current look; false when there is none
  - `stats` *function(): Object* - `{ path, scale, targets }` of the last frame
  - `readTexels` *function(): {width: number, height: number, data: Uint8Array}\|null* - the indexed path's resolved texels (bottom row first), or null on the direct path
  - `dispose` *function(): void*

## look-panel.js

> Kit look panel: the controls of the stylised look as reusable pieces, so every screen that draws through a
> `Kit.look.view` offers the same preset, knob and palette controls in the same panel: the `look` showcase,
> the `figures` screen and the character creator.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.look.panel`: `lookControls(view)` (signals for the preset and every knob, bound to the view),
>        the components `Choice` (a labelled row of exclusive buttons), `KnobControl` (one knob of
>        `Kit.look.KNOBS`), `LookControls` (preset, its note and every knob) and `Panel` (the scrollable sheet
>        at the bottom in portrait and the side in landscape, with hide and show buttons), its stylesheet, and
>        `freeArea` (the part of the screen the panel leaves for the scene).
> Does not own: the look (kit/look-*.js), what a screen draws, or its extra rows (palettes, edits), which a
>        screen passes in as children.
>
> Needs the DOM and kit/ui, so it is exercised in the browser (`Kit.play('look')`, `Kit.play('figures')`).

### `Kit.look.panel.PANEL_SHARE`

Share of the screen the panel takes: the bottom in portrait, the right side in landscape.

### `Kit.look.panel.TOP_BAR_PIXELS`

Height of the shell's top bar the scene must stay clear of, CSS pixels.

### `Kit.look.panel.Choice({ label, options, value, onPick, idPrefix })`

A labelled row of exclusive buttons. `idPrefix` makes each button addressable by tools ('<prefix>-<value>').

- `props` *{label: string, options: Array\<{value: \*, label: string}\>, value: Object, onPick: function(\*): void, idPrefix: string}* - `value` is a signal

**Returns** *Object* a vnode

### `Kit.look.panel.KnobControl({ knob, value, onChange })`

One editor control for a knob row of `Kit.look.KNOBS`, bound to that knob's signal.

- `props` *{knob: Object, value: Object, onChange: function(\*): void}*

**Returns** *Object* a vnode

### `Kit.look.panel.LookControls({ controls, readout, children })`

The preset choice, its description (plus an optional readout signal) and every knob.

- `props` *{controls: LookControlState, readout?: Object, children?: \*}* - `children` go between the preset and the knobs (palettes)

**Returns** *Object* a vnode

**Complexity** O(presets + knobs)

### `Kit.look.panel.Panel({ isShown, label, children })`

The panel: a scrollable sheet (bottom in portrait, right in landscape) with a hide button, and a show
button while it is hidden.

- `props` *{isShown: Object, label: string, children: \*}* - `isShown` is a boolean signal

**Returns** *Object* a vnode

### `Kit.look.panel.lookControls(view, presetId = 'toon')`

Signals for the preset and every knob, bound to a view: any change is applied to the view at once.

- `view` *Object* - from `Kit.look.view`
- `[presetId='toon']` *string*

**Returns** *LookControlState*

**Complexity** O(knobs) to make, and per preset change or knob read

```js
const controls = Kit.look.panel.lookControls(view); // later: controls.stop()
```

#### `Kit.look.panel.lookControls().choosePreset(id)`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

#### `Kit.look.panel.lookControls().knobValues()`

_Undocumented: add a JSDoc block in the source._

### `Kit.look.panel.freeArea(size, isPanelShown)`

The part of the screen the panel and the top bar leave for the scene, CSS pixels.

- `size` *{w: number, h: number, portrait: boolean}* - the shell's `ctx.size`
- `isPanelShown` *boolean*

**Returns** *{width: number, height: number, centerX: number, centerY: number}* its size, and its centre measured from the top left of the screen

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
const free = Kit.look.panel.freeArea(ctx.size, true);
```

### Types

- **LookControlState**
  - `preset` *Object* - signal of the preset id
  - `knobs` *Object\<string, Object\>* - knob id -> signal of its value
  - `choosePreset` *function(string): void* - switch preset and reset every knob to its value
  - `knobValues` *function(): Object* - knob id -> current value
  - `stop` *function(): void* - unbinds the view (call from the screen's dispose)

## look-show.js

> Kit look show: the showcase screen of the stylised look (`Kit.play('look')`): two stand-in figures built from
> primitives, each with its own palette, slowly turning, with controls for the preset, the palettes and every knob.
>
> Owns:  the `look` screen; the stand-in figures as data (`PARTS`, `FIGURES`); the control panel generated from
>        `Kit.look.KNOBS`; and the measurements a tool can ask for (`Kit.currentGame.measure()`): exact-colour
>        readback, cost per look, shimmer under rotation and motion, skinned and instanced meshes.
> Does not own: the look itself (kit/palette.js, look-settings.js, look-toon.js, look-ink.js, look-view.js) or real characters
>        (the body system builds those; this screen only needs something with a head, limbs and a held prop).
>
> It is registered like a game, so the shell gives it a HUD, a back button and clean-up, and the smoke and
> play tools reach it like any other. It draws its own frame (see `render` in kit/shell.js).

### Types

- **PartRow** - One primitive of a figure.
  - `shape` *string* - a key of SHAPES
  - `size` *number[]* - what that shape reads
  - `at` *number[]* - centre, [x, y, z]
  - `[turn]` *number[]* - rotation, [x, y, z] radians
  - `[stretch]` *number[]* - scale along the part's own axes
  - `ramp` *string* - ramp name in the figure's palette
  - `[group]` *number* - ink group (default 1; 0 is never outlined)
  - `[shiny]` *boolean*
  - `[emissive]` *boolean*
  - `[tone]` *number* - fixed tone 0..1

## figure-shapes.js

> Kit figure shapes: the surface of one bone, as plain arrays. Each body shape primitive (capsule, sphere, box,
> lathe, extrude; kit/body-plan.js `SHAPES`) has one builder from the shape's data to positions, normals and
> triangle indices in the bone's own space, at a chosen level of detail, plus a key that is equal exactly when
> two shapes would build the same surface (so identical shapes share one geometry).
>
> Owns:  `Kit.figure.LEVELS` (segment counts per level of detail), `Kit.figure.ACROSS_AXIS` (the extrude
>        convention), `describeShapes` (a bone of a plan -> its canonical shape descriptors), `shapeKey`,
>        `buildShape` and the builders behind it (`BUILDERS`, including `stamps`: the flat polygons of a face),
>        the three things any built solid may then have done to it (`scale`, `basis`, `inflate`),
>        `smoothProfile`, `triangulate` (ear clipping for extrude and stamp outlines) and `writeBoneMatrix` (a
>        bone's world transform as a column-major 4 x 4 matrix).
> Does not own: the shape data (kit/body-plan.js and a catalogue's plans and parts), which bones get drawn and
>        in what colour (kit/figure-layout.js), or anything THREE (kit/figure.js wraps these arrays in
>        BufferGeometry).
>
> Conventions: the bone's own space of kit/body-plan.js (metres, +Y up, +Z forward, +X left). A shape lives
> in that space, so the bone's three-axis scale (the dials) stretches it with no rebuild. Triangles wind
> counter-clockwise seen from outside; normals are unit length and point out.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-figure.js.

### Types

- **ShapeDescriptor** - Everything that decides a bone's surface, in canonical form (no defaults left to fill in).
  - `kind` *('capsule'\|'sphere'\|'box'\|'lathe'\|'extrude')*
  - `[end]` *number[]* - capsule: [x, y, z] of its far end in bone space (its start is the joint)
  - `[radius]` *number* - capsule: radius at the joint; sphere: radius
  - `[endRadius]` *number* - capsule: radius at the far end (a limb tapers into the next segment)
  - `[size]` *number[]* - box: [x, y, z]
  - `[profile]` *number[][]* - lathe: [[radius, along], ...]
  - `[outline]` *number[][]* - extrude: [[across, along], ...]
  - `[depth]` *number* - extrude: thickness
  - `[axis]` *number* - lathe and extrude: the long axis, 0 = x, 1 = y, 2 = z
  - `[across]` *number* - extrude: the axis the outline's first coordinate lies along
  - `at` *number[]* - [x, y, z] offset in bone space
  - `[scale]` *number[]* - [x, y, z] stretch about `at` (an egg-shaped head, a torso deeper than wide); absent = none
  - `[basis]` *number[][]* - three unit vectors [across, up, out] in bone space: the solid, built and stretched along the bone's axes, is then turned about `at` so its x, y and z lie along them (an ear that leans back, a nose on a tilted face); absent = the bone's own axes
  - `[inflate]` *number* - metres every vertex is pushed out along its normal after all of the above: the shell a raised region is drawn on (cloth over a limb, hair over a scalp); absent = none
  - `[smooth]` *boolean* - lathe: round the authored profile (a curve through its points) instead of joining them with straight runs; `LEVELS[level].smoothSteps` points per run
  - `[layers]` *StampLayer[]* - stamps: flat polygons lying on the bone's surface, drawn in order
- **StampLayer** - One flat polygon of a `stamps` shape: a layer of an eye, a brow, a lip, a freckle.
  - `points` *number[]* - x, y, z per vertex, in bone space, counter-clockwise seen from outside
  - `normal` *number[]* - unit [x, y, z], out of the surface
  - `slot` *number* - which of the figure's ramps paints it
  - `tone` *number* - a fixed tone, 0 (darkest) .. 1 (lightest) of that ramp
  - `[least=0]` *number* - the fewest target texels its smaller side may cover: the look grows a layer that would be smaller (a glint, a pupil) so it survives a low-resolution target
  - `[drop=0]` *number* - leave the layer out while its smaller side covers fewer target texels than this
- **ShapeArrays** - A surface as plain arrays, ready for a BufferGeometry.
  - `positions` *Float32Array* - 3 per vertex, bone space, metres
  - `normals` *Float32Array* - 3 per vertex, unit length, outward
  - `indices` *Uint16Array\|Uint32Array* - 3 per triangle, counter-clockwise from outside
  - `[paint]` *Float32Array* - stamps only, 4 per vertex: ramp slot, tone 0..1, size rule in texels (positive: the least size, grow to it; negative: drop the layer below that size), order of the layer (later layers draw over earlier ones)
  - `[centres]` *Float32Array* - stamps only, 4 per vertex: its layer's centre (x, y, z) and smaller half extent, metres
- **BoneSurface**
  - `shape` *ShapeDescriptor*
  - `child` *number* - -1 for the bone's own (first) surface; else the child bone an extra link reaches

## figure-layout.js

> Kit figure layout: what a renderer draws for one body bake, decided as plain data. A layout lists the
> pieces (one per bone that has a surface, plus any extension pieces), which ramp of the individual paints each
> one, which ink group it belongs to, and what has to change when the bake changes. It also measures the body
> at rest, so a figure can stand on the ground and a camera can be fitted to it.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.figure.layout` (bake -> pieces), `resolveRamps` (bones that name no paint), `inkKeyOf` /
>        `inkGroupOf` / `INK_GROUPS` (which pieces share an outline), `planUpdate` (old layout + new layout ->
>        what to keep, add, remove and recolour), `measureRest` (lowest point and extent of the body at rest,
>        props left out) and `fitView` (a camera window that holds a measured body).
> Does not own: surfaces (kit/figure-shapes.js), THREE objects (kit/figure.js), bakes and what changed in the
>        individual (kit/body-recipe.js, `editor.changes` in kit/body-edit.js), or colours (kit/palette.js).
>
> Two extension points (see `layout`): surface features (eyes, a mouth, a nose) arrive as extra pieces per
> bone, and surface regions (clothing, hair) as a baked map per bone that the look reads per fragment, plus one
> shell piece per raised layer. The body system fills both (`Kit.body.FIGURE_HOOKS`), and they are the default
> for a bake that has a `surface`; anything else with the same shapes can fill them too.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-figure.js.

### Types

- **FigurePiece** - One drawn surface: a mesh in the meshes binding, a run of triangles in a merged one.
  - `id` *string* - stable across rebuilds: the bone's name; '<bone>><child>' for an extra link of a hub bone (a chest to a shoulder); '<bone>#<n>' for an extension piece
  - `bone` *number* - bone index in the layout's plan (changes when the outfit changes; `id` does not)
  - `shape` *Object* - a `ShapeDescriptor` of kit/figure-shapes.js, in the bone's space
  - `key` *string* - `shapeKey(shape, level)`: equal keys share one geometry
  - `ramp` *number* - index into the bake's `ramps` (never -1: see `resolveRamps`)
  - `inkGroup` *number* - 0..255
  - `isProp` *boolean* - the bone belongs to an attached part (left out of grounding and framing)
  - `[shiny]` *boolean* - passed to the look (`LookPart.shiny`)
  - `[emissive]` *boolean* - passed to the look
  - `[tone]` *number* - passed to the look: a fixed tone 0..1
  - `[regions]` *RegionMapOnBone* - the baked regions of the piece's bone: the look paints the piece from it
  - `[shell]` *number* - 0 or absent: the bone's own surface; k: the shell of `regions.shells[k - 1]`, which shows only the texels raised that far (id '<bone>~<k>')
  - `[stamps]` *boolean* - the piece is a `stamps` shape: each vertex carries its paint
  - `[outlined]` *boolean* - false: no hull outline of its own
- **RegionMapOnBone** - EXTENSION POINT (surface regions: clothing and hair as predicates over bone-local coordinates).
  - `key` *string* - equal keys are equal maps (they share one texture)
  - `width` *number* - texels around
  - `height` *number* - texels along
  - `data` *Uint8Array* - a `RegionMap` of kit/region.js: per texel [ramp slot or 255, line-class slot or 255, shell index, flags]
  - `shells` *number[]* - raises in metres, ascending; shell index k in `data` is `shells[k - 1]`
  - `end` *number[]* - [x, y, z] the far end of the bone's long axis in bone space: s runs 0 at the joint to 1 there
  - `front` *number* - the axis (0 = x, 1 = y, 2 = z) the around-angle is measured from
  - `side` *number* - the axis it grows towards
- **FeaturePiece** - EXTENSION POINT (surface features: eyes, a mouth, a nose, horns).
  - `shape` *Object* - a `ShapeDescriptor` in the bone's space (already placed on the surface: its `at`)
  - `[ramp]` *number* - index into the bake's `ramps`; default the bone's own
  - `[glow]` *boolean* - true for eyes and glows: ink group 0, never outlined
  - `[shiny]` *boolean*
  - `[emissive]` *boolean*
  - `[tone]` *number*
  - `[stamps]` *boolean* - the shape is of kind 'stamps' (flat polygons that carry their own paint): it is drawn in one call, never outlined, and hidden where the bone's regions occlude (hair over a brow)
  - `[outlined]` *boolean* - false: the piece gets no hull outline of its own (a nose inside a face)
- **RegionLookup** - A point lookup `regionOf(plan, bone, x, y, z)` -> ramp index (`Kit.body.figureHooks(bake).regionOf`).
- **FigureLayout**
  - `plan` *Object* - the bake's assembled `BodyPlan`
  - `level` *string* - the level of detail of every key
  - `boneRamp` *Int16Array* - per bone: the resolved ramp index (see `resolveRamps`)
  - `boneChannel` *Array\<?string\>* - per bone: the paint channel it is drawn with, inherited like its ramp
  - `pieces` *FigurePiece[]* - in bone order
  - `rampCount` *number* - how many ramps the bake has
- **FigureUpdate**
  - `keep` *Array\<{from: FigurePiece, to: FigurePiece}\>* - pieces drawn as they are; only their bone index may move
  - `add` *FigurePiece[]* - pieces to build (a geometry from the cache or new, two new materials)
  - `remove` *FigurePiece[]* - pieces to take down
  - `recolor` *boolean* - the palette rows must be rewritten (a colour changed)
  - `remeasure` *boolean* - the rest pose changed, so grounding and framing must be measured again
- **RestMeasure**
  - `lowestY` *number* - the lowest point of any body surface, metres (props left out: a sword held low does not lift the body)
  - `highestY` *number* - the highest point of any body surface
  - `centerX` *number* - the middle of the body's footprint across X, so a long body (a hound) can turn about its middle
  - `centerZ` *number* - the middle of the footprint along Z
  - `reach` *number* - how far the body's footprint reaches from that middle (half its diagonal), metres: a figure turning about the middle stays inside it
  - `lowestPropY` *number* - the lowest point of a prop, or Infinity when none is worn
- **ViewWindow** - An orthographic camera's window onto a figure: the world point at its centre and its half extents.
  - `centerX` *number* - world units across the camera (0: the figure's vertical axis)
  - `centerY` *number* - world height of the centre
  - `halfWidth` *number*
  - `halfHeight` *number*

## sculpt.js

> Kit sculpt: smooth surfaces from simple solids. Several solids (capsules, balls, boxes, turned profiles, flat
> outlines) are blended into one distance field, like clay pressed together, and the field's surface is turned
> into one mesh whose every vertex knows which solids it belongs to. A limb then runs into a torso without a
> seam, a layer of cloth is the same surface pushed out and cut along a hem, and a rig can bend the result by
> its weights instead of moving rigid pieces that overlap.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.sculpt.distanceOf` (a shape descriptor of kit/figure-shapes.js -> a signed distance function),
>        `solid` (a descriptor placed by a transform, with its bounds), `sample` (solids -> a `Field`: distances on
>        a grid, blended), `surface` (a field -> positions, normals and triangles by surface nets; a `cut` trims
>        it along any boundary, found exactly), `weights` (vertices -> the up to four owners that move each),
>        `smoothMin` and the `DETAIL` table.
> Does not own: what the solids are (a body's bones, a part), which layers a figure has (kit/figure-sculpt.js),
>        THREE, or drawing.
>
> The field is the contract, and both of its ends can be replaced:
>   Sources. A solid is anything that answers a signed distance at a point (`Solid.distance`), with bounds, a
>     thinness and an owner. The analytic shapes are rows of `KINDS`; so is `grid`, distances stored on a grid
>     (a field sampled earlier, a bake kept between sessions, the output of a learned implicit evaluated once).
>     A new source is a new row of `KINDS` (or a `Solid` made by hand); nothing downstream asks what it was.
>   The field. `sample` blends solids into a `Field`; `valueAt` (distance), `nearestAt` (which solid, so whose
>     paint and whose bones) and `weights` (owners of any points) read it. None of them knows of triangles.
>   Consumers. `surface` (surface nets) is one consumer and the only place a mesh is made. Another (points or
>     splats laid on the zero set, a ray marcher) would read the same three functions and the band.
>
> Conventions: metres; a field's grid is axis-aligned in the space the solids were placed in (a body's rest
> pose). Distances are negative inside. Triangles wind counter-clockwise seen from outside.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-sculpt.js.

### `Kit.sculpt.DETAIL`

Grid cell size by level of detail, metres, and the most cells along the longest side of what is sampled
(`cellFor`): a body twice as tall is meshed with cells twice as large, so its cost and its look on screen
stay those of a small one. A solid thinner than `FEWEST_CELLS_ACROSS` cells cannot be meshed (`isTooThin`);
what to do with it then (kit/figure-sculpt.js leaves it a rigid piece) is the caller's choice, so one
earring never makes a whole body's grid finer.

### `Kit.sculpt.KINDS`

Distance functions by shape kind, each `(shape) -> (x, y, z) -> metres` in the shape's own space, before
its `at`, `scale` and `basis`. A kind listed in `SLOPED` also writes the distance's slope into `SLOPE`.
Adding a kind of solid means adding a row here and a builder in kit/figure-shapes.js.

### `Kit.sculpt.FAR`

"Far outside": the value of a node nothing was measured at, metres (its negative: far inside).

### `Kit.sculpt.invertAffine(matrix)`

Inverts the upper 3 x 4 of a column-major 4 x 4 affine matrix.

- `matrix` *ArrayLike\<number\>* - 16 numbers, column-major

**Returns** *number[]* the inverse's 12 numbers row by row (three rows of x, y, z, translation)

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.sculpt.invertAffine([2, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 0, 1, 0, 0, 1]) // [0.5, 0, 0, -0.5, 0, 0.5, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0.5, 0]
```

### `Kit.sculpt.smoothMin(first, second, radius)`

The smaller of two distances, rounded where they meet: within `radius` of each other the result dips below
both, which fills the crease between two solids with a fillet about that wide.

- `first` *number*
- `second` *number*
- `radius` *number* - metres; 0 is a plain minimum (a crisp crease)

**Returns** *number* never more than `radius / 4` below the smaller

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.sculpt.smoothMin(0.01, 0.01, 0.04) // 0: two surfaces a centimetre away each are joined here
```

### `Kit.sculpt.polygonDistance(points, u, v)`

Distance from (u, v) to a closed polygon, negative inside: the even-odd rule for the sign, nearest edge for the size.

- `points` *ArrayLike\<number\>\|number[][]* - u, v per corner, flat; or [[u, v], ...]
- `u` *number*
- `v` *number*

**Returns** *number*

**Complexity** O(corners), no allocation for a flat array

```js
Kit.sculpt.polygonDistance([[0, 0], [1, 0], [1, 1], [0, 1]], 0.5, 0.5) // -0.5
```

### `Kit.sculpt.sectionTable(section, leastU = -Infinity)`

The distance table of a closed flat section, shared by every shape with the same section (a dial that
stretches a torso keeps its profile, so it keeps its table).

- `section` *number[][]* - [[u, v], ...]
- `leastU` *number* - the table starts no lower than this along u (0 for a profile turned about its axis)

**Returns** *SectionTable*

**Complexity** O(table nodes x corners) the first time a section is seen (about 2000 x 30), O(corners) to look it up

```js
const table = Kit.sculpt.sectionTable([[0, 0], [0.1, 0], [0.1, 0.3], [0, 0.3]], 0);
```

### `Kit.sculpt.distanceOf(shape)`

The signed distance function of a shape descriptor (kit/figure-shapes.js `ShapeDescriptor`), honouring its
`at`, `scale`, `basis` and `inflate`.

- `shape` *Object* - a `ShapeDescriptor`

**Returns** *function(number, number, number): number* metres, negative inside, in the descriptor's (bone) space

**Complexity** O(1) to make (O(table) the first time a lathe's or an extrude's section is seen); O(1) per call

```js
Kit.sculpt.distanceOf({ kind: 'sphere', radius: 0.1, at: [0, 0.2, 0] })(0, 0.2, 0) // -0.1
```

### `Kit.sculpt.solid(shape, matrix, { owner = 0, blend = 0, positions } = {})`

Places a shape in the field's space.

- `shape` *Object* - a `ShapeDescriptor`
- `matrix` *Float32Array\|number[]* - column-major 4 x 4 from the shape's space to the field's (a bone's rest transform, `Kit.figure.writeBoneMatrix`)
- `[options]` *{owner?: number, blend?: number, positions?: Float32Array}* - `positions`: points of the shape's surface in its own space (its built mesh), for the bounds; without them the shape is built at low detail

**Returns** *Solid*

**Complexity** O(surface points) for the bounds

```js
const forearm = Kit.sculpt.solid({ kind: 'capsule', end: [0, -0.25, 0], radius: 0.04, endRadius: 0.03, at: [0, 0, 0] }, boneMatrix, { owner: 7 });
```

### `Kit.sculpt.cellFor(solids, detail = 'high')`

The grid cell for a set of solids at a level of detail: the level's own, or larger when the solids' longest
side would otherwise span more than the level's `most` cells.

- `solids` *Solid[]*
- `[detail='high']` *string* - a key of `DETAIL`

**Returns** *number* metres

**Complexity** O(solids)

```js
Kit.sculpt.cellFor(bodySolids, 'high') // 0.011 for a body up to 1.32 m on its longest side
```

### `Kit.sculpt.isTooThin(part, cell)`

Is a solid too thin to be meshed on a grid of this cell (it could fall between the samples)?

- `part` *Solid*
- `cell` *number* - metres (`cellFor`)

**Returns** *boolean*

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.sculpt.isTooThin(earring, Kit.sculpt.cellFor(solids)) // true: draw it as a rigid piece instead
```

### `Kit.sculpt.sample(solids, options)`

`sampleSteps` run to its end in one go (see it for the method).

- `solids` *Solid[]*
- `[options]` *{detail?: string, cell?: number, margin?: number}* - as `sampleSteps`

**Returns** *Field*

**Complexity** as `sampleSteps`

```js
const field = Kit.sculpt.sample(bodySolids, { detail: 'medium', margin: 0.03 });
```

### `Kit.sculpt.valueAt(field, x, y, z)`

The field's value at any point, by blending the eight nodes round it.

- `field` *Field*
- `x` *number*
- `y` *number*
- `z` *number*

**Returns** *number* metres; meaningless (a blend with `FAR`) further than the field's band from its surfaces

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.sculpt.valueAt(field, 0, 1.2, 0) < 0 // inside the chest
```

### `Kit.sculpt.slopeAt(field, x, y, z, out)`

The slope of the field at a point: that of the blend of the eight nodes round it (`valueAt`), so it reads
no node `valueAt` would not. Not unit length.

- `field` *Field*
- `x` *number*
- `y` *number*
- `z` *number*
- `out` *number[]\|Float64Array* - receives [x, y, z], metres per metre

**Returns** *number[]\|Float64Array* `out`

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.sculpt.slopeAt(field, 0.2, 1.2, 0, [0, 0, 0]) // about [1, 0, 0] on the right side of a chest
```

### `Kit.sculpt.nearestAt(field, x, y, z)`

Which solid is nearest a point. A node knows its nearest solid; where the nodes round a point disagree (it
is near the border between two solids), the solids they name are measured at the point itself, so the
border is as smooth as the solids and not stepped like the grid.

- `field` *Field*
- `x` *number*
- `y` *number*
- `z` *number*

**Returns** *number* index into `field.solids`, or -1 where no node round the point was measured

**Complexity** O(1): eight lookups, and up to eight distance evaluations near a border

```js
const bone = field.solids[Kit.sculpt.nearestAt(field, x, y, z)].owner;
```

### `Kit.sculpt.surface(field, { offset = 0, cut = null, near = null, depth = 0.002, walls = true } = {})`

The surface of a field as a mesh, by surface nets: one vertex in every grid cell the surface passes
through, placed at the average of where the surface crosses the cell's edges and then moved onto the
field's true surface, and one quad across every grid edge it crosses. The mesh is closed, evenly
tessellated and smooth where the field is.

`offset` pushes the surface out (a layer of cloth over a body is the body's field at a positive offset, at
most the field's `margin`). `cut(x, y, z)` keeps only the part of the solid where it returns true. The
cut's edge is found by bisection along each grid edge, and a vertex where the cut meets the surface (a hem,
a hairline) is placed on the cut itself, so an edge is as crisp as the predicate, not as coarse as the
grid. A cut solid is a shell: it reaches `depth` below the field's own surface and is hollow beneath, and
faces wholly deeper than that are dropped (a layer does not carry what is buried in the body it lies on).
`walls: false` also drops the faces a cut makes through the thickness, leaving an open sheet that lies on
the surface (paint, not cloth).

- `field` *Field*
- `[options]` *{offset?: number, cut?: function(number, number, number): boolean, near?: ArrayLike\<number\>, depth?: number, walls?: boolean}* - `depth`: metres (default 0.002), used only with a `cut`; `near`: per solid of the field, non-zero when the cut can hold at points nearest that solid (a sleeve's cut holds only near the arm's solids): the cut is then not asked anywhere else, which is most of a body

**Returns** *SurfaceArrays*

**Complexity** O(nodes of the field's band) time (a `cut` is called once per band node within the shell, and 7 times per grid edge it crosses); O(surface cells) memory for the mesh

```js
const skin = Kit.sculpt.surface(field); const sleeve = Kit.sculpt.surface(field, { offset: 0.008, cut: isSleeve });
```

### `Kit.sculpt.weights(positions, solids, { spread = 0.03, reach = 0, sharpness = 2 } = {})`

Which owners move each vertex, and how much. A vertex belongs fully to the solid it lies on; near another
solid (within `spread` of being as close) it is shared, so the surface bends smoothly across a joint.

- `positions` *Float32Array* - 3 per vertex, in the field's space
- `solids` *Solid[]*
- `[options]` *{spread?: number, reach?: number, sharpness?: number}* - `spread`: metres over which ownership fades from one solid to the next (default 0.03); `reach`: how far off the solids the vertices lie (a layer's offset), metres; `sharpness`: the power a solid's nearness is raised to (default 2): higher keeps a vertex with its nearest solid for longer across a wide `spread` (cloth right over a knee follows that knee, cloth between two is shared)

**Returns** *SkinArrays*

**Complexity** O(vertices x solids whose bounds are near)

```js
const { owners, weights } = Kit.sculpt.weights(skin.positions, field.solids);
```

### Types

- **SectionTable** - A flat section's distance, measured once on a 2D grid.
  - `values` *Float32Array* - distance at each node, u fastest
