  - `sizeU` *number* - nodes along u
  - `sizeV` *number*
  - `fromU` *number* - u of node 0
  - `fromV` *number*
  - `step` *number* - metres between nodes
  - `corners` *Float64Array* - the section, flat (for points off the table)
- **Solid**
  - `owner` *number* - who moves it (a bone index): becomes the vertices' skin
  - `distance` *function(number, number, number): number* - signed distance in the field's space
  - `min` *number[]* - [x, y, z] of its bounds in the field's space
  - `max` *number[]*
  - `thinness` *number* - its smallest half-thickness, metres (see `isTooThin`)
  - `blend` *number* - how wide a fillet it makes where it meets other solids, metres
  - `[group=0]` *number* - solids of one group blend with each other everywhere (the segments of an arm)
  - `[anchor]` *number[]* - [x, y, z]: where the solid's group joins the groups sampled before it (a shoulder). The join's fillet fades to nothing `joinReach` away from here, so an arm hanging beside a torso stays a separate arm instead of melting into the ribs. Without it the group joins everywhere
  - `[join]` *number* - the fillet where the solid's group joins the rest, metres (default: the group's widest `blend`)
  - `[joinReach]` *number* - metres from `anchor` over which the join stays full (default: 2.5 x `join`)
- **Field**
  - `values` *Float32Array* - distance at each node, x fastest, then y, then z. Only nodes near a surface are measured (`band`); the rest hold `FAR` outside the solids and `-FAR` deep inside them
  - `nearest` *Int16Array* - index (into the solids sampled) of the solid nearest each measured node, else -1
  - `band` *Int32Array* - the measured nodes
  - `margin` *number* - the furthest a surface may be pushed out of this field (`surface`'s `offset`), metres
  - `size` *number[]* - nodes along [x, y, z]
  - `origin` *number[]* - [x, y, z] of node (0, 0, 0)
  - `cell` *number* - metres between nodes
  - `solids` *Solid[]* - what was sampled
- **SurfaceArrays**
  - `positions` *Float32Array* - 3 per vertex, in the field's space
  - `normals` *Float32Array* - 3 per vertex, unit length, outward
  - `indices` *Uint16Array\|Uint32Array* - 3 per triangle
- **SkinArrays**
  - `owners` *Uint16Array* - 4 per vertex: owner ids (bone indices), strongest first; unused slots are 0
  - `weights` *Float32Array* - 4 per vertex: their shares, summing to 1

## figure-sculpt.js

> Kit figure sculpt: a body bake as a few smooth, skinned surfaces instead of one rigid solid per bone. The
> body's own solids are blended into one skin per paint (kit/sculpt.js), so limbs run into the torso and a
> head into its neck; every painted region (a sleeve, trousers, hair) becomes a layer of its own, the same
> surface pushed out and cut along the region's edge, so cloth has thickness and a hem instead of being a
> colour on the skin. Each layer's vertices carry bone weights, so one mesh bends with the rig.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.figure.sculptLayers` (a bake and its layout -> the layers as plain arrays, and which layout
>        pieces they replace), `SCULPT` (its tunables) and `restMatrices` (a bake's rest pose as bone matrices).
> Does not own: the blending and meshing (kit/sculpt.js), what pieces a bake has (kit/figure-layout.js), the
>        regions' predicates (kit/region.js, kit/body-surface.js), or THREE (kit/figure.js binds the arrays
>        to skinned meshes).
>
> What is sculpted: the solids of the body itself and of its solid features (a nose, an ear). What stays rigid:
> attached parts (a sword, a hat: thin and hard-edged, which a grid would blur) and flat stamps (a face).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-sculpt.js.

### Types

- **Limb**
  - `lead` *number* - the limb's first bone (the one whose parent belongs to something else)
  - `bones` *number[]* - the bones further along it
  - `direction` *number[]* - unit [x, y, z]: where it points at rest, from its joint
  - `outward` *number[]* - unit [x, y, z]: away from the trunk
  - `offset` *number* - metres its joint is to the side of the root
  - `key` *string* - its ink key ('arms-left')
- **SculptLayer**
  - `id` *string* - 'skin:<ramp>' for a body surface, 'region:<part>|<ramp>|<raise>' for a painted layer, 'worn:<socket>|<ramp>|<wear>' for a worn object
  - `ramp` *number* - index into the bake's ramps
  - `inkGroup` *number*
  - `inkClass` *number* - which line class it draws as (its ramp)
  - `positions` *Float32Array* - 3 per vertex, in the bake's sculpting pose (`matrices`)
  - `normals` *Float32Array*
  - `indices` *Uint16Array\|Uint32Array*
  - `owners` *Uint16Array* - 4 bone indices per vertex
  - `weights` *Float32Array* - 4 shares per vertex
  - `[faceShare]` *Float32Array* - on a body surface: per vertex, how much of it the head's bones move (0..1); a look lights a face and a form differently
- **Sculpted**
  - `layers` *SculptLayer[]* - shared between equal bakes: never write to their arrays
  - `replaced` *Set\<string\>* - ids of the layout pieces the layers stand in for (do not draw them)
  - `matrices` *Float32Array* - the bone matrices the layers were made in (the bind pose), 16 per bone
  - `clearance` *{leads: Int16Array, turns: Float32Array}* - see `limbClearance`
  - `signature` *string* - what the layers depend on (see `sculptSignature`)
- **Garment** - The regions of one worn part with one paint and one raise (its waist, chest and sleeves are separate regions, one per bone): one layer.
  - `key` *string* - '<part>|<ramp>|<raise>'
  - `part` *?string*
  - `ramp` *number*
  - `raise` *number* - metres; 0 is paint
  - `order` *number* - its place in the paint order (the highest of its regions')
  - `tests` *Map\<number, Function[]\>* - per bone, the predicates of its regions there

## figure.js

> Kit figure: a body bake plus a pose, drawn in 3D through Kit.look. It is the renderer between the body
> system (data) and the look pipeline (shading): one mesh per piece of kit/figure-layout.js, its geometry from
> kit/figure-shapes.js shared through a cache, its material from `view.shading.dress`, its colour from the
> figure's rows of the view's palette atlas, and its matrix written each frame from the pose's world transforms.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.figure.geometryCache` (BufferGeometry per shape key, counted and shared), `Kit.figure.regionTextures`
>        (one texture per baked region map, counted and shared), `Kit.figure.build`
>        (bake -> a figure in a view) and what it returns: `local` (the pose buffer a caller writes), `pose()`
>        (one forward pass and the matrix writes, no allocation), `setBake` (incremental update from
>        `editor.changes`), `measured` / `frame` (grounding and camera fitting) and `dispose`.
> Does not own: what pieces exist (kit/figure-layout.js), surfaces (kit/figure-shapes.js), shading, outlines
>        and colour (kit/look-*.js, kit/palette.js), kinematics (kit/rig.js, kit/reach.js) or motion over time.
>
> Why one mesh per piece (DECISIONS.md, "Kit.figure: one mesh per piece"): measured against one skinned mesh
> per paint region and against instancing across figures that share a bake; the per-piece mesh is exact
> (normals under a dial's non-uniform scale, bounds, culling), makes every incremental update a keyed diff,
> and costs draw calls that matter only past a few dozen figures, where `Kit.figure.crowd` is the answer.
>
> Needs THREE, so it is exercised in the browser (`Kit.play('figures')`); its decisions are pure and tested.

### Types

- **GeometryCache**
  - `acquire` *function(Object): THREE.BufferGeometry* - the geometry of a piece (`{ shape, key }`), built on the first request
  - `release` *function(string): void* - one user fewer; the geometry is disposed when none is left
  - `positions` *function(Object): Float32Array* - a piece's bone-space positions (builds and caches them without a geometry)
  - `stats` *function(): {created: number, alive: number}* - geometries ever created, and alive now
  - `dispose` *function(): void* - free every geometry
- **RegionTextureCache**
  - `acquire` *function(Object): Object* - the `LookRegions` (kit/look-toon.js) of a `RegionMapOnBone`, its texture made on the first request
  - `release` *function(string): void* - one user fewer; the texture is disposed when none is left
  - `stats` *function(): {created: number, alive: number}*
  - `dispose` *function(): void*
- **Figure**
  - `root` *THREE.Group* - a direct child of `view.scene`; move and turn it to place the figure
  - `bake` *Object* - the bake drawn now
  - `layout` *Object* - the `FigureLayout` drawn now
  - `local` *Object* - the pose buffer (a `JointTransforms` of kit/rig.js, in `bake.plan` bone order): write it, then call `pose()`. Reset to the rest pose with `rest()`. Replaced by `setBake` when the plan changes.
  - `world` *Object* - the world transforms of the last `pose()`, in figure space
  - `measured` *Object* - the `RestMeasure` of the body (kit/figure-layout.js), in figure space after grounding
  - `rest` *function(): void* - copies the bake's rest pose into `local`
  - `pose` *function(): void* - forward kinematics over `local` and a matrix write per mesh; no allocation
  - `setBake` *function(Object, Object=, {settle?: boolean}=): Object* - draws another bake with the fewest changes; returns the plan carried out. A colour change never sculpts. `{ settle: false }` says the edit is still moving (a dial being dragged): the sculpted surfaces are bent to the new proportions by the skeleton at once, and sculpted again by `settle()`
  - `settle` *function(number=): boolean* - sculpts the surfaces for the bake shown if they were only bent to it (or, built with `sculpt: 'steps'`, not sculpted yet); true when it did. With a number it works for about that many milliseconds and returns false until the work, spread over as many calls as it takes, is done
  - `isSettled` *function(): boolean* - false while the surfaces wait for `settle()`
  - `frame` *function(number): Object* - a `ViewWindow` (kit/figure-layout.js) for a screen area of this aspect
  - `counters` *function(): {meshes: number, skinned: number, materialsCreated: number, geometriesCreated: number}* - `meshes`: draw objects in all, of which `skinned` are sculpted surfaces
  - `dispose` *function(): void*
- **Crowd**
  - `root` *THREE.Group* - a direct child of `view.scene`
  - `count` *number*
  - `locals` *Object[]* - one pose buffer per member (write it, then `pose`)
  - `places` *Float32Array* - 4 per member: x, y, z, heading (radians); written by the caller
  - `cost` *{triangles: number, meshes: number, isSculpted: boolean}* - triangles per member, instanced meshes for the whole crowd
  - `pose` *function(): void* - forward kinematics, an instance matrix per member per rigid piece and a bone texture row per member; no allocation
  - `dispose` *function(): void*

## figure-show.js

> Kit figure show: the `figures` screen (`Kit.play('figures')`): the sample bodies of kit/body-samples.js (a
> human, a winged variant, a hound, a carp and a sword on its own) drawn in 3D by Kit.figure through Kit.look,
> slowly turning, with the shared look controls, the human's colour choices, a row of live edits that show
> incremental updates (a dial, a colour, a hat, a sword), and a debug pose that raises an arm by Kit.reach.
>
> Owns:  the screen: the samples as rows (`SAMPLES`), the edits as rows (`EDITS`), the stage layout and the
>        cost probe a tool can run (`Kit.currentGame.measure()`): draw calls and milliseconds per frame for
>        1, 20 and 200 figures under three bindings (a mesh per piece, a skinned mesh per paint region, and
>        instancing across figures that share a bake).
> Does not own: anything it shows (kit/body-*.js, kit/figure*.js, kit/look-*.js) or the look's controls
>        (kit/look-panel.js).
>
> Registered like a game, so the shell gives it a HUD, a back button and clean-up, and the smoke and play
> tools reach it like any other. It draws its own frame (see `render` in kit/shell.js).

## motion.js

> Kit motion: movement as small stages that compose. A stage is a pair of functions of the same shape,
> `(motion, plan, dt)`: `step` advances what must be simulated on the fixed clock (phases, footfalls), `pose`
> writes this frame's pose from that state. Combinators turn stages into bigger stages, so a new body or a new
> behaviour is a new composition of the same parts, not new code. A motion state is one instance's movement:
> where it is, how fast it is going, and the pose buffers it owns.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.motion.stage` and the combinators `sequence`, `blend`, `layer`, `when`, `byDetail`; `refusal` /
>        `requireFit` (can this plan run this stage, and if not, what is missing); `state` (a per-instance
>        motion state), `advance` (one frame: fixed steps, then the pose, then the world transforms), `place`
>        (teleport), `advanceStore` (every instance of a `Kit.body.store`); the two stages every kind of
>        movement shares, `lean` (tilt into acceleration and turns) and `trail` (tails and other loose chains
>        follow by simulation); and `course` / `drive` (a body steered along a table of speeds and turns).
> Does not own: how legs walk (kit/gait.js) or bodies swim (kit/swim.js), the fixed clock (`Kit.dynamics.stepsDue`),
>        kinematics (kit/rig.js, kit/reach.js), plans (kit/body-plan.js), where an instance should go (the
>        caller moves it; this reads the movement), or drawing.
>
> Conventions (kit/body-plan.js): metres, seconds, radians; +Y up; heading 0 faces +Z and grows towards +X, the
> body's left. "Body frame" means left, up, forward.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-gait.js.

### `Kit.motion.GRAVITY`

Metres per second squared. Leans, strides and tails are sized against it.

### `Kit.motion.SMOOTHING`

How quickly the measured facts of a movement settle, per second. The caller moves the body once a frame, so
the raw speed is a staircase; these rates turn it into something a stride and a lean can follow.

### `Kit.motion.LEAN_DEFAULTS`

Tunables of `lean`. Angles in radians.

### `Kit.motion.TRAIL_DEFAULTS`

Tunables of `trail`.

### `Kit.motion.COURSE_DEFAULTS`

Tunables of `course`.

### `Kit.motion.stage(parts)`

Makes a stage from its parts, or passes one through. Every combinator calls this on what it is given, so
a plain `(motion, plan, dt) => {}` function can be used anywhere a stage can.

- `parts` *Stage\|Function\|Object* - a stage, a function (motion, plan, dt), or { name?, step?, pose?, needs?, detailLevels? }

**Returns** *Stage* frozen

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
const nod = Kit.motion.stage({ name: 'nod', pose: (motion, plan) => Kit.rig.turn(motion.pose.rotation, motion.pose.rotation, head, 0, 0.2) });
```

### `Kit.motion.sequence(...parts)`

Stages run one after another: every `step` in order on the fixed clock, every `pose` in order each frame.
Later stages see what earlier ones wrote, so order is meaning: lean before the legs are solved, tails last.

- `parts` *...(Stage\|Function)*

**Returns** *Stage* needs everything its parts need

**Complexity** O(parts) per call on top of the parts' own cost; no allocation

```js
const walk = Kit.motion.sequence(Kit.motion.lean(), Kit.gait.arms(), Kit.gait.plant(), Kit.motion.trail());
```

### `Kit.motion.blend(weight, part)`

A stage whose pose is mixed over what was there before it by a weight: 0 leaves the pose alone, 1 is the
stage outright. Its `step` always runs, so the stage is warm when its weight comes up. Mixing happens on
joint rotations, so a mixed walk no longer holds its feet exactly on their plants: blend to change
movement, not as a movement.

- `weight` *number\|function(MotionState, Object): number* - 0..1, or a function giving it each frame
- `part` *Stage\|Function*

**Returns** *Stage*

**Complexity** O(joints) per frame when the weight is strictly between 0 and 1 (one copy, one mix); the first such frame of an instance allocates one pose buffer, none after

```js
const wade = Kit.motion.sequence(walk, Kit.motion.blend((motion) => motion.memory.water.depth, swim));
```

### `Kit.motion.layer(roles, part)`

A stage confined to part of the body: it runs in full, then every bone that carries none of the given
roles is put back as it was. This is how an upper-body movement is laid over a walk.

- `roles` *string[]* - role names (`Kit.body.ROLES`); a bone carrying ANY of them keeps the stage's changes
- `part` *Stage\|Function*

**Returns** *Stage*

**Complexity** O(joints) per frame (one copy, one pass); the first frame of an instance allocates one pose buffer

```js
const wave = Kit.motion.layer(['arm', 'hand'], greeting);   // legs keep walking
```

### `Kit.motion.when(condition, part, otherwise)`

A stage that runs only where it can, or only while something holds. Given a requirement (from
`Kit.body.requirement`, or a spec for one), the question is asked of the plan once and remembered: this is
capability gating, and it is how one controller serves bodies of different shapes. Given a function, it is
asked every time. A gated stage's needs are no longer needs of the whole.

- `condition` *Object\|function(MotionState, Object): boolean* - a requirement, a requirement spec ({ needs, atLeast }), or a predicate (motion, plan)
- `part` *Stage\|Function* - runs when the condition holds
- `[otherwise]` *Stage\|Function* - runs when it does not

**Returns** *Stage*

**Complexity** O(1) per call beyond the chosen stage (a requirement costs one map lookup per plan)

```js
const move = Kit.motion.when({ atLeast: { legs: 2 } }, Kit.gait.walk(), Kit.swim.swim());   // legs walk, the rest swim
```

### `Kit.motion.byDetail(levels)`

One stage per level of detail: `levels[motion.detail]` runs, and a detail beyond the list runs nothing at
all (`advance` then skips the pose and the world transforms too, so a far instance costs a few additions).

- `levels` *Array\<Stage\|Function\>* - the full stage first, cheaper ones after

**Returns** *Stage* needs what its first level needs

**Complexity** O(1) per call beyond the chosen stage

```js
const walk = Kit.motion.byDetail([fullWalk, swingOnly]);   // detail 2 and up: not evaluated
```

### `Kit.motion.refusal(part, plan, rest)`

Why a plan cannot run a stage, in words, or null when it can: the capability gate with a named refusal.

- `part` *Stage\|Function*
- `plan` *Object* - a `BodyPlan`
- `[rest]` *Object* - its proportioned rest pose, for needs that measure the body (default: the plan's own)

**Returns** *?string* e.g. 'the walk gait cannot move the plan "fish": it lacks legs. This plan has: tail, spine, ...'

**Complexity** O(needs x capabilities); call it at setup, not per frame

```js
Kit.motion.refusal(Kit.gait.walk(), fishPlan) // 'the walk gait cannot move the plan "fish": it lacks legs. ...'
```

### `Kit.motion.requireFit(part, plan, rest)`

Throws unless the plan can run the stage.

- `part` *Stage\|Function*
- `plan` *Object* - a `BodyPlan`
- `[rest]` *Object* - its proportioned rest pose

**Returns** *Object* the plan

**Complexity** as `refusal`

```js
Kit.motion.requireFit(Kit.gait.walk(), bake.plan, bake.rest);
```

### `Kit.motion.state(body, controller, { x = 0, y = 0, z = 0, heading = 0, detail = 0 } = {})`

Makes one instance's motion state. It owns the instance's pose and world buffers (a bake is shared between
instances; a pose cannot be), so a renderer reads `motion.world` and nothing else needs allocating.

- `body` *{plan: Object, rest?: Object}* - a bake of kit/body-recipe.js, or any plan with an optional proportioned rest pose (default: the plan's own rest pose)
- `controller` *Stage\|Function* - the stage to run, usually a composition
- `[placement]` *{x?: number, y?: number, z?: number, heading?: number, detail?: number}* - where it starts (default the origin, facing +Z, full detail)

**Returns** *MotionState*

**Complexity** O(bones) time and space, once per instance: two transform sets (88 bytes a bone) and a mass per bone

```js
const motion = Kit.motion.state(bake, Kit.gait.walk(), { x: 2, z: 5 });
Kit.motion.advance(motion, dt, x, 0, z, heading);     // every frame, with where the game moved it to
Kit.body.debug.draw(context, motion.plan, motion.world, view);
```

### `Kit.motion.memory(motion, key, create)`

A stage's own record on a motion state, made the first time it is asked for. Stages keep their per-instance
state here (springs, planted feet, chains), so a state carries exactly what its controller uses.

- `motion` *MotionState*
- `key` *string* - the stage's name for its record
- `create` *function(MotionState): Object* - makes the record; called once per state

**Returns** *Object*

**Complexity** O(1) after the first call; the first call allocates whatever `create` allocates

```js
const springs = Kit.motion.memory(motion, 'lean', () => ({ pitch: Kit.dynamics.spring(), roll: Kit.dynamics.spring() }));
```

### `Kit.motion.refreshWorld(motion)`

Brings `motion.world` up to date with `motion.pose` if a stage has changed the pose since. Stages that read
world positions call this first; `advance` calls it last.

- `motion` *MotionState*

**Returns** *Object* `motion.world`

**Complexity** O(bones) when the world was stale, O(1) otherwise; no allocation

```js
const hipHeight = Kit.motion.refreshWorld(motion).translation[hip * 3 + 1];
```

### `Kit.motion.place(motion, x, y, z, heading = motion.heading)`

Puts a body somewhere without it having travelled there: speeds are cleared and `epoch` moves on, so stages
that hold world positions (planted feet, trailing chains) start again from the new place.

- `motion` *MotionState*
- `x` *number* - world metres
- `y` *number* - the ground height there
- `z` *number*
- `[heading=motion.heading]` *number* - radians

**Returns** *MotionState* `motion`

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.motion.place(motion, spawn.x, 0, spawn.z, spawn.heading);
```

### `Kit.motion.advance(motion, dt, x = motion.x, y = motion.y, z = motion.z, heading = motion.heading)`

One frame of an instance. The caller says where the body is now; the movement between the last call and
this one is replayed in fixed steps (`Kit.dynamics.stepsDue`), so the controller's `step` sees the same
positions whether the game runs at 20 or 60 frames a second. Then the controller's `pose` writes the pose
from the rest pose, and the world transforms are brought up to date.

- `motion` *MotionState*
- `dt` *number* - seconds since the last call, any frame rate
- `[x=motion.x]` *number* - where the body stands now, world metres
- `[y=motion.y]` *number* - the ground height under it
- `[z=motion.z]` *number*
- `[heading=motion.heading]` *number* - radians

**Returns** *MotionState* `motion`; read `motion.world` (and `motion.pose`) after

**Complexity** O(steps x step cost + pose cost + bones) time, at most 12 steps; no allocation after an instance's first frames

```js
Kit.motion.advance(motion, dt, player.x, groundHeight, player.z, player.heading);
```

### `Kit.motion.advanceStore(store, states, dt)`

Advances every live instance of a body store: reads each slot's position, heading and detail columns,
advances its motion state, and writes the phase column back.

- `store` *Object* - a `Kit.body.store`
- `states` *Array\<?MotionState\>* - slot -> its motion state (slots without one are skipped)
- `dt` *number* - seconds since the last call

**Returns** *void*

**Complexity** O(live slots x the cost of `advance`); no allocation

```js
states[slot] = Kit.motion.state(bakes[crowd.recipe[slot]], controller, { x, z });
Kit.motion.advanceStore(crowd, states, dt);      // after the game moved the columns
```

### `Kit.motion.carrier(plan)`

The bone that carries the body: the child of the root bone with the most bones under it (a pelvis, a
quadruped's hips, a fish's trunk). Movement shifts and tilts this one bone and everything rides on it.

- `plan` *Object* - a `BodyPlan`

**Returns** *number* bone index; the root itself when it has no children

**Complexity** O(bones) the first time for a plan, O(1) after

```js
const pelvis = Kit.motion.carrier(bipedPlan);
```

### `Kit.motion.lean(options = {})`

A stage that tilts the body's carrier bone into what it is doing: forwards when it speeds up, back when it
brakes, and into a turn, by a share of the angle that would balance it exactly. Each angle rides a spring,
so the tilt has weight. Walkers lean and swimmers bank with the same stage.

- `[options]` *{pitchShare?: number, pitchPerSpeed?: number, rollShare?: number, mostPitch?: number, mostRoll?: number, stiffness?: number, pitchWeight?: function(MotionState, Object): number}* - see `Kit.motion.LEAN_DEFAULTS`; a share of 0 switches that tilt off. `pitchWeight` (motion, plan) scales the forward tilt per body: an upright body nods from its hips, a long level one must not

**Returns** *Stage*

**Complexity** O(1) per frame plus two springs' fixed steps; allocates two springs on an instance's first frame

```js
const bank = Kit.motion.lean({ pitchShare: 0, rollShare: 1.2 });   // a fish rolls into turns and does not nod
```

### `Kit.motion.trail(options = {})`

A stage that lets loose limbs follow the body by simulation: each chain of bones carrying the given roles
(a tail by default) is shadowed by a `Kit.dynamics` chain of points dragged from its base, and the bones
are aimed along the points with `Kit.reach.follow`. Put it last, after whatever moves the body.

- `[options]` *{roles?: string[], gravity?: number[], drag?: number, stiffnessScale?: number}* - which limbs (`roles`, default ['tail']; a chain is a run of bones carrying ALL of them), and how they behave: see `Kit.motion.TRAIL_DEFAULTS`. How strongly a chain keeps its rest direction follows the `stiffness` hint of its base bone in the plan.

**Returns** *Stage* needs nothing: a body without such limbs is left alone

**Complexity** O(chain points x iterations x fixed steps) per frame; allocates the chains on an instance's first frame

```js
const wings = Kit.motion.trail({ roles: ['wing'], gravity: [0, -2, 0] });
```

### `Kit.motion.course(legs, options = {})`

Makes a course: a looping table of legs, each a speed and a turn rate held for some seconds, with the body's
speed and turn easing from one to the next. It is the smallest thing that starts, stops and turns, for
demos, benchmarks and tests of movement; a game steers its own bodies.

- `legs` *CourseLeg[]*
- `[options]` *{acceleration?: number, turnAcceleration?: number, x?: number, z?: number, heading?: number}* - see `Kit.motion.COURSE_DEFAULTS`

**Returns** *Course*

**Complexity** O(legs)

```js
const course = Kit.motion.course([{ seconds: 3, speed: 1.4 }, { seconds: 2, speed: 1.4, turn: 0.8 }, { seconds: 1.5, speed: 0 }]);
Kit.motion.drive(course, dt);
Kit.motion.advance(motion, dt, course.x, 0, course.z, course.heading);
```

### `Kit.motion.drive(route, dt)`

Advances a course by `dt` seconds on the fixed clock, so it traces the same line at any frame rate.

- `route` *Course*
- `dt` *number* - seconds since the last call

**Returns** *Course* `route`

**Complexity** O(dt / FIXED_STEP) time, at most 12 steps; no allocation

```js
Kit.motion.drive(course, dt);
```

### `Kit.motion.shortestTurn(from, to)`

The signed smallest turn from one heading to another, radians in (-pi, pi].

### Types

- **StageNeed** - One thing a stage needs from a plan before it can run on it.
  - `consumer` *string* - who is asking, for the refusal ('the walk gait')
  - `[requirement]` *Object* - capabilities, from `Kit.body.requirement`
  - `[check]` *function(Object, Object): ?string* - (plan, rest pose) -> what is wrong, or null; for needs a capability mask cannot state ("every leg has three bones")
- **Stage**
  - `name` *string* - what it is, for refusals and debugging
  - `step` *?function(MotionState, Object, number): void* - (motion, plan, dt) on the fixed clock: `dt` is always `Kit.dynamics.FIXED_STEP`. Null when the stage simulates nothing.
  - `pose` *?function(MotionState, Object, number): void* - (motion, plan, dt) once per `advance`: writes `motion.pose`; `dt` is the frame's. Null when the stage only simulates.
  - `needs` *ReadonlyArray\<StageNeed\>*
  - `detailLevels` *number* - how many levels of detail it evaluates; at `motion.detail` equal or above, `advance` runs neither its pose nor the world transforms (Infinity unless made by `byDetail`)
- **MotionState** - One instance's movement.
  - `plan` *Object* - the `BodyPlan`
  - `rest` *Object* - the proportioned rest pose every frame starts from (shared with the bake; never written)
  - `pose` *Object* - the local pose this frame (`JointTransforms`), owned by this state
  - `world` *Object* - the world transforms this frame, owned by this state
  - `isWorldCurrent` *boolean* - false after a stage changed `pose` without refreshing `world`
  - `controller` *Stage* - the stage `advance` runs
  - `x` *number* - where the body stands, world metres
  - `y` *number* - the height of the ground under it
  - `z` *number*
  - `heading` *number* - radians about +Y
  - `velocityX` *number* - smoothed, metres per second
  - `velocityZ` *number*
  - `speed` *number* - metres per second, never negative
  - `forwardSpeed` *number* - the part of the velocity along the heading (negative when backing up)
  - `acceleration` *number* - change of `forwardSpeed`, smoothed, metres per second squared
  - `turnRate` *number* - smoothed, radians per second, positive towards the body's left
  - `time` *number* - seconds simulated
  - `phase` *number* - 0..1, where the body is in its movement cycle; the stage that runs the cycle owns it
  - `detail` *number* - level of detail, 0 = full (copy `store.detail[slot]` here, or use `advanceStore`)
  - `epoch` *number* - counts teleports; a stage that keeps world positions starts again when it changes
  - `masses` *Float32Array* - one per bone, kilograms
  - `totalMass` *number* - kilograms
  - `memory` *Object\<string, Object\>* - per-stage records, made on first use (`Kit.motion.memory`)
  - `carry` *number* - seconds not yet simulated (owned by `advance`)
  - `pending` *number* - seconds since the last fixed step ran (owned by `advance`)
  - `spares` *Object[]* - pose buffers lent to `blend` and `layer` (owned by them)
  - `sparesUsed` *number*
- **CourseLeg**
  - `seconds` *number* - how long the leg lasts
  - `speed` *number* - metres per second to settle at (0 stands still)
  - `[turn=0]` *number* - radians per second to settle at, positive towards the left
- **Course** - Plain state; read `x`, `z`, `heading` after `drive`.
  - `legs` *ReadonlyArray\<CourseLeg\>*
  - `x` *number* - world metres
  - `z` *number*
  - `heading` *number* - radians
  - `speed` *number* - metres per second now
  - `turn` *number* - radians per second now
  - `leg` *number* - index of the leg being travelled
  - `legTime` *number* - seconds into that leg
  - `acceleration` *number* - how fast speed changes, metres per second squared
  - `turnAcceleration` *number* - how fast the turn rate changes, radians per second squared
  - `carry` *number* - seconds not yet simulated (owned by `drive`)

## gait.js

> Kit gait: walking, trotting and running for a body with any number of legs, as `Kit.motion` stages. Nothing
> here is an animation: a clock turns speed and leg length into a stride, a table says when each leg is on the
> ground, feet are planted in the world and stay there until they lift, legs are solved by inverse kinematics,
> and the body rides on the legs that carry it.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.gait.GAITS` (walk, trot, run: duty factor and per-leg phase offsets, with rows for two and four
>        legs and a rule for any other count), `strideLength` and `froude` (the physics the clock runs on),
>        `legs` (what a plan's legs are, measured from its data), the stages `plant` (full detail: planted
>        feet, inverse kinematics, a pelvis held where the legs can reach, a sprung bob and a shift over the
>        support), `swing` (level of detail 1: the same clock driving joint angles, no solving) and `arms`
>        (arms swing against the legs), the ready composition `walk`, and `feet` / `support` (read where the
>        feet are and whether the centre of mass is over them).
> Does not own: the stage machinery, lean, trailing chains or the fixed clock (kit/motion.js, kit/dynamics.js),
>        the solvers (kit/reach.js), plans and which way a knee bends (kit/body-plan.js), where the body goes
>        (the caller moves it; a gait reads the movement), uneven ground (the ground is flat at `motion.y`),
>        or swimming (kit/swim.js).
>
> Conventions (kit/motion.js): metres, seconds, radians; heading 0 faces +Z and grows towards +X, the body's
> left; "body frame" is left, up, forward. A leg's phase runs 0..1: on the ground for the first `duty` of it.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-gait.js.

### `Kit.gait.STRIDE`

Stride length from speed, by dynamic similarity: animals of any size moving at the same Froude number
(speed squared over gravity times leg length) move alike, and across walkers and runners the stride is
about `factor` x leg length x Froude^`exponent` (R. McNeill Alexander's fit). So a longer leg takes a longer,
slower stride at the same speed, with no table per body.

### `Kit.gait.GAITS`

The gaits, slowest first. A body uses the first gait whose `fastestFroude` its Froude number does not
exceed (people break into a run near 0.5, horses into a gallop near 2.5). `duty` is the fraction of a
stride each foot spends on the ground; `offsets` are where in the stride each leg starts its stance, in
the order left legs hind to front, then right legs hind to front. `rows` are written out for two and four
legs (the gallop cannot be made by a rule); any other body uses `generic`, whose `rule` is 'sequence'
(legs take turns in that order, evenly spaced: a wave) or 'alternate' (neighbours in opposite halves of the
stride: a trot, an insect's tripod). `clearance` is how high a swinging foot lifts, in leg lengths.

### `Kit.gait.CLOCK`

The clock. Rates are per second; Froude numbers are dimensionless.

### `Kit.gait.SWING`

A swing lasts between these fractions of the leg's pendulum period (2 pi sqrt(leg length / g)), whatever the stride asks.

### `Kit.gait.STANCE`

Standing and reaching, in leg lengths unless noted.

### `Kit.gait.SWING_ONLY`

Level of detail 1: joint angles from the clock. Radians unless noted.

### `Kit.gait.ARMS`

Arms. Radians unless noted.

### `Kit.gait.froude(speed, legLength)`

The Froude number of a body moving on legs: speed squared over gravity times leg length. Two bodies at the
same number move alike whatever their size, which is what lets one gait table serve every body.

- `speed` *number* - metres per second
- `legLength` *number* - metres, hip to ankle

**Returns** *number*

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.gait.froude(1.4, 0.87) // 0.23: a person walking
```

### `Kit.gait.strideLength(speed, legLength)`

How far a body travels in one full stride (until the same foot lands again) at a speed.

- `speed` *number* - metres per second
- `legLength` *number* - metres, hip to ankle

**Returns** *number* metres; 0 at a standstill

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.gait.strideLength(1.4, 0.87) // 1.29; with legs a fifth longer, 1.47
```

### `Kit.gait.footOffset(legPhase, duty)`

Where a foot is, fore and aft of its hip, through a stride: +1 as it lands in front, falling evenly to -1
as the body passes over it, then swung smoothly back to +1.

- `legPhase` *number* - 0..1
- `duty` *number* - the fraction of the stride spent on the ground

**Returns** *number* -1..1

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
Kit.gait.footOffset(0, 0.6) // 1: just landed, out in front
```

### `Kit.gait.legs(plan, rest)`

Measures a plan's legs (and arms) for walking: which bones they are, how long, where the hips and feet sit,
which way each knee bends (from its limits, by `Kit.body.bendDirection`; a joint with no preferred side
bends forwards, or backwards when the leg is in front of the body's middle, as elbows do), and when each
leg steps in each gait. Remembered per rest pose, so every instance of a bake shares one.

- `plan` *Object* - a `BodyPlan`
- `rest` *Object* - its proportioned rest pose (a bake's `rest`); dials change every length here

**Returns** *LegRig* frozen

**Complexity** O(bones x legs) time once per rest pose, O(1) after; O(legs) space

```js
Kit.gait.legs(bake.plan, bake.rest).legLength // 0.87 for the sample biped
```

### `Kit.gait.plant()`

The stage that walks at full detail. On the fixed clock: the gait clock, and for each leg the lift, the
swing towards where the hip will be half a stance after touchdown, and the plant. Each frame: the body is
set a little below a straight-legged stand (riding a spring that every landing kicks, shifted towards the
feet that are down), lowered as far as the most stretched leg needs, and every leg is solved to its foot
by `Kit.reach.twoBone` (`Kit.reach.chain` for a leg of more than three bones). A planted foot does not
move: at the end of a stance the foot pitches about its toe, so the ankle rises and the toe stays put.

**Returns** *Object* a `Stage` of kit/motion.js; needs two or more legs of three or more bones

**Complexity** per fixed step O(legs); per frame O(bones) for one forward pass plus O(legs) solves; no allocation after an instance's first frame

```js
const legsOnly = Kit.motion.sequence(Kit.motion.lean(), Kit.gait.plant());
```

### `Kit.gait.swing()`

The stage that walks at level of detail 1: the same clock as `plant`, but each hip and knee is simply
turned through the stride. No feet are planted and nothing is solved, so feet slide a little and the cost
is a few turns and one forward pass. For bodies too far away for that to show.

**Returns** *Object* a `Stage`; needs what `plant` needs

**Complexity** per fixed step O(1); per frame O(legs); no allocation after an instance's first frame

```js
const cheapWalk = Kit.motion.sequence(Kit.gait.swing(), Kit.gait.arms());
```

### `Kit.gait.arms()`

The stage that swings arms against the legs: each arm goes forward with the nearest leg on the other side,
by an angle that grows with the stride, and its elbow folds more the faster the body goes. It reads the
clock that `plant` or `swing` runs, so put it in a sequence with one of them; a body without arms is left alone.

**Returns** *Object* a `Stage`; needs nothing

**Complexity** O(arms) per frame, no allocation

```js
const walk = Kit.motion.sequence(Kit.gait.arms(), Kit.gait.plant());
```

### `Kit.gait.walk()`

Walking, ready made: lean, arms, planted feet and trailing tails and wings at full detail; clock-driven
swing at level of detail 1; nothing beyond. The same stage walks any plan with two or more legs.

**Returns** *Object* a `Stage`

**Complexity** see `plant` (detail 0) and `swing` (detail 1)

```js
const motion = Kit.motion.state(bake, Kit.gait.walk());
Kit.motion.advance(motion, dt, x, 0, z, heading);
```

### `Kit.gait.feet(motion)`

An instance's feet: which are planted and where. Null until the `plant` stage has run for it.

- `motion` *Object* - a `MotionState`

**Returns** *?FeetState*

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
const feet = Kit.gait.feet(motion); if (feet && feet.planted[0]) markFootprint(feet.footX[0], feet.footZ[0]);
```

### `Kit.gait.support(motion, out)`

Measures balance: the centre of mass of the posed body against the span of its planted feet, along and
across the heading. A walker's centre of mass leaves the span while it vaults over one foot; how far is
the honest measure of how dynamic the gait is.

- `motion` *Object* - a `MotionState` that has been advanced
- `out` *Support* - receives the measurement

**Returns** *Support* `out`

**Complexity** O(bones + legs) time, no allocation

```js
const balance = Kit.gait.support(motion, { planted: 0, centerX: 0, centerY: 0, centerZ: 0, forwardOutside: 0, sideOutside: 0 });
```

### Types

- **LegRig** - A plan's legs and arms, measured once from the plan and a proportioned rest pose.
  - `count` *number* - legs
  - `upper` *Int16Array* - per leg: the bone at the body (hip)
  - `middle` *Int16Array* - per leg: its child (knee)
  - `end` *Int16Array* - per leg: the last bone of the leg, whose joint is the ankle
  - `longChains` *Array\<?Int16Array\>* - per leg: every joint from hip to ankle when the leg has more than three bones (solved as a chain), else null (solved as two bones)
  - `length` *Float32Array* - per leg: hip to ankle along the bones, metres
  - `hipX` *Float32Array* - per leg: the hip in the body frame at rest (left, up, forward), metres
  - `hipY` *Float32Array*
  - `hipZ` *Float32Array*
  - `homeX` *Float32Array* - per leg: where the ankle rests in the body frame (left, forward)
  - `homeZ` *Float32Array*
  - `sole` *Float32Array* - per leg: how high the ankle is above the lowest point of its foot
  - `toe` *Float32Array* - 3 per leg: from the ankle to the end of the foot bone in the body frame at rest; zeros for a foot with no extent (it cannot roll onto its toe)
  - `footRotation` *Float32Array* - 4 per leg: the foot bone's rotation in the body frame at rest
  - `bendX` *Float32Array* - per leg: the way the knee points, in the body frame (left, forward), unit
  - `bendZ` *Float32Array*
  - `flexSign` *Int8Array* - per leg: the sign of the knee's own X turn that folds it
  - `side` *Uint8Array* - per leg: 0 left, 1 on the centre line, 2 right
  - `row` *Uint8Array* - per leg: 0 for the hindmost row of hips, counting forwards
  - `legLength` *number* - the mean leg length, metres: what the clock scales by
  - `pendulumPeriod` *number* - seconds: 2 pi sqrt(legLength / g)
  - `standLift` *number* - how far the carrier bone is raised from the rest pose so the lowest sole rests on the ground (dials that lengthen legs push the rest pose's feet below the root)
  - `upright` *number* - 1 for a body whose hips are all in one row (it can lean from them), falling to 0 as the rows of hips spread a leg length apart (a level trunk on legs at both ends must not nod)
  - `carrier` *number* - the bone the body is carried by (`Kit.motion.carrier`)
  - `stretchX` *number* - how much the carrier's parent stretches offsets, left and up (for writing a world shift into the local pose)
  - `stretchY` *number*
  - `gaits` *Array\<{duty: number, offsets: Float32Array, clearance: number, fastestFroude: number}\>* - one per `GAITS` row, in order: this body's duty factor and per-leg offsets
  - `armCount` *number*
  - `armUpper` *Int16Array* - per arm: the bone at the shoulder
  - `armElbow` *Int16Array* - per arm: its child, or -1
  - `elbowSign` *Int8Array* - per arm: the sign of the elbow's own X turn that folds it
  - `armLeg` *Int16Array* - per arm: the leg it swings with (the nearest on the other side)
  - `armShift` *Float32Array* - per arm: phase added to that leg's (0.5 when no leg is on the other side)
- **FeetState** - Where an instance's feet are (full detail only).
  - `planted` *Uint8Array* - 1 while the foot is on the ground
  - `footX` *Float32Array* - where the ankle stands (planted) or last stood, world metres
  - `footZ` *Float32Array*
  - `footHeading` *Float32Array* - the way the planted foot points, radians
  - `targetX` *Float32Array* - where the ankle is asked to be this frame (in the air while swinging, raised over the toe at the end of a stance), world metres
  - `targetY` *Float32Array*
  - `targetZ` *Float32Array*
  - `pitch` *Float32Array* - how far the foot is pitched about its toe, radians
  - `swing` *Float32Array* - 0..1 through the swing, for a foot in the air
  - `plantCount` *Uint32Array* - how many times the foot has landed
  - `bob` *Object* - the spring the body's height rides on (`Kit.dynamics` spring)
  - `shift` *Object* - the spring the body's sideways shift rides on
- **GaitClock** - An instance's gait, kept in `motion.memory.gait`.
  - `rig` *LegRig*
  - `gait` *number* - index into `Kit.gait.GAIT_NAMES`
  - `froude` *number*
  - `duty` *number* - the fraction of a stride each foot is down (eased between gaits)
  - `stride` *number* - metres per stride at this speed
  - `cadence` *number* - strides per second
  - `halfStance` *number* - metres a planted foot travels each side of its hip
  - `isMoving` *boolean* - false while standing
  - `offsets` *Float32Array* - per leg: where in the stride it starts its stance
  - `feet` *?FeetState* - made by the `plant` stage; null at lower detail
- **Support**
  - `planted` *number* - how many feet are down
  - `centerX` *number* - the body's centre of mass, world metres
  - `centerY` *number*
  - `centerZ` *number*
  - `forwardOutside` *number* - how far the centre of mass lies ahead of the foremost planted foot or behind the hindmost, along the heading, metres; 0 when it is between them
  - `sideOutside` *number* - the same across the heading

## swim.js

> Kit swim: movement for bodies that are a chain - a fish's spine and tail, a snake - as `Kit.motion` stages.
> A wave of bending travels down the chain from front to back; how fast it beats and how wide it swings
> follow the body's speed by the two relations real swimmers keep to, so nothing is keyed by hand.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.swim.STYLES` (carangiform: a fish, the bend growing towards the tail; serpentine: a limbless
>        body on the ground, the same bend all along), `axis` (which bones are the chain, measured from the
>        plan), the stages `wave` (the travelling wave, bent into turns) and `fins` (paired fins paddle), and
>        the ready composition `swim` (bank into turns, wave, fins).
> Does not own: the stage machinery, banking (`Kit.motion.lean`) or the fixed clock (kit/motion.js), legs
>        (kit/gait.js), plans (kit/body-plan.js), water (there is none: buoyancy, drag and thrust are the
>        caller's; a swimmer here is moved, and its body answers), or flight.
>
> Conventions (kit/motion.js): metres, seconds, radians; a chain's joints bend about their own up axis (+Y), so
> the chain's bones must not be rolled at rest.
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-gait.js.

### `Kit.swim.STYLES`

Swimming styles, name -> row. Lengths are in body lengths (the chain's), speeds in body lengths per second.
  restFrequency, frequencyPerSpeed: beats per second = restFrequency + frequencyPerSpeed x speed. For fish
    this is Bainbridge's line (speed = (3 x frequency - 4) / 4 body lengths a second). A snake's wave must
    travel back along its body as fast as the body goes forward, so its line starts at 0 with a slope of
    one over its wavelength.
  strouhal: beat frequency x tail swing (peak to peak) / speed. Efficient swimmers of every size cruise
    near 0.3, which is what sets how wide the tail swings at a speed.
  idleAmplitude, mostAmplitude: the tail tip's swing each side of the middle is held between these.
  waves: how many wavelengths lie along the body at once.
  envelope: how the bend is shared out: a joint's share is (its distance along the body)^envelope, so 0 bends
    every joint alike and higher values keep the front still and whip the tail.
  recoil: the front of the body counter-swings by this share of the tail's angle.
  turnBend: radians the whole chain curls into a turn, per radian per second of turning; mostBend caps it.
  amplitudeRate: per second, how fast the swing follows a change of speed.

### `Kit.swim.FINS`

Paired fins paddle: radians of sweep, beats per body beat, and the share of the sweep kept when the body is still.

### `Kit.swim.FEWEST_JOINTS`

The fewest joints a chain needs to carry a wave, per style.

### `Kit.swim.axis(plan, rest)`

Finds and measures a plan's swimming chain: the longest run of spine and tail bones, front to back, and
its paired fins. Remembered per rest pose, so every instance of a bake shares one.

- `plan` *Object* - a `BodyPlan`
- `rest` *Object* - its proportioned rest pose (a bake's `rest`)

**Returns** *AxisRig* frozen; `joints` is empty for a plan with no spine or tail

**Complexity** O(bones) time once per rest pose, O(1) after

```js
Kit.swim.axis(carp.plan, carp.rest).joints.length // 5: trunk, rear and three tail bones
```

### `Kit.swim.wave(styleName = 'carangiform')`

The stage that swims: a wave of bending travels down the body's chain. On the fixed clock, the beat
frequency follows the speed and the tail's swing follows from the Strouhal number (see `STYLES`), eased so
a change of speed does not snap. Each frame, every joint of the chain is turned about its up axis by its
share of the wave, the front counter-swings, and the whole chain curls into a turn.

- `[styleName='carangiform']` *string* - a `STYLES` name

**Returns** *Object* a `Stage` of kit/motion.js; a carangiform wave needs a tail of two or more bones and a chain of three or more joints, a serpentine wave a chain of four or more

**Complexity** per fixed step O(1); per frame O(chain joints); no allocation after an instance's first frame

```js
const slither = Kit.motion.sequence(Kit.swim.wave('serpentine'));
```

### `Kit.swim.fins()`

The stage that paddles paired fins (bones carrying `fin` and `left` or `right`): each sweeps about the
body's long axis, the two sides in mirror image, in time with the body's wave. It reads the clock `wave`
runs, so put it after one; a body without paired fins is left alone.

**Returns** *Object* a `Stage`; needs nothing

**Complexity** O(fins) per frame, no allocation

```js
const swim = Kit.motion.sequence(Kit.swim.wave(), Kit.swim.fins());
```

### `Kit.swim.swim(styleName = 'carangiform')`

Swimming, ready made: bank into turns, the travelling wave and paddling fins at full detail; the wave alone
at level of detail 1; nothing beyond.

- `[styleName='carangiform']` *string* - a `STYLES` name

**Returns** *Object* a `Stage`

**Complexity** see `wave`

```js
const motion = Kit.motion.state(carpBake, Kit.swim.swim());
```

### Types

- **AxisRig** - A plan's swimming chain, measured once from the plan and a proportioned rest pose.
  - `joints` *Int16Array* - the chain from front to back, each the parent of the next
  - `along` *Float32Array* - per joint: distance from the first joint along the chain, metres
  - `length` *number* - the whole chain, including the last bone's own extent, metres
  - `fins` *Int16Array* - the paired fin bones
  - `finSide` *Int8Array* - per fin: +1 on the left, -1 on the right
- **SwimState** - An instance's swimming, kept in `motion.memory.swim`.
  - `rig` *AxisRig*
  - `frequency` *number* - beats per second now
  - `amplitude` *number* - the tail tip's swing each side of the middle now, metres

## gait-gallery.js

> Kit gait gallery: the `gaits` screen (`Kit.play('gaits')`). Four bodies made by the body system - a biped, a
> winged biped, a quadruped and a fish - each steered along its own course of starting, speeding up, turning
> and stopping, all moved by ONE controller (`when` the plan has legs, walk; otherwise swim). The camera
> follows and turns with each body, so a foot that stays on its footprint is a foot that does not slide.
>
> Owns:  the screen: its layout, the courses, the camera, and the marks (footprints, a ring under each planted
>        foot, the centre of mass and where it falls on the ground), with one button to show or hide the marks
>        and one to look at a single body.
> Does not own: anything it shows. Bodies are kit/body-samples.js; movement is kit/motion.js, kit/gait.js and
>        kit/swim.js; drawing is `Kit.body.debug`. It is a debug view, not a renderer.
>
> It is registered like a game, so the shell gives it a HUD, a back button and clean-up, and the smoke and
> play tools reach it like any other. Its `debugState` reports the furthest any planted foot has slid, measured
> in the page.

## body-sheet.js

> Kit body sheet: the `wardrobe` screen (`Kit.play('wardrobe')`). Characters rolled from the wardrobe catalogue,
> drawn in 3D by the figure renderer (`Kit.figure.build`) through the toon look, facing the camera, in a grid;
> the first is the base person at its default dials. Faces come in through the renderer's `featuresOf` extension
> point (`Kit.body.figureHooks`). Tap to roll more. It exists to judge the catalogue by eye; it is not the
> character creator.
>
> Owns:  the screen: which characters it shows, the grid and the camera.
> Does not own: the catalogue (kit/body-wardrobe.js), baking (kit/body-recipe.js), the face adapter
>        (kit/body-surface.js) or drawing (kit/figure*.js, kit/look-*.js).
>
> Registered like a game (after the bodies and figures screens), so the shell gives it a HUD, a back button and
> clean-up, and the smoke and play tools reach it like any other. It draws its own frame (`render`).

## body-portrait.js

> Kit body portrait: the `portrait` screen (`Kit.play('portrait')`), the evidence sheet for how wardrobe
> characters look. It shows the default person (`Kit.body.wardrobe.DEFAULT_PERSON`) and twelve seeded rolls
> across the species, in three framings: `body` (everyone, head to toe, in a grid), `head` (head and shoulders,
> a few to a page, with the camera really zoomed in rather than the figures scaled) and `compare` (the `look`
> showcase's hand-built stand-ins beside the default person and one roll, in the same view and preset). The
> look panel switches preset and knobs.
>
> Owns:  the screen: its cast, the three framings, the camera, and the numbers a tool can ask for
>        (`Kit.currentGame.measure()`: draw calls, triangles and milliseconds per frame).
> Does not own: the catalogue (kit/body-wardrobe.js), baking (kit/body-recipe.js), regions and faces
>        (kit/body-surface.js), drawing (kit/figure*.js, kit/look-*.js) or the stand-ins (kit/look-show.js).
>
> Registered like a game, so the shell gives it a HUD, a back button and clean-up, and the play tool reaches it:
>   node tools/dev.js play portrait --size 1400x800 "log (Kit.currentGame.apply({framing:'head',preset:'pixel'}),1)" "wait 1" "shot head-pixel"

### `Kit.body.portrait.FRAMINGS`

The framings, world units. `columns`: characters per row (0: as many whole cells as the screen is wide, so
a close-up fills a phone held upright with one face and a wide window with seven); `rows`: rows per page (0:
as many as the cast needs); `cellWidth` / `cellHeight`: the window each character gets; `top`: how far above the crown of the
head the window starts (room for a hat); `anchor`: what sits at a fixed height in every cell ('feet' or 'crown').

### `Kit.body.portrait.castRecipes(catalog)`

The characters of the sheet as recipes: the default person with every dial at its default, then the rolls.

- `catalog` *Object* - from `Kit.body.catalog`

**Returns** *Object[]* recipes

**Complexity** O(dials + rolls)

```js
const recipes = castRecipes(Kit.body.catalog(Kit.body.wardrobe.CATALOG));
```

### `Kit.body.portrait.slotPlace(framing, slot, count)`

Which page a slot is on and where in the page's grid, for a framing.

- `framing` *{columns: number, rows: number}* - a row of `FRAMINGS` with its columns settled (`columnsFor`)
- `slot` *number* - index among the things shown in this framing
- `count` *number* - how many things the framing shows

**Returns** *{page: number, column: number, row: number, pages: number, rows: number}*

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
slotPlace({ columns: 5, rows: 1 }, 7, 13) // { page: 1, column: 2, row: 0, pages: 3, rows: 1 }
```

### `Kit.body.portrait.columnsFor(framing, aspect)`

How many columns a framing gets in a screen area: its own count, or (for 0) as many whole cells as fit
across when one row of cells fills the area's height.

- `framing` *{columns: number, cellWidth: number, cellHeight: number}* - a row of `FRAMINGS`
- `aspect` *number* - width / height of the area

**Returns** *number* at least 1

**Complexity** O(1)

```js
columnsFor(FRAMINGS.head, 2) // 2
```

## body-proof.js

> Kit body proof: coverage sheets for judging the body system by eye. One polished character proves nothing, so
> a sheet is a grid of many cells, each one figure of some recipe, seen from some direction, at some moment of
> some movement. Suites generate the grids that matter: every species turned through eight directions, every
> option of every socket, every dial at both ends, seeded rolls, and every movement in eight phases.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.body.proof.SUITES` and `suite(name, catalog)` (pure: a catalogue -> the cells of a sheet), `ACTS`
>        (the movements a cell can show, open for other modules to add to), `CATALOGS` (the catalogues a sheet
>        can be made of) and the `proof` workbench screen that draws a sheet (`Kit.currentGame.show(job)`).
> Does not own: the catalogues, baking, drawing or movement themselves; taking the pictures (tools/dev/proof.js).
>
> Used by `node tools/dev.js proof <suite> [--catalog wardrobe] [--preset toon]`, which writes shots/proof/*.png.
> The suites are pure and unit-tested by tools/test-kit.js; the screen needs THREE.

### `Kit.body.proof.DIRECTIONS`

Directions of a turnaround, in turns of a full circle; 0 faces the camera and the figure turns to its left.

### `Kit.body.proof.THREE_VIEWS`

The three directions that between them show a front, a side and a back.

### `Kit.body.proof.SUITES`

The suites. Each is `(catalog, editor, options) -> ProofSheet`; `options.species` narrows a suite to one species.
Adding a suite here adds it to the tool.

### `Kit.body.proof.ACTS`

The movements a cell can show, by id. Open: a module that adds a movement adds a row (`Kit.body.proof.ACTS.wave = ...`),
and the `acts` suite shows it for every species it fits. `rest` is the rest pose and needs no stage.

### `Kit.body.proof.CATALOGS`

The catalogues a sheet can be made of, by name; each value makes the raw catalogue data.

### `Kit.body.proof.suite(name, catalog, options = {})`

The cells of a suite for a catalogue.

- `name` *string* - a key of `SUITES`
- `catalog` *Object* - from `Kit.body.catalog`
- `[options]` *{species?: string[], count?: number, acts?: string[]}* - `species`: which (default all)

**Returns** *ProofSheet*

**Complexity** O(cells); the options and extremes suites call `editor.schema` once per species

```js
Kit.body.proof.suite('turn', catalog).cells.length // 8 per species
```

### `Kit.body.proof.perform(figure, actId, phase)`

Poses a figure at a phase of an act by simulating it from a standing start, so the same cell is the same
picture every time.

- `figure` *Object* - from `Kit.figure.build`
- `actId` *string* - a key of `ACTS`
- `phase` *number* - 0..1

**Returns** *?string* why the body cannot do it (the stage's refusal), or null when posed

**Complexity** O(seconds simulated / SIMULATION_STEP) motion steps

```js
Kit.body.proof.perform(figure, 'walk', 0.25)
```

### Types

- **ProofCell**
  - `label` *string*
  - `recipe` *Object*
  - `yaw` *number* - turns of a full circle
  - `[act='rest']` *string* - a key of `ACTS`
  - `[phase=0]` *number* - 0..1 through the act's cycle
- **ProofSheet**
  - `title` *string*
  - `columns` *number* - cells per row
  - `framing` *('body'\|'head')*
  - `cells` *ProofCell[]*
- **ProofAct** - A movement a cell can show.
  - `stage` *function(): Object* - makes the motion stage (kit/motion.js)
  - `speed` *function(Object): number* - metres per second for a bake (0: on the spot)
  - `cycle` *function(Object, number): number* - seconds of one cycle for a bake at that speed

## creator-rows.js

> Kit creator rows: turns what `Kit.body.editor(...).schema(recipe)` says is editable into the rows a character
> creator shows, so no control is ever written per body part: a dial, a colour channel or a socket that the
> catalogue gains next month appears in its group with no change here.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.rows`: `groupsOf` (schema -> tab groups, each with its ordered rows), `valuesOf` (the
>        current value of every field by key), `structureKey` (a string that changes only when the set of rows
>        or their shape changes, so a view re-renders on that and merely updates values otherwise), and the small
>        pure helpers a row's view needs (`dialReadout`, `isAtDefault`, `swatchGradient`).
> Does not own: the schema (kit/body-edit.js), any element (kit/creator-controls.js draws these rows) or what an
>        edit does (kit/creator-session.js).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-creator.js.

### Types

- **ControlRow** - One thing a person can change, ready to draw.
  - `kind` *('dial'\|'color'\|'socket')*
  - `id` *string* - stable and unique within the schema: a field key, or `dial:<base>` for a sided pair
  - `lockKeys` *string[]* - the field keys a lock on this row protects from a randomise
  - `valueKeys` *string[]* - the field keys whose current values the row shows
  - `label` *string*
  - `hint` *?string* - a sentence for a tooltip
  - `icon` *?string* - an icon name from the catalogue
  - `group` *string*
  - `order` *number*
  - `depth` *number* - how deeply a socket is nested under another (0 for everything else)
  - `[sliders]` *Array\<DialSlider\>* - dial rows: one slider while linked or unsided, two while unlinked
  - `[isSided]` *boolean* - dial rows: is this a left / right pair?
  - `[isLinked]` *boolean* - dial rows: do both sides move together?
  - `[base]` *string* - dial rows of a sided pair: the shared id (what `editor.link` takes)
  - `[ramps]` *Object[]* - colour rows: the ramps of the channel
  - `[channel]` *string* - colour rows: the channel id
  - `[choices]` *Array\<{id: ?string, label: string, icon: ?string, hint: ?string}\>* - socket rows: what fits, None first when allowed
  - `[path]` *string* - socket rows: the socket path
- **DialSlider**
  - `setId` *string* - what `editor.setDial` takes: the dial id, or the base id for a linked pair
  - `valueKey` *string* - the field key whose value this slider shows
  - `caption` *string* - 'Left' / 'Right' while a pair is unlinked, else ''
  - `min` *number*
  - `max` *number*
  - `step` *number*
  - `default` *number*
- **RowGroup**
  - `id` *string*
  - `label` *string*
  - `icon` *?string*
  - `hint` *?string*
  - `rows` *ControlRow[]*

## creator-session.js

> Kit creator session: the state and every editing action of a character creator, with no interface in it.
> One session holds the recipe being edited, its undo history, the controls the person has locked, the sided
> dials they moved one side at a time, the character's name and the recipe the session started from (what a
> "compare" shows).
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.session(options)` and what it returns; `Kit.creator.parseShareCode` / `shareCode` (a
>        recipe as a short code and back, with every failure explained in plain words); `Kit.creator.switchSpecies`
>        (change body type keeping what still fits); `Kit.creator.suggestName`.
> Does not own: what an edit means (kit/body-edit.js: every change goes through the editor, so a recipe is always
>        one the catalogue accepts), how a recipe is stored or shared on the wire (kit/body-recipe.js,
>        kit/pack.js), history itself (`Kit.history` in kit/space.js) or any element (kit/creator-controls.js).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-creator.js. Time and randomness are
> passed in, so a test controls both.

### Types

- **CreatorSession**
  - `recipe` *function(): Object* - the recipe being edited
  - `baseline` *function(): Object* - the recipe the session started from, or the last one loaded
  - `schema` *function(): Object* - `editor.schema` of the recipe (kept until the recipe changes)
  - `groups` *function(): Object[]* - `Kit.creator.rows.groupsOf` of the schema, honouring unlinked pairs
  - `setDial` *function(string, number): void* - set a dial by its set id (a sided pair's base moves both sides)
  - `setColor` *function(string, number): void* - set a colour channel to a ramp index
  - `setSocket` *function(string, ?string): void* - put a part in a socket, or null to empty it
  - `resetDial` *function(Object): void* - put one slider (`DialSlider`) back to its default
  - `toggleLock` *function(string[]): void* - lock the keys if any is open, else open them all
  - `isLocked` *function(string): boolean*
  - `areLocked` *function(string[]): boolean*
  - `toggleLink` *function(string): boolean* - link a sided pair, or let its sides move apart; returns whether it is linked now
  - `randomizeAll` *function(): void*
  - `randomizeGroup` *function(string): void*
  - `mirror` *function(string=): void*
  - `setSpecies` *function(string): {kept: number, dropped: string[]}*
  - `undo` *function(): boolean*
  - `redo` *function(): boolean*
  - `canUndo` *function(): boolean*
  - `canRedo` *function(): boolean*
  - `endGesture` *function(): void* - the next edit to the same control starts a new undo step
  - `name` *function(): string*
  - `setName` *function(string): string*
  - `code` *function(): string* - the share code of the recipe
  - `importCode` *function(string): {ok: boolean, message?: string}* - load a pasted code (one undo step)
  - `load` *function(Object, string=): void* - replace the recipe (one undo step) and make it the new baseline
  - `summary` *function(): Object* - `{ revision, canUndo, canRedo, isChanged }`

## creator-slots.js

> Kit creator slots: several named characters kept between visits, as plain data over any key-value store.
> A slot holds the character's share code (not the recipe), so a saved character survives changes to the
> catalogue the way a shared one does: it either decodes or says in words why it cannot.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.slots(store, options)` (list, save, update, rename, remove, open a slot) and
>        `Kit.creator.chipColors` (the few colours that stand in for a character in a list).
> Does not own: the store (`ctx.save` or `Kit.store(name)`), the share code (kit/creator-session.js), or the
>        list's interface (kit/creator-sheets.js).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-creator.js.

### Types

- **Slot**
  - `id` *string* - unique within the list
  - `name` *string*
  - `code` *string* - the character's share code
  - `savedAt` *number* - milliseconds since 1970
- **SlotList**
  - `list` *function(): Slot[]* - every saved character, newest first
  - `find` *function(string): ?Slot*
  - `save` *function(string, string): {ok: boolean, slot?: Slot, message?: string}* - a new slot from a name and a share code; refused when full
  - `update` *function(string, string): ?Slot* - replace a slot's code; null when there is no such slot
  - `rename` *function(string, string): ?Slot*
  - `remove` *function(string): boolean* - false when there was nothing to remove
  - `isFull` *function(): boolean*
  - `most` *number*

## creator-camera.js

> Kit creator camera: where the stage camera looks and how it gets there, as plain numbers. The character is
> framed by two numbers, the share of the body's height in view (`span`, 1 is the whole body) and the height
> at the middle of the view (`anchor`, as a share of the body's height); a turn is a `yaw`. Choosing a group of
> controls moves the target to the body area that group edits, and the real camera eases toward it.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.camera.director(options)` (the state, `focus`, `zoomBy`, `zoomTo`, `turnBy`, `step`,
>        `windowFor`), the table `GROUP_FOCUS` (group id -> the body area it edits) and the gesture maths
>        (`pinchFactor`, `yawFromDrag`).
> Does not own: the camera object (the screen sets an orthographic camera from the window this returns), the
>        measuring of the body (`figure.measured`), or pointer events (kit/creator.js reads those).
>
> Pure (no DOM, no THREE): runs in node and is unit-tested by tools/test-creator.js.

### Types

- **CameraDirector**
  - `focus` *function(string): void* - aim at the body area of an editor group (an unknown group aims at the whole body)
  - `zoomBy` *function(number): void* - multiply the span (below 1 zooms in); the anchor follows unless a group fixed it
  - `zoomTo` *function(('full'\|'close')): void* - jump the target to the whole body or to head and shoulders
  - `turnBy` *function(number): void* - turn the character by radians, instantly (a drag)
  - `turnTo` *function(number): void* - ease the character to a yaw (a button or key)
  - `step` *function(number, boolean=): void* - move toward the target by `dt` seconds; `instant` jumps there
  - `windowFor` *function(Object, number): {centerX: number, centerY: number, halfWidth: number, halfHeight: number}* - the camera window for a measured body and a screen area of an aspect
  - `state` *function(): {yaw: number, anchor: number, span: number}* - the values in view now
  - `target` *function(): {yaw: number, anchor: number, span: number}* - the values being eased to

## creator-icons.js

> Kit creator icons: a small set of line icons as data, and the component that draws one. Group, action and
> part icons in the catalogue are names; this table is what turns a name into a drawing, so a name the table
> does not know still shows something (a dot) instead of nothing.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.ICONS` (name -> SVG path data in a 24 x 24 box) and `Kit.creator.Icon`.
> Does not own: the names (the catalogue's `icon` fields and the creator's own buttons) or any layout.
>
> Every icon is an original, simple stroke drawing. Needs the DOM (kit/ui/), so it is exercised in the browser.

## creator-controls.js

> Kit creator controls: the interface generated from the rows of a schema. A row of kind `dial` becomes sliders,
> `color` a grid of swatches that show the real ramp tones, `socket` a scrolling strip of option cards (None
> first); a tab per group holds its rows. Nothing here names a body part: a new control in the catalogue is a new
> row and gets a view from the table `ROW_VIEWS`.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.controlHost` (the bridge between a session and the views: one signal per field, one per
>        lock, the row structure as a signal, and the actions the views call), the row views (`DialControl`,
>        `ColorControl`, `SocketControl`), `GroupPane` and `ControlTabs`, and their stylesheet.
> Does not own: what an edit does (kit/creator-session.js), the rows (kit/creator-rows.js), the stage around the
>        character (kit/creator-stage.js) or the screen (kit/creator.js).
>
> Why signals per field: a slider drag changes one value, so exactly one slider and one readout update; the page
> is not rebuilt. The row structure is a signal too, but it changes only when `structureKey` does (a part with
> sockets, a pair unlinked, a body type), and then only the open pane redraws.
>
> Needs the DOM and kit/ui/, so it is exercised in the browser (tools/test-creator-ui.js).

### Types

- **ControlHost**
  - `groups` *Object* - signal of the `RowGroup[]`; replaced only when the row structure changes
  - `value` *function(string): Object* - signal of a field's current value, by field key
  - `lock` *function(string): Object* - signal (boolean) of a field's lock
  - `queueDial` *function(string, number): void* - remember a slider's newest value; `flush` applies it on the next frame
  - `flush` *function(): boolean* - apply queued slider values as one edit each; true when something was applied
  - `endGesture` *function(): void* - apply what is queued and let the next drag start a new undo step
  - `setColor` *function(string, number): void*
  - `setSocket` *function(string, ?string): void*
  - `resetRow` *function(Object): void* - put every slider of a dial row at its default
  - `resetSlider` *function(Object, Object): void* - put one slider at its default
  - `toggleLock` *function(Object): void*
  - `toggleLink` *function(Object): void*
  - `sync` *function(): void* - re-read the session into the signals (call after anything done to the session directly)
  - `act` *function(function(): void): void* - run a session change, then sync and notify

## creator-stage.js

> Kit creator stage: the part of the screen the character stands in, as components. A gesture layer (drag to
> turn, pinch or wheel to zoom, arrow keys and + / - for the keyboard), a left rail (undo, redo), a right rail
> (zoom, hold-to-compare, walk), the character's name, and the quick look presets.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.StageGestures`, `StageRails`, `StageTitle`, `LookChips`, `HoldButton` and the stage
>        stylesheet. Each is a component over plain props, so a screen composes them and a test can mount one.
> Does not own: the camera maths (kit/creator-camera.js), the 3D view (kit/creator.js) or the controls panel
>        (kit/creator-controls.js).
>
> Needs the DOM and kit/ui/, so it is exercised in the browser (tools/test-creator-ui.js).

## creator-panels.js

> Kit creator panels: the parts of the creator that are not generated from the schema. The Setup tab (body
> type, name, whole-character actions, the way into saves and sharing, the full look controls) and the two
> dialogs: saved characters (a sheet) and the share code (a dialog with copy and paste).
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.SetupView`, `SavesBody`, `ShareBody`, `SPECIES_NOTES` (a sentence per body type, shown as
>        a tooltip) and the panels' stylesheet.
> Does not own: saving (kit/creator-slots.js), codes (kit/creator-session.js), the dialog component
>        (`Kit.ui.openDialog`) or the screen that opens them (kit/creator.js).
>
> Every piece of text a person might paste or type (a name, a code) is shown as text, never as markup. Needs the
> DOM and kit/ui/, so it is exercised in the browser (tools/test-creator-ui.js).

## creator-theme.js

> Kit creator theme: the character creator's own look, as a theme for `Kit.register`. Calm and tool-like, so the
> character is the loudest thing on the screen: a graphite panel, one soft mint accent, flat buttons with no
> lip, sentence-case labels and a geometric display face over a friendly text face.
>
> Owns:  `Kit.creator.THEME` (tokens, fonts and scoped CSS in the shape kit/theme.js takes).
> Does not own: the layout of the creator (the components' own styles), or the 3D backdrop colour (kit/creator.js).
>
> Fonts come from Google Fonts only (the one font host the published page allows); every stack falls back to a
> system face, so the screen is complete without them.

## creator.js

> Kit creator: the `creator` screen (`Kit.play('creator')`, "Character Maker" in the menu). A character editor for
> the wardrobe catalogue, generated from data: the 3D character on a stage (drag to turn, pinch or buttons to
> zoom, the camera moves to the body area being edited), and a panel of tabs whose controls are built from
> `editor.schema` (sliders for dials, swatches for colours, option cards for parts).
>
> Owns:  the screen: which pieces are composed and how (session, control host, camera director, figure, look,
>        saves), the per-frame loop (apply queued slider values, update the figure with the fewest changes, fit the
>        camera, draw), the layout stylesheet, the draft that survives leaving the screen, and the measurements a
>        tool can read (`debugState`).
> Does not own: any piece it composes: edits and history (kit/creator-session.js), the rows and controls
>        (kit/creator-rows.js, creator-controls.js), the stage (kit/creator-stage.js), dialogs and the Setup tab
>        (kit/creator-panels.js), saves (kit/creator-slots.js), the camera maths (kit/creator-camera.js), the
>        look (kit/look-*.js), the figure (kit/figure*.js) and what a character is (kit/body-*.js).
>
> Layout: portrait puts the stage above the panel; landscape puts the stage left and the panel right. Both are
> one CSS grid, so the screen has no layout code. The 3D canvas fills the page and the camera is fitted to the
> stage element's box, so it follows the layout.
>
> Registered like a game, so the shell gives it a HUD, a back button and clean-up. It draws its own frame (`render`).

## ui/gallery.js

> Kit ui gallery: every component of kit/ui/ in every state, on one screen, under any skin.
>
> Owns:  the `gallery` screen (`Kit.play('gallery')`, or "Components" in the menu footer): a skin switcher and
>        one section per component file, with live values so bindings can be watched and a readout that shows a
>        pane really moved under a finger (useful on a real phone, where the automated touch test cannot go).
> Does not own: the components (the files beside this one). A new component is added here in the same change.
>
> It is registered like a game (last on the menu), so the shell gives it a HUD, a back button and clean-up, and
> `bun tools/smoke.js gallery` and the play tools reach it like any other.

## shell.js

> Kit shell: the renderer, the frame loop, the game lifecycle and the menu. Loaded last, after
> every kit file and every game.
>
> Owns:  the one WebGL renderer and its size, building the `ctx` each game receives, launching,
>        restarting and disposing games, the frame loop (game time, pause, hitstop, slow motion),
>        crash recovery back to the menu, the quality governor, the idle backdrop behind the
>        menu, the menu cards and the top-bar buttons (back, restart, mute).
> Does not own: anything a game does with its ctx; the pieces of the ctx themselves (input.js,
>        fx.js, ui.js, make.js, post.js, theme.js, core.js); page markup and styles (index.html).
>
> Public surface: `Kit.play(id)`, `Kit.menu()`, `Kit.current` (the live ctx, for tests and
> tools), `Kit.lastCrash` and `Kit.post_` (test hook).
> Needs the DOM and WebGL, so it is exercised by tools/smoke.js rather than unit tests.

### `Kit.play(id)`

Starts a registered game by id.

- `id` *string*

**Returns** *boolean* false when no game has that id

### `Kit.menu`

Returns to the menu.

### `Kit.post_`

Test hook: tools/smoke.js switches bloom off while playing and on for screenshots.

### Types

- **ViewSize** - The stage size, shared (as one live object) with every game through `ctx.size`.
  - `w` *number* - width, CSS pixels
  - `h` *number* - height, CSS pixels
  - `aspect` *number* - w / h
  - `portrait` *boolean* - is it at least as tall as it is wide?

## ui/menu.js

> Kit ui menu: the main menu's list of games, built from the component library (its first real consumer).
>
> Owns:  what is inside `#menu-body`: a search field, the scroll pane of game cards (`#menu-list`, the element
>        tools/touch-scroll.js drags), the empty state of a search with no match, and the footer's "Components"
>        link to the gallery.
> Does not own: the menu's frame, title and footer (index.html), launching games (kit/shell.js, through
>        `Kit.play`) or the mute button (kit/shell.js).
>
> Loaded after kit/shell.js. The shell has already filled `#cards` with plain buttons; this file replaces them,
> keeping each card's id (`card-<game id>`), which the smoke and play tools press. If the component library did
> not load, this file does nothing and the shell's cards stay, inside a plain native scroller.
